Of course. Here are 250 WAEC-standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations, generated from the provided textbook content.

---

### WAEC Chemistry Practice Questions

**Chapter 1: Fundamentals of Atomic Structure**

1.  The fundamental particle of an atom that determines its chemical identity is the...
    A. Neutron
    B. Proton
    C. Electron
    D. Nucleon
    **Answer:** B. Proton
    **Explanation:** The number of protons in the nucleus (the atomic number, Z) is unique to each element and defines its identity.

2.  Which scientist is credited with the discovery of the electron through experiments with cathode rays?
    A. Ernest Rutherford
    B. James Chadwick
    C. J.J. Thomson
    D. Dmitri Mendeleev
    **Answer:** C. J.J. Thomson
    **Explanation:** J.J. Thomson's experiments with cathode rays led to the discovery of the electron and the determination of its charge-to-mass ratio.

3.  An atom of uranium-238 (²³⁸₉₂U) contains...
    A. 92 protons, 238 neutrons, 92 electrons
    B. 92 protons, 146 neutrons, 92 electrons
    C. 146 protons, 92 neutrons, 146 electrons
    D. 238 protons, 92 neutrons, 238 electrons
    **Answer:** B. 92 protons, 146 neutrons, 92 electrons
    **Explanation:** The atomic number (Z=92) gives the number of protons (92). The mass number (A=238) is the sum of protons and neutrons. Number of neutrons = A - Z = 238 - 92 = 146. In a neutral atom, electrons equal protons.

4.  Isotopes of an element have the same number of...
    A. Neutrons but different numbers of protons.
    B. Protons but different numbers of neutrons.
    C. Protons and neutrons.
    D. Electrons and neutrons.
    **Answer:** B. Protons but different numbers of neutrons.
    **Explanation:** Isotopes are atoms of the same element, so they must have the same atomic number (number of protons). They differ in their mass number due to having different numbers of neutrons.

5.  Which of the following represents an ion?
    A. An atom with 12 protons, 12 neutrons, and 12 electrons.
    B. An atom with 17 protons, 18 neutrons, and 18 electrons.
    C. An atom with 6 protons, 6 neutrons, and 6 electrons.
    D. An atom with 20 protons, 20 neutrons, and 20 electrons.
    **Answer:** B. An atom with 17 protons, 18 neutrons, and 18 electrons.
    **Explanation:** An ion is a charged atom, meaning the number of electrons is not equal to the number of protons. In option B, there are 17 protons (+17 charge) and 18 electrons (-18 charge), giving a net charge of -1. This is the chloride ion, Cl⁻.

6.  The electron configuration of a neutral Carbon atom (Z=6) is...
    A. 1s² 2s¹ 2p³
    B. 1s¹ 2s² 2p³
    C. 1s² 2s² 2p²
    D. 1s² 2p⁴
    **Answer:** C. 1s² 2s² 2p²
    **Explanation:** According to the Aufbau principle, electrons fill the lowest energy orbitals first. For 6 electrons, the configuration is 1s², then 2s², and the remaining two electrons go into the 2p subshell.

7.  Which principle states that orbitals of equal energy are each occupied by one electron before any orbital is occupied by a second electron?
    A. Pauli Exclusion Principle
    B. Aufbau Principle
    C. Hund's Rule of Maximum Multiplicity
    D. Le Châtelier's Principle
    **Answer:** C. Hund's Rule of Maximum Multiplicity
    **Explanation:** Hund's rule governs the filling of degenerate orbitals (like the three p-orbitals), stating that electrons fill them singly with parallel spins before pairing up.

8.  What is the maximum number of electrons that can be held in the n=3 principal energy shell?
    A. 2
    B. 8
    C. 18
    D. 32
    **Answer:** C. 18
    **Explanation:** The maximum number of electrons a shell can hold is given by the formula 2n². For n=3, the maximum is 2(3)² = 2(9) = 18.

9.  Which set of quantum numbers (n, l, m_l, m_s) is NOT possible for an electron in an atom?
    A. (2, 1, -1, +½)
    B. (3, 2, 0, -½)
    C. (3, 1, 2, +½)
    D. (4, 0, 0, +½)
    **Answer:** C. (3, 1, 2, +½)
    **Explanation:** The magnetic quantum number (m_l) can only have integer values from -l to +l. In this case, l=1, so m_l can only be -1, 0, or +1. A value of m_l = 2 is not allowed.

10. The shape of a p orbital is best described as...
    A. Spherical
    B. Dumbbell-shaped
    C. Clover-leaf shaped
    D. Donut-shaped
    **Answer:** B. Dumbbell-shaped
    **Explanation:** s orbitals are spherical, p orbitals are dumbbell-shaped, and d orbitals have more complex shapes, including clover-leaf patterns.

11. An electron in an atom moves from the n=2 shell to the n=4 shell. This process involves...
    A. Absorption of energy
    B. Emission of energy
    C. Formation of an ion
    D. Emission of an alpha particle
    **Answer:** A. Absorption of energy
    **Explanation:** Electrons must absorb energy (e.g., a photon of light) to move to a higher energy level (a shell further from the nucleus).

12. The valence electrons of an element with Z=13 are in which subshells?
    A. 2s and 2p
    B. 3s and 3d
    C. 3p and 3d
    D. 3s and 3p
    **Answer:** D. 3s and 3p
    **Explanation:** The element with Z=13 is Aluminum (Al). Its electron configuration is 1s² 2s² 2p⁶ 3s² 3p¹. The outermost (valence) shell is n=3, which contains the 3s and 3p electrons.

---

**Chapter 2: The Periodic Table and Periodicity**

13. The modern periodic law states that the properties of elements are periodic functions of their...
    A. Atomic mass
    B. Mass number
    C. Atomic number
    D. Number of neutrons
    **Answer:** C. Atomic number
    **Explanation:** Henry Moseley's work showed that the atomic number (number of protons) is the fundamental property that determines an element's position and recurring properties.

14. Which of the following is an alkali metal?
    A. Ca
    B. Na
    C. Al
    D. B
    **Answer:** B. Na
    **Explanation:** Alkali metals are the elements in Group 1 of the periodic table. Sodium (Na) is in Group 1.

15. As you move from left to right across a period in the periodic table, the atomic radius generally...
    A. Increases
    B. Decreases
    C. Stays the same
    D. Decreases then increases
    **Answer:** B. Decreases
    **Explanation:** Across a period, the number of protons (effective nuclear charge) increases, pulling the electrons in the same principal shell closer to the nucleus, thus decreasing the atomic size.

16. Which of the following elements has the highest first ionization energy?
    A. Li
    B. C
    C. F
    D. Ne
    **Answer:** D. Ne
    **Explanation:** First ionization energy increases across a period. Neon (Ne) is a noble gas at the end of Period 2 with a stable, full valence shell, making it very difficult to remove an electron.

17. Electronegativity is a measure of an atom's ability to...
    A. Lose electrons
    B. Attract electrons in a chemical bond
    C. Conduct electricity
    D. Form a positive ion
    **Answer:** B. Attract electrons in a chemical bond
    **Explanation:** Electronegativity describes the tendency of an atom to pull a shared pair of bonding electrons towards itself.

18. Elements in the same group of the periodic table have similar chemical properties because they have the same...
    A. Number of electron shells
    B. Number of valence electrons
    C. Atomic mass
    D. Number of protons and neutrons
    **Answer:** B. Number of valence electrons
    **Explanation:** Chemical properties are primarily determined by the number of electrons in the outermost shell (valence electrons), which is the same for elements in the same group.

19. Which of the following is a transition metal?
    A. Mg
    B. P
    C. Fe
    D. Ar
    **Answer:** C. Fe
    **Explanation:** Transition metals are found in the d-block of the periodic table (Groups 3-12). Iron (Fe) is in the d-block.

20. Which of the following describes the trend of metallic character in the periodic table?
    A. Increases from left to right and increases down a group.
    B. Decreases from left to right and increases down a group.
    C. Increases from left to right and decreases down a group.
    D. Decreases from left to right and decreases down a group.
    **Answer:** B. Decreases from left to right and increases down a group.
    **Explanation:** Metallic character (tendency to lose electrons) increases down a group as atoms get larger and valence electrons are easier to remove. It decreases across a period as effective nuclear charge increases.

21. The halogens are found in which group of the periodic table?
    A. Group 1
    B. Group 2
    C. Group 17
    D. Group 18
    **Answer:** C. Group 17
    **Explanation:** Group 17 contains the halogens (F, Cl, Br, I, At), which are highly reactive non-metals with 7 valence electrons.

22. An anion is larger than its parent atom because...
    A. It has more protons
    B. The nucleus shrinks
    C. It has more electron-electron repulsion
    D. It has lost a shell of electrons
    **Answer:** C. It has more electron-electron repulsion
    **Explanation:** When an atom gains electrons to form an anion, the added electrons increase the repulsion among the valence electrons, causing the electron cloud to expand. The nuclear charge remains the same.

23. The scientist who left gaps in his periodic table for undiscovered elements was...
    A. John Newlands
    B. Dmitri Mendeleev
    C. Henry Moseley
    D. Johann Döbereiner
    **Answer:** B. Dmitri Mendeleev
    **Explanation:** A key strength of Mendeleev's periodic table was his prediction of the existence and properties of undiscovered elements like gallium (eka-aluminum) and germanium (eka-silicon), for which he left gaps.

24. Elements in the f-block of the periodic table are known as...
    A. Main group elements
    B. Transition metals
    C. Halogens
    D. Inner transition metals
    **Answer:** D. Inner transition metals
    **Explanation:** The f-block elements consist of the lanthanide and actinide series and are also called inner transition metals.

---

**Chapter 3: Ionic and Covalent Bonding**

25. An ionic bond is formed by the...
    A. Sharing of electrons between a metal and a non-metal
    B. Transfer of electrons from a metal to a non-metal
    C. Sharing of electrons between two non-metals
    D. Attraction between positive ions in a sea of electrons
    **Answer:** B. Transfer of electrons from a metal to a non-metal
    **Explanation:** Ionic bonding involves the complete transfer of valence electrons, typically from a metal (forming a cation) to a non-metal (forming an anion), leading to electrostatic attraction between the ions.

26. Which of the following compounds contains a covalent bond?
    A. NaCl
    B. MgO
    C. H₂O
    D. KBr
    **Answer:** C. H₂O
    **Explanation:** Water (H₂O) is formed between two non-metals (hydrogen and oxygen) through the sharing of electrons, which is characteristic of covalent bonding. The other options are formed between metals and non-metals (ionic).

27. Which of the following contains a triple covalent bond?
    A. O₂
    B. H₂
    C. N₂
    D. Cl₂
    **Answer:** C. N₂
    **Explanation:** In a nitrogen molecule (N₂), each nitrogen atom shares three pairs of electrons with the other to achieve a stable octet, forming a triple bond (N≡N).

28. The tendency of atoms to attain an electron configuration of a noble gas is known as the...
    A. Aufban principle
    B. Octet rule
    C. Hund's rule
    D. VSEPR theory
    **Answer:** B. Octet rule
    **Explanation:** The octet rule describes the tendency of main group elements to bond in such a way that each atom has eight electrons in its valence shell, giving it the same electronic configuration as a noble gas.

29. A polar covalent bond is formed between two atoms of...
    A. The same element
    B. The same electronegativity
    C. Different electronegativity
    D. Different atomic size
    **Answer:** C. Different electronegativity
    **Explanation:** When two atoms with different electronegativity values form a covalent bond, the electrons are shared unequally, creating a partial positive (δ⁺) and a partial negative (δ⁻) charge, which makes the bond polar.

30. Which of the following is NOT a typical property of ionic compounds?
    A. High melting point
    B. Conducts electricity in the solid state
    C. Forms a crystal lattice
    D. Brittle
    **Answer:** B. Conducts electricity in the solid state
    **Explanation:** In the solid state, the ions in an ionic compound are held in fixed positions in the crystal lattice and are not free to move. Therefore, solid ionic compounds do not conduct electricity. They conduct when molten or dissolved in water.

31. Metallic bonding is best described as the attraction between...
    A. Positive ions and negative ions
    B. Positive nuclei and shared electron pairs
    C. Metal atoms and non-metal atoms
    D. Positive metal ions and delocalized electrons
    **Answer:** D. Positive metal ions and delocalized electrons
    **Explanation:** The electron sea model describes metallic bonding as an electrostatic attraction between a lattice of positive metal ions and a "sea" of delocalized valence electrons that are free to move throughout the structure.

32. The formation of a positive ion from a neutral atom involves the...
    A. Gain of protons
    B. Loss of protons
    C. Gain of electrons
    D. Loss of electrons
    **Answer:** D. Loss of electrons
    **Explanation:** A neutral atom has an equal number of protons and electrons. To form a positive ion (cation), it must lose one or more negatively charged electrons.

33. How many lone pairs of electrons are on the central atom in a molecule of ammonia (NH₃)?
    A. 0
    B. 1
    C. 2
    D. 3
    **Answer:** B. 1
    **Explanation:** Nitrogen (Group 15) has 5 valence electrons. In NH₃, it forms three single covalent bonds with three hydrogen atoms (using 3 electrons). The remaining 2 valence electrons exist as one non-bonding lone pair on the nitrogen atom.

34. A mixture of copper and zinc is an example of...
    A. An ionic compound
    B. An interstitial alloy
    C. A substitutional alloy
    D. A covalent network
    **Answer:** C. A substitutional alloy
    **Explanation:** Brass is an alloy where zinc atoms, which are similar in size to copper atoms, replace some of the copper atoms in the metallic crystal lattice. This is a substitutional alloy.

35. The energy released when one mole of an ionic crystalline solid is formed from its gaseous ions is called...
    A. Ionization energy
    B. Electron affinity
    C. Lattice energy
    D. Bond energy
    **Answer:** C. Lattice energy
    **Explanation:** Lattice energy is a measure of the strength of the electrostatic forces in an ionic lattice, defined as the energy change upon formation of one mole of the ionic solid from its gaseous ions.

36. An element from Group 2 will react with an element from Group 16 to form a compound with the general formula...
    A. MX
    B. M₂X
    C. MX₂
    D. M₂X₃
    **Answer:** A. MX
    **Explanation:** Group 2 elements (like Mg, Ca) form +2 ions (M²⁺). Group 16 elements (like O, S) form -2 ions (X²⁻). To form a neutral compound, one M²⁺ ion will balance one X²⁻ ion, giving the formula MX (e.g., MgO, CaS).

---

**Chapter 4: Molecular Geometry and VSEPR Theory**

37. The VSEPR theory is used to predict the...
    A. Polarity of a bond
    B. Shape of a molecule
    C. Rate of a reaction
    D. Energy of a reaction
    **Answer:** B. Shape of a molecule
    **Explanation:** The Valence Shell Electron Pair Repulsion (VSEPR) theory predicts the three-dimensional arrangement of atoms in a molecule by assuming that electron pairs around a central atom will position themselves to minimize repulsion.

38. The molecular geometry of methane (CH₄) is...
    A. Linear
    B. Trigonal planar
    C. Tetrahedral
    D. Bent
    **Answer:** C. Tetrahedral
    **Explanation:** The central carbon atom in methane has four single bonds (four electron domains) and no lone pairs. This arrangement minimizes repulsion, resulting in a tetrahedral geometry with bond angles of 109.5°.

39. The bond angle in a water molecule (H₂O) is approximately...
    A. 180°
    B. 120°
    C. 109.5°
    D. 104.5°
    **Answer:** D. 104.5°
    **Explanation:** The central oxygen atom in water has four electron domains (two bonding pairs, two lone pairs). The electron domain geometry is tetrahedral, but the two lone pairs exert greater repulsion than the bonding pairs, compressing the H-O-H bond angle to approximately 104.5°.

40. Which of the following molecules is nonpolar, even though it contains polar bonds?
    A. H₂O
    B. NH₃
    C. CCl₄
    D. HCl
    **Answer:** C. CCl₄
    **Explanation:** Carbon tetrachloride (CCl₄) has a symmetrical tetrahedral shape. Although the individual C-Cl bonds are polar, their bond dipoles are arranged symmetrically and cancel each other out, resulting in an overall nonpolar molecule.

41. The strongest type of intermolecular force is...
    A. London dispersion force
    B. Dipole-dipole force
    C. Hydrogen bonding
    D. Covalent bonding
    **Answer:** C. Hydrogen bonding
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen bonding is a particularly strong type of dipole-dipole interaction that occurs when hydrogen is bonded to N, O, or F. It is the strongest of the intermolecular forces (but much weaker than intramolecular covalent bonds).

42. London dispersion forces are present in...
    A. Only nonpolar molecules
    B. Only polar molecules
    C. Only ionic compounds
    D. All molecules and atoms
    **Answer:** D. All molecules and atoms
    **Explanation:** London dispersion forces arise from temporary fluctuations in electron distribution, creating temporary dipoles. Since all atoms and molecules have electrons, they all exhibit these forces.

43. Which substance is expected to have the highest boiling point?
    A. CH₄ (Molar Mass ≈ 16)
    B. NH₃ (Molar Mass ≈ 17)
    C. H₂O (Molar Mass ≈ 18)
    D. Ne (Molar Mass ≈ 20)
    **Answer:** C. H₂O
    **Explanation:** Despite having similar molar masses, H₂O can form extensive hydrogen bonds, which are very strong intermolecular forces. NH₃ also has hydrogen bonds but they are weaker than in H₂O. CH₄ and Ne only have weak London dispersion forces. The strong H-bonds in water require the most energy to overcome, resulting in the highest boiling point.

44. A pi (π) bond is formed by the...
    A. End-to-end overlap of orbitals
    B. Sideways overlap of p orbitals
    C. Overlap of two s orbitals
    D. Sharing of four electrons in a single line
    **Answer:** B. Sideways overlap of p orbitals
    **Explanation:** Sigma (σ) bonds are formed by direct, end-to-end overlap of orbitals. Pi (π) bonds, found in double and triple bonds, are formed by the sideways overlap of parallel p orbitals.

45. The hybridization of the carbon atoms in ethene (C₂H₄) is...
    A. sp
    B. sp²
    C. sp³
    D. sp³d
    **Answer:** B. sp²
    **Explanation:** Each carbon atom in ethene is bonded to three other atoms (two H and one C) and has one double bond. This corresponds to three electron domains, requiring sp² hybridization to form a trigonal planar geometry.

46. What is the molecular shape of carbon dioxide (CO₂)?
    A. Linear
    B. Bent
    C. Trigonal planar
    D. Tetrahedral
    **Answer:** A. Linear
    **Explanation:** The central carbon atom in CO₂ has two electron domains (two double bonds) and no lone pairs. To maximize the distance between these domains, the molecule adopts a linear shape with a bond angle of 180°.

47. The boiling points of halogens increase down the group because of an increase in...
    A. Electronegativity
    B. Ionization energy
    C. Strength of London dispersion forces
    D. Polarity
    **Answer:** C. Strength of London dispersion forces
    **Explanation:** Halogens (F₂, Cl₂, Br₂, I₂) are nonpolar molecules. The only intermolecular forces they experience are London dispersion forces. As you go down the group, the number of electrons and molar mass increase, making the molecules more polarizable and strengthening the LDFs, which requires more energy to overcome (higher boiling point).

48. A molecule with a trigonal pyramidal shape has...
    A. 3 bonding pairs and 0 lone pairs
    B. 2 bonding pairs and 1 lone pair
    C. 3 bonding pairs and 1 lone pair
    D. 4 bonding pairs and 0 lone pairs
    **Answer:** C. 3 bonding pairs and 1 lone pair
    **Explanation:** This describes a central atom with four electron domains (tetrahedral electron geometry) where one domain is a lone pair. The resulting arrangement of atoms is trigonal pyramidal, as seen in ammonia (NH₃).

---

**Chapter 5: The Mole Concept and Stoichiometry**

49. Avogadro's number represents the number of...
    A. Grams in one mole of a substance
    B. Atoms in one gram of an element
    C. Particles in one mole of a substance
    D. Moles in one gram of a substance
    **Answer:** C. Particles in one mole of a substance
    **Explanation:** Avogadro's number (≈ 6.022 × 10²³) is the defined number of elementary entities (atoms, molecules, ions, etc.) in one mole of any substance.

50. What is the molar mass of sulfuric acid, H₂SO₄? (H=1, S=32, O=16)
    A. 49 g/mol
    B. 50 g/mol
    C. 98 g/mol
    D. 114 g/mol
    **Answer:** C. 98 g/mol
    **Explanation:** Molar mass of H₂SO₄ = (2 × H) + (1 × S) + (4 × O) = (2 × 1) + (1 × 32) + (4 × 16) = 2 + 32 + 64 = 98 g/mol.

51. How many moles of CO₂ are present in 22 g of carbon dioxide? (C=12, O=16)
    A. 0.25 mol
    B. 0.50 mol
    C. 1.0 mol
    D. 2.0 mol
    **Answer:** B. 0.50 mol
    **Explanation:** First, find the molar mass of CO₂: 12 + (2 × 16) = 44 g/mol. Then, use the formula: Moles = Mass / Molar Mass = 22 g / 44 g/mol = 0.50 mol.

52. The empirical formula of a compound is CH₂O and its molar mass is 180 g/mol. What is its molecular formula? (C=12, H=1, O=16)
    A. C₂H₄O₂
    B. C₃H₆O₃
    C. C₆H₁₂O₆
    D. C₇H₁₆O₄
    **Answer:** C. C₆H₁₂O₆
    **Explanation:** First, find the mass of the empirical formula (CH₂O): 12 + (2 × 1) + 16 = 30 g/mol. Next, find the ratio n = Molar Mass / Empirical Formula Mass = 180 / 30 = 6. Finally, multiply the empirical formula by n: (CH₂O)₆ = C₆H₁₂O₆.

53. What is the percentage by mass of oxygen in magnesium oxide, MgO? (Mg=24, O=16)
    A. 20%
    B. 40%
    C. 50%
    D. 60%
    **Answer:** B. 40%
    **Explanation:** Molar mass of MgO = 24 + 16 = 40 g/mol. Percentage of O = (Mass of O / Molar Mass of MgO) × 100% = (16 / 40) × 100% = 0.4 × 100% = 40%.

54. For the reaction: N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) → 2NH₃(g). How many moles of ammonia (NH₃) are produced from 6 moles of hydrogen (H₂)?
    A. 2 moles
    B. 3 moles
    C. 4 moles
    D. 6 moles
    **Answer:** C. 4 moles
    **Explanation:** From the balanced equation, the mole ratio of H₂ to NH₃ is 3:2. Using this ratio: Moles of NH₃ = (6 moles H₂) × (2 moles NH₃ / 3 moles H₂) = 4 moles NH₃.

55. The simplest whole-number ratio of atoms in a compound is its...
    A. Molecular formula
    B. Structural formula
    C. Isomeric formula
    D. Empirical formula
    **Answer:** D. Empirical formula
    **Explanation:** The empirical formula represents the simplest ratio of elements in a compound, while the molecular formula represents the actual number of atoms in a molecule.

56. How many atoms are present in 0.5 moles of Helium gas? (Avogadro's number = 6.02 x 10²³)
    A. 1.204 x 10²³
    B. 3.01 x 10²³
    C. 6.02 x 10²³
    D. 1.204 x 10²⁴
    **Answer:** B. 3.01 x 10²³
    **Explanation:** Number of atoms = Moles × Avogadro's number = 0.5 mol × (6.02 × 10²³ atoms/mol) = 3.01 × 10²³ atoms.

57. Stoichiometry is based on the law of...
    A. Constant Composition
    B. Multiple Proportions
    C. Conservation of Mass
    D. Definite Proportions
    **Answer:** C. Conservation of Mass
    **Explanation:** Stoichiometry, which involves balancing equations and relating amounts of reactants and products, is founded on the principle that mass is conserved in a chemical reaction.

58. One mole of any ideal gas at standard temperature and pressure (STP) occupies a volume of...
    A. 1.0 L
    B. 22.4 L
    C. 24.0 L
    D. 100 L
    **Answer:** B. 22.4 L
    **Explanation:** The molar volume of an ideal gas at STP (0°C and 1 atm) is a standard value of 22.4 L/mol.

59. A compound was found to contain 75% Carbon and 25% Hydrogen by mass. What is its empirical formula? (C=12, H=1)
    A. CH
    B. CH₂
    C. CH₃
    D. CH₄
    **Answer:** D. CH₄
    **Explanation:** Assume a 100g sample: 75g C, 25g H.
    Moles C = 75g / 12g/mol = 6.25 mol.
    Moles H = 25g / 1g/mol = 25 mol.
    Divide by smallest (6.25): C = 6.25/6.25 = 1. H = 25/6.25 = 4. The ratio is 1:4, so the empirical formula is CH₄.

60. To convert from mass of a substance to the number of particles, you must first convert mass to...
    A. Volume
    B. Density
    C. Moles
    D. Empirical formula
    **Answer:** C. Moles
    **Explanation:** The mole is the central unit linking the macroscopic world (mass) to the microscopic world (number of particles). The path is Mass → Moles → Particles.

---

**Chapter 6: Chemical Reactions and Equations**

61. Which of the following is a balanced chemical equation?
    A. H₂ + O₂ → H₂O
    B. Mg + HCl → MgCl₂ + H₂
    C. 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂
    D. CH₄ + O₂ → CO₂ + H₂O
    **Answer:** C. 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂
    **Explanation:** In option C, there are 4 hydrogen atoms and 2 oxygen atoms on both the reactant and product sides. The other equations are not balanced.

62. In the reaction: Zn(s) + 2HCl(aq) → ZnCl₂(aq) + H₂(g), what is the oxidizing agent?
    A. Zn
    B. H⁺
    C. Cl⁻
    D. H₂
    **Answer:** B. H⁺
    **Explanation:** Zinc (Zn) is oxidized (goes from oxidation state 0 to +2). Hydrogen (H⁺) is reduced (goes from +1 to 0). The species that gets reduced (H⁺) is the oxidizing agent because it causes the oxidation of Zn.

63. The reaction 2KClO₃(s) → 2KCl(s) + 3O₂(g) is an example of...
    A. Combination
    B. Decomposition
    C. Single displacement
    D. Combustion
    **Answer:** B. Decomposition
    **Explanation:** A single compound (potassium chlorate) breaks down into simpler substances (potassium chloride and oxygen). This is a decomposition reaction.

64. When an acid and a base react to form a salt and water, the reaction is called...
    A. Precipitation
    B. Combustion
    C. Neutralization
    D. Oxidation
    **Answer:** C. Neutralization
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of an acid-base neutralization reaction, which is a type of double displacement reaction.

65. For the reaction 2SO₂(g) + O₂(g) → 2SO₃(g), if 4 moles of SO₂ react with 3 moles of O₂, what is the limiting reactant?
    A. SO₂
    B. O₂
    C. SO₃
    D. There is no limiting reactant.
    **Answer:** A. SO₂
    **Explanation:** The mole ratio of SO₂ to O₂ is 2:1. To react completely with 4 moles of SO₂, you would need (4 moles SO₂) × (1 mole O₂ / 2 moles SO₂) = 2 moles of O₂. Since you have 3 moles of O₂, you have more than enough O₂. Therefore, SO₂ will be consumed first and is the limiting reactant.

66. Theoretical yield is the...
    A. Amount of product actually obtained in the lab
    B. Maximum amount of product that can be formed
    C. Efficiency of the reaction
    D. Amount of limiting reactant used
    **Answer:** B. Maximum amount of product that can be formed
    **Explanation:** The theoretical yield is the maximum possible amount of product calculated from stoichiometry, assuming the reaction goes to completion and is 100% efficient.

67. If a reaction has a theoretical yield of 50.0 g and the actual yield obtained is 40.0 g, what is the percent yield?
    A. 20%
    B. 80%
    C. 125%
    D. 90%
    **Answer:** B. 80%
    **Explanation:** Percent Yield = (Actual Yield / Theoretical Yield) × 100% = (40.0 g / 50.0 g) × 100% = 0.8 × 100% = 80%.

68. How many moles of solute are in 500 mL of a 2.0 M solution?
    A. 0.5 mol
    B. 1.0 mol
    C. 2.0 mol
    D. 4.0 mol
    **Answer:** B. 1.0 mol
    **Explanation:** First, convert volume to Liters: 500 mL = 0.5 L. Then, use the formula Moles = Molarity × Volume(L) = 2.0 mol/L × 0.5 L = 1.0 mol.

69. In balancing a redox equation in an acidic solution, H₂O is added to balance ______ and H⁺ is added to balance ______.
    A. Oxygen, Hydrogen
    B. Hydrogen, Oxygen
    C. Charge, Atoms
    D. Atoms, Charge
    **Answer:** A. Oxygen, Hydrogen
    **Explanation:** In the half-reaction method in acidic solution, after balancing the main elements, oxygen atoms are balanced by adding water molecules, and then hydrogen atoms are balanced by adding hydrogen ions.

70. The complete combustion of a hydrocarbon in excess oxygen produces...
    A. Carbon monoxide and water
    B. Carbon and water
    C. Carbon dioxide and hydrogen
    D. Carbon dioxide and water
    **Answer:** D. Carbon dioxide and water
    **Explanation:** Complete combustion of any compound containing carbon and hydrogen (a hydrocarbon) yields carbon dioxide (CO₂) and water (H₂O).

71. When an aqueous solution of silver nitrate (AgNO₃) is mixed with an aqueous solution of sodium chloride (NaCl), a precipitate is formed. This reaction is best classified as...
    A. Combustion
    B. Precipitation
    C. Decomposition
    D. Synthesis
    **Answer:** B. Precipitation
    **Explanation:** This is a double displacement reaction where an insoluble solid (precipitate), AgCl(s), is formed from two aqueous solutions.

72. Molarity is defined as...
    A. Moles of solute per kg of solvent
    B. Moles of solute per liter of solution
    C. Mass of solute per liter of solution
    D. Moles of solvent per kg of solute
    **Answer:** B. Moles of solute per liter of solution
    **Explanation:** Molarity (M) is the most common unit of concentration in stoichiometry, defined as moles of solute divided by the total volume of the solution in liters.

---

**Chapter 7: Gases and Gas Laws**

73. The Ideal Gas Law is given by the equation...
    A. P₁V₁ = P₂V₂
    B. V₁/T₁ = V₂/T₂
    C. PV = nRT
    D. P_total = P₁ + P₂
    **Answer:** C. PV = nRT
    **Explanation:** The Ideal Gas Law relates pressure (P), volume (V), number of moles (n), and temperature (T) of an ideal gas through the gas constant R.

74. According to Boyle's Law, if the pressure of a gas is doubled at constant temperature, its volume...
    A. Is doubled
    B. Is halved
    C. Stays the same
    D. Is quadrupled
    **Answer:** B. Is halved
    **Explanation:** Boyle's Law states that pressure and volume are inversely proportional (P ∝ 1/V). If you double the pressure, the volume must be reduced by half to keep the product PV constant.

75. For gas law calculations, temperature must always be expressed in...
    A. Celsius (°C)
    B. Fahrenheit (°F)
    C. Kelvin (K)
    D. Degrees Rankine
    **Answer:** C. Kelvin (K)
    **Explanation:** The gas laws are based on absolute temperature, where zero corresponds to the complete absence of thermal motion. The Kelvin scale is the absolute temperature scale, and T(K) = T(°C) + 273.15.

76. "Equal volumes of all ideal gases at the same temperature and pressure contain the same number of molecules." This is a statement of...
    A. Boyle's Law
    B. Charles's Law
    C. Avogadro's Law
    D. Dalton's Law
    **Answer:** C. Avogadro's Law
    **Explanation:** Avogadro's Law establishes the direct relationship between the volume and the number of moles (or molecules) of a gas at constant temperature and pressure.

77. A gas is collected over water at 25°C. The total pressure is 760 torr. If the vapor pressure of water at 25°C is 24 torr, what is the partial pressure of the dry gas?
    A. 24 torr
    B. 736 torr
    C. 760 torr
    D. 784 torr
    **Answer:** B. 736 torr
    **Explanation:** According to Dalton's Law of Partial Pressures, P_total = P_gas + P_water. Therefore, P_gas = P_total - P_water = 760 torr - 24 torr = 736 torr.

78. The Kinetic Molecular Theory of gases assumes that...
    A. Gas particles have strong attractive forces
    B. Collisions between particles are inelastic
    C. The volume of the gas particles is significant
    D. Gas particles are in constant, random motion
    **Answer:** D. Gas particles are in constant, random motion
    **Explanation:** This is a key postulate of the KMT. It also assumes negligible particle volume and no intermolecular forces, and that collisions are perfectly elastic.

79. Real gases deviate from ideal behavior at...
    A. High temperature and low pressure
    B. High temperature and high pressure
    C. Low temperature and low pressure
    D. Low temperature and high pressure
    **Answer:** D. Low temperature and high pressure
    **Explanation:** At low temperatures, particles move slower and intermolecular forces become significant. At high pressures, particles are forced closer together and their finite volume becomes a significant fraction of the container volume. Both effects cause deviation from ideal gas assumptions.

80. A balloon has a volume of 2.0 L at 27°C. If the temperature is raised to 127°C at constant pressure, what will the new volume be?
    A. 1.5 L
    B. 2.7 L
    C. 4.0 L
    D. 9.4 L
    **Answer:** B. 2.7 L
    **Explanation:** Use Charles's Law (V₁/T₁ = V₂/T₂). First, convert temperatures to Kelvin: T₁ = 27 + 273 = 300 K; T₂ = 127 + 273 = 400 K.
    V₂ = V₁ (T₂/T₁) = 2.0 L × (400 K / 300 K) = 2.0 L × (4/3) ≈ 2.67 L.

81. The spreading of a gas to fill its container is called...
    A. Effusion
    B. Diffusion
    C. Compression
    D. Sublimation
    **Answer:** B. Diffusion
    **Explanation:** Diffusion is the process by which particles of a substance spread out from a region of higher concentration to a region of lower concentration to fill the available space. Effusion is the escape of gas through a tiny hole.

82. The pressure of a gas is caused by...
    A. The weight of the gas particles
    B. Collisions of gas particles with the container walls
    C. Attractions between the gas particles
    D. The kinetic energy of the particles being converted to potential energy
    **Answer:** B. Collisions of gas particles with the container walls
    **Explanation:** According to KMT, the force exerted by gas particles as they collide with the walls of their container results in the macroscopic property of pressure.

83. Which of the following relationships represents the Combined Gas Law?
    A. PV = nRT
    B. P₁V₁ = P₂V₂
    C. (P₁V₁)/T₁ = (P₂V₂)/T₂
    D. P_total = P_i / χ_i
    **Answer:** C. (P₁V₁)/T₁ = (P₂V₂)/T₂
    **Explanation:** The Combined Gas Law merges Boyle's, Charles's, and Gay-Lussac's laws to relate P, V, and T for a fixed amount of gas (n is constant).

84. The value 'b' in the van der Waals equation, [P + a(n/V)²][V - nb] = nRT, accounts for...
    A. Intermolecular attractions
    B. The finite volume of gas particles
    C. The kinetic energy of the particles
    D. The molar mass of the gas
    **Answer:** B. The finite volume of gas particles
    **Explanation:** The term 'nb' is subtracted from the container volume (V) to give the actual free volume available for the gas particles to move in, thus accounting for the volume the particles themselves occupy. The 'a' term accounts for intermolecular attractions.

---

**Chapter 8: Liquids and Solids**

85. Which of the following substances has the highest vapor pressure at room temperature?
    A. Water
    B. Honey
    C. Ethanol
    D. Diethyl ether
    **Answer:** D. Diethyl ether
    **Explanation:** High vapor pressure corresponds to weak intermolecular forces. Diethyl ether has very weak London dispersion and weak dipole-dipole forces, making it very volatile. Water and ethanol have strong hydrogen bonds, giving them lower vapor pressures.

86. The boiling point of a liquid is the temperature at which its vapor pressure equals...
    A. 0 torr
    B. The critical pressure
    C. The triple point pressure
    D. The external atmospheric pressure
    **Answer:** D. The external atmospheric pressure
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of boiling point. When the vapor pressure inside the liquid is strong enough to push back against the surrounding atmospheric pressure, boiling occurs throughout the liquid.

87. A solid in which the constituent particles are arranged in a random, disordered manner is called...
    A. Crystalline
    B. Amorphous
    C. Ionic
    D. Metallic
    **Answer:** B. Amorphous
    **Explanation:** Amorphous solids, like glass and plastic, lack the long-range ordered structure characteristic of crystalline solids.

88. Diamond and graphite are both examples of...
    A. Ionic solids
    B. Covalent network solids
    C. Molecular solids
    D. Metallic solids
    **Answer:** B. Covalent network solids
    **Explanation:** In both diamond and graphite, carbon atoms are held together by strong covalent bonds in an extensive network, making the entire crystal essentially one giant molecule. They are allotropes of carbon.

89. A solid substance melts at 801°C, is hard and brittle, and conducts electricity when dissolved in water. This substance is most likely...
    A. Diamond
    B. Sugar (Sucrose)
    C. Sodium Chloride (NaCl)
    D. Iron (Fe)
    **Answer:** C. Sodium Chloride (NaCl)
    **Explanation:** These properties—high melting point, hard/brittle nature, and conducting electricity only when molten or aqueous—are characteristic of an ionic solid.

90. The phase transition from a solid directly to a gas is called...
    A. Evaporation
    B. Condensation
    C. Deposition
    D. Sublimation
    **Answer:** D. Sublimation
    **Explanation:** Sublimation is the direct conversion of a solid to a gas, e.g., dry ice (solid CO₂) turning into CO₂ gas. The reverse process (gas to solid) is deposition.

91. In a phase diagram, the point at which all three phases of a substance coexist in equilibrium is the...
    A. Critical point
    B. Triple point
    C. Boiling point
    D. Eutectic point
    **Answer:** B. Triple point
    **Explanation:** The triple point is the unique combination of temperature and pressure where the solid, liquid, and gas phases are all simultaneously in equilibrium.

92. The high surface tension of water is primarily due to...
    A. Its low molar mass
    B. Covalent bonds within the molecule
    C. Strong hydrogen bonding between molecules
    D. Its ability to dissolve many substances
    **Answer:** C. Strong hydrogen bonding between molecules
    **Explanation:** The strong intermolecular attraction due to hydrogen bonding creates a strong net inward pull on surface molecules, resulting in high surface tension.

93. How many atoms are there per unit cell in a face-centered cubic (FCC) structure?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 4
    D. 8
    **Answer:** C. 4
    **Explanation:** In an FCC unit cell, there is 1/8 of an atom at each of the 8 corners (8 × 1/8 = 1 atom) and 1/2 of an atom on each of the 6 faces (6 × 1/2 = 3 atoms). The total is 1 + 3 = 4 atoms.

94. The technique used to determine the crystal structure of solids is...
    A. X-ray diffraction
    B. Mass spectrometry
    C. Titration
    D. Calorimetry
    **Answer:** A. X-ray diffraction
    **Explanation:** X-ray diffraction involves passing X-rays through a crystal and analyzing the resulting diffraction pattern, which reveals information about the arrangement of atoms in the crystal lattice according to Bragg's Law.

95. Ice is less dense than liquid water because...
    A. The molecules are closer together in ice.
    B. The hydrogen bonds form an open, ordered, lattice structure in ice.
    C. Ice has a lower molar mass than water.
    D. Freezing releases energy, making the molecules lighter.
    **Answer:** B. The hydrogen bonds form an open, ordered, lattice structure in ice.
    **Explanation:** In ice, hydrogen bonds lock the water molecules into a fixed, hexagonal lattice with more empty space than in the disordered liquid state. This increased volume for the same mass results in lower density.

96. Which type of solid is generally characterized by being soft with a low melting point?
    A. Ionic
    B. Metallic
    C. Covalent Network
    D. Molecular
    **Answer:** D. Molecular
    **Explanation:** Molecular solids are held together by weak intermolecular forces (LDFs, dipole-dipole, H-bonds). These forces require relatively little energy to overcome, resulting in softness and low melting/boiling points.

---

**Chapter 9: Solutions and Their Properties**

97. A solution that contains the maximum amount of dissolved solute at a given temperature is called...
    A. Unsaturated
    B. Saturated
    C. Supersaturated
    D. Concentrated
    **Answer:** B. Saturated
    **Explanation:** A saturated solution is at equilibrium, containing the maximum amount of solute that can be dissolved in the solvent under the given conditions.

98. Which of the following is an example of a solid-in-solid solution?
    A. Salt water
    B. Air
    C. Brass
    D. Soda water
    **Answer:** C. Brass
    **Explanation:** Brass is an alloy, which is a solid solution of zinc (solute) dissolved in copper (solvent).

99. According to the principle "like dissolves like", which of the following is most likely to be soluble in water?
    A. Oil (nonpolar)
    B. Hexane (C₆H₁₄, nonpolar)
    C. Iodine (I₂, nonpolar)
    D. Ethanol (C₂H₅OH, polar)
    **Answer:** D. Ethanol (C₂H₅OH, polar)
    **Explanation:** Water is a highly polar solvent. Ethanol is also a polar molecule due to its -OH group, and it can form hydrogen bonds with water, making it very soluble ("miscible"). The other options are nonpolar.

100. The solubility of a gas in a liquid generally...
    A. Increases with increasing temperature
    B. Decreases with increasing temperature
    C. Is not affected by temperature
    D. Increases with decreasing pressure
    **Answer:** B. Decreases with increasing temperature
    **Explanation:** As temperature increases, the dissolved gas molecules gain more kinetic energy and are more likely to escape from the liquid phase back into the gas phase, thus decreasing solubility.

101. Adding a non-volatile solute to a pure solvent will cause the...
    A. Boiling point to decrease
    B. Freezing point to increase
    C. Vapor pressure to increase
    D. Vapor pressure to decrease
    **Answer:** D. Vapor pressure to decrease
    **Explanation:** This is a colligative property. The solute particles at the surface interfere with the solvent's ability to evaporate, thus lowering the vapor pressure of the solution compared to the pure solvent.

102. Which of the following solutions would have the lowest freezing point?
    A. 0.1 m NaCl
    B. 0.1 m CaCl₂
    C. 0.1 m C₆H₁₂O₆ (glucose)
    D. Pure water
    **Answer:** B. 0.1 m CaCl₂
    **Explanation:** Freezing point depression is a colligative property depending on the concentration of solute *particles*. CaCl₂ dissociates into three ions (Ca²⁺ + 2Cl⁻), giving an effective particle concentration of 0.3 m. NaCl gives two ions (0.2 m). Glucose is a non-electrolyte and gives one particle (0.1 m). The greatest particle concentration causes the largest depression, hence the lowest freezing point.

103. Osmosis is the net movement of...
    A. Solute particles through a semipermeable membrane
    B. Solvent particles through a semipermeable membrane
    C. Both solute and solvent particles
    D. Gas particles into a solution
    **Answer:** B. Solvent particles through a semipermeable membrane
    **Explanation:** Osmosis specifically describes the movement of solvent molecules from an area of high solvent concentration (low solute conc.) to an area of low solvent concentration (high solute conc.).

104. A red blood cell placed in a hypotonic solution (e.g., pure water) will...
    A. Shrink
    B. Swell and may burst
    C. Remain unchanged
    D. Undergo plasmolysis
    **Answer:** B. Swell and may burst
    **Explanation:** A hypotonic solution has a lower solute concentration than the cell's interior. Due to osmosis, water will move into the cell to try to equalize concentrations, causing it to swell and potentially lyse (burst).

105. According to Henry's Law, the solubility of a gas in a liquid is directly proportional to the...
    A. Temperature of the gas
    B. Volume of the liquid
    C. Partial pressure of the gas above the liquid
    D. Molar mass of the gas
    **Answer:** C. Partial pressure of the gas above the liquid
    **Explanation:** Henry's Law (S_gas = k_H * P_gas) states that as you increase the pressure of a gas above a liquid, more of that gas will be forced to dissolve in the liquid.

106. Which of the following is NOT a colligative property?
    A. Boiling point elevation
    B. Freezing point depression
    C. Osmotic pressure
    D. Density of the solution
    **Answer:** D. Density of the solution
    **Explanation:** Colligative properties depend only on the number of solute particles, not their identity. Density depends on the mass and volume, which are specific to the identity of the solute and solvent.

107. The van't Hoff factor (i) for sodium sulfate (Na₂SO₄) is ideally...
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** C. 3
    **Explanation:** When Na₂SO₄ dissolves, it dissociates into its constituent ions: Na₂SO₄(s) → 2Na⁺(aq) + SO₄²⁻(aq). This produces a total of three ions per formula unit.

108. Using salt to melt ice on roads is an application of...
    A. Vapor pressure lowering
    B. Freezing point depression
    C. Osmotic pressure
    D. Boiling point elevation
    **Answer:** B. Freezing point depression
    **Explanation:** Dissolving salt in the thin layer of water on top of the ice creates a solution with a freezing point below 0°C. This allows the ice to melt even at temperatures where pure water would be frozen.

---

**Chapter 10: Acid-Base Equilibria and pH**

109. According to the Arrhenius definition, a base is a substance that...
    A. Donates a proton
    B. Accepts a proton
    C. Produces H⁺ ions in water
    D. Produces OH⁻ ions in water
    **Answer:** D. Produces OH⁻ ions in water
    **Explanation:** The Arrhenius theory defines a base as a compound that increases the concentration of hydroxide ions (OH⁻) when dissolved in water.

110. In the reaction: NH₃(aq) + H₂O(l) ⇌ NH₄⁺(aq) + OH⁻(aq), water acts as...
    A. A Brønsted-Lowry acid
    B. A Brønsted-Lowry base
    C. A spectator ion
    D. An Arrhenius base
    **Answer:** A. A Brønsted-Lowry acid
    **Explanation:** In this reaction, the H₂O molecule donates a proton (H⁺) to the NH₃ molecule to form NH₄⁺. A proton donor is a Brønsted-Lowry acid.

111. What is the conjugate base of HSO₄⁻?
    A. H₂SO₄
    B. SO₄²⁻
    C. SO₃²⁻
    D. H₃O⁺
    **Answer:** B. SO₄²⁻
    **Explanation:** A conjugate base is formed when an acid donates a proton. When HSO₄⁻ acts as an acid and donates its proton (H⁺), what remains is the sulfate ion, SO₄²⁻.

112. A solution with a pH of 3 is...
    A. Strongly basic
    B. Weakly basic
    C. Neutral
    D. Acidic
    **Answer:** D. Acidic
    **Explanation:** On the pH scale (at 25°C), solutions with a pH less than 7 are acidic. A pH of 3 indicates a significant concentration of H⁺ ions.

113. If the [H⁺] of a solution is 1.0 x 10⁻⁹ M, what is its pOH at 25°C?
    A. 3
    B. 5
    C. 7
    D. 9
    **Answer:** B. 5
    **Explanation:** First, calculate the pH: pH = -log[H⁺] = -log(1.0 x 10⁻⁹) = 9. Then, use the relationship pH + pOH = 14. So, pOH = 14 - pH = 14 - 9 = 5.

114. Which of the following is a strong acid?
    A. CH₃COOH (Acetic acid)
    B. H₂CO₃ (Carbonic acid)
    C. HNO₃ (Nitric acid)
    D. H₃PO₄ (Phosphoric acid)
    **Answer:** C. HNO₃ (Nitric acid)
    **Explanation:** Nitric acid (HNO₃) is one of the common strong acids that dissociates completely in water. The others are weak acids.

115. A buffer solution is made by mixing...
    A. A strong acid and a strong base
    B. A weak acid and its conjugate base
    C. A strong acid and a weak acid
    D. Two different salts
    **Answer:** B. A weak acid and its conjugate base
    **Explanation:** A buffer's ability to resist pH change relies on the presence of a weak acid (to neutralize added base) and its conjugate base (to neutralize added acid) in appreciable amounts.

116. The pH at the equivalence point of a titration between a strong acid and a strong base is...
    A. Less than 7
    B. Equal to 7
    C. Greater than 7
    D. Equal to the pK_a
    **Answer:** B. Equal to 7
    **Explanation:** At the equivalence point, the strong acid and strong base have completely neutralized each other, forming a salt (from strong acid and strong base) and water. Such a salt does not hydrolyze water, so the resulting solution is neutral with a pH of 7 (at 25°C).

117. Which of the following acts as a Lewis acid but not a Brønsted-Lowry acid?
    A. HCl
    B. H₂O
    C. NH₃
    D. BF₃
    **Answer:** D. BF₃
    **Explanation:** Boron trifluoride (BF₃) does not have a proton (H⁺) to donate, so it's not a Brønsted-Lowry acid. However, the boron atom has an incomplete octet and can accept an electron pair, making it a Lewis acid.

118. The value of K_w, the ion-product constant for water, at 25°C is...
    A. 1.0 x 10⁻⁷
    B. 7.0
    C. 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴
    D. 14.0
    **Answer:** C. 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴
    **Explanation:** K_w = [H⁺][OH⁻] and at 25°C, its value is 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴ (mol/L)². This constant is fundamental to all aqueous acid-base chemistry.

119. A small pK_a value indicates a...
    A. Stronger acid
    B. Weaker acid
    C. Stronger base
    D. Weaker base
    **Answer:** A. Stronger acid
    **Explanation:** pK_a = -log(K_a). A smaller pK_a corresponds to a larger K_a value. A larger K_a means the acid dissociates more extensively, so it is a stronger acid.

120. When titrating a weak acid with a strong base, the pH at the half-equivalence point is equal to...
    A. 7.0
    B. The pK_b of the conjugate base
    C. The pK_a of the weak acid
    D. 14.0
    **Answer:** C. The pK_a of the weak acid
    **Explanation:** At the half-equivalence point, exactly half of the weak acid has been neutralized, so the concentrations of the weak acid ([HA]) and its conjugate base ([A⁻]) are equal. According to the Henderson-Hasselbalch equation (pH = pK_a + log([A⁻]/[HA])), log(1) = 0, so pH = pK_a.

---

**Chapter 11: Chemical Thermodynamics**

121. The first law of thermodynamics is a statement of the law of...
    A. Conservation of Mass
    B. Conservation of Energy
    C. Increasing Entropy
    D. Constant Composition
    **Answer:** B. Conservation of Energy
    **Explanation:** The first law (ΔU = q + w) states that energy cannot be created or destroyed, only transferred (as heat or work) or transformed.

122. A process that releases heat to the surroundings is called...
    A. Endothermic
    B. Exothermic
    C. Isentropic
    D. Isothermal
    **Answer:** B. Exothermic
    **Explanation:** In an exothermic process, the system loses heat (q < 0), and that heat is transferred to the surroundings. The enthalpy change (ΔH) for an exothermic process is negative.

123. Which of the following represents an increase in entropy?
    A. Water freezing into ice
    B. A gas condensing into a liquid
    C. A block of salt dissolving in water
    D. Two gases reacting to form a single solid product
    **Answer:** C. A block of salt dissolving in water
    **Explanation:** Entropy is a measure of disorder. When a highly ordered solid salt dissolves, its ions become dispersed randomly in the solution, leading to a significant increase in disorder (entropy). The other options represent an increase in order (decrease in entropy).

124. For a chemical reaction to be spontaneous at all temperatures, the signs of ΔH and ΔS must be...
    A. ΔH > 0, ΔS > 0
    B. ΔH < 0, ΔS < 0
    C. ΔH < 0, ΔS > 0
    D. ΔH > 0, ΔS < 0
    **Answer:** C. ΔH < 0, ΔS > 0
    **Explanation:** Spontaneity is predicted by ΔG = ΔH - TΔS. For ΔG to always be negative (spontaneous), the reaction must be exothermic (ΔH is negative) and result in an increase in entropy (ΔS is positive). This ensures that both terms contribute to a negative ΔG regardless of temperature.

125. The standard enthalpy of formation (ΔH°_f) of an element in its standard state is...
    A. Always positive
    B. Always negative
    C. Equal to zero
    D. Dependent on temperature
    **Answer:** C. Equal to zero
    **Explanation:** By definition, the enthalpy change for forming an element (like O₂(g), C(graphite), Fe(s)) from itself in its most stable form under standard conditions is zero.

126. A chemical reaction is at equilibrium when...
    A. ΔH = 0
    B. ΔS = 0
    C. ΔG = 0
    D. T = 0
    **Answer:** C. ΔG = 0
    **Explanation:** The change in Gibbs free energy (ΔG) indicates the driving force of a reaction. When ΔG is zero, there is no net driving force in either direction, and the system is at equilibrium.

127. Hess's Law is possible because enthalpy is a...
    A. Path function
    B. State function
    C. Temperature-dependent function
    D. Kinetic function
    **Answer:** B. State function
    **Explanation:** A state function is a property whose value depends only on the current state of the system, not on the path taken to reach that state. Because enthalpy is a state function, we can calculate the enthalpy change of a reaction by summing the changes of any series of steps that leads from the same initial to the same final state.

128. Calorimetry is a technique used to measure...
    A. Reaction rate
    B. Heat flow
    C. Equilibrium position
    D. Gas pressure
    **Answer:** B. Heat flow
    **Explanation:** A calorimeter is an instrument designed to measure the amount of heat absorbed or released (q) during a chemical or physical process.

129. Breaking chemical bonds...
    A. Always releases energy
    B. Always requires energy
    C. Is always a spontaneous process
    D. Can either release or require energy
    **Answer:** B. Always requires energy
    **Explanation:** Energy must be put into a system to overcome the attractive forces that hold atoms together in a chemical bond. It is an endothermic process. Forming bonds is exothermic.

130. Given the reaction: CH₄(g) + 2O₂(g) → CO₂(g) + 2H₂O(l)  ΔH = -890 kJ. This reaction is...
    A. Endothermic and absorbs 890 kJ of heat.
    B. Endothermic and releases 890 kJ of heat.
    C. Exothermic and absorbs 890 kJ of heat.
    D. Exothermic and releases 890 kJ of heat.
    **Answer:** D. Exothermic and releases 890 kJ of heat.
    **Explanation:** A negative sign for ΔH indicates that the reaction is exothermic, meaning that the system releases 890 kJ of heat to the surroundings for every mole of methane combusted.

131. The second law of thermodynamics states that the entropy of the universe...
    A. Is always constant
    B. Always decreases for a spontaneous process
    C. Always increases for a spontaneous process
    D. Is conserved in all processes
    **Answer:** C. Always increases for a spontaneous process
    **Explanation:** The second law dictates the direction of spontaneous change. A process is spontaneous if it leads to a net increase in the total entropy of the universe (system + surroundings).

132. Work done by the system on the surroundings (e.g., expansion of a gas) results in...
    A. An increase in the internal energy of the system
    B. A decrease in the internal energy of the system
    C. No change in the internal energy of the system
    D. An increase in the temperature of the surroundings only
    **Answer:** B. A decrease in the internal energy of the system
    **Explanation:** When the system does work, it expends energy, which decreases its own internal energy (w is negative in ΔU = q + w, using the common chemistry convention for work done *by* the system).

---

**Chapter 12: Chemical Kinetics**

133. The rate of a chemical reaction is defined as the...
    A. Change in temperature per unit time
    B. Change in concentration per unit time
    C. Total time taken for the reaction to complete
    D. Energy released per mole
    **Answer:** B. Change in concentration per unit time
    **Explanation:** Chemical kinetics measures how fast reactants are consumed or products are formed, which is expressed as the change in their concentration over a period of time.

134. Which of the following factors does NOT affect the rate of a chemical reaction?
    A. Temperature
    B. Concentration of reactants
    C. Enthalpy change of the reaction (ΔH)
    D. Presence of a catalyst
    **Answer:** C. Enthalpy change of the reaction (ΔH)
    **Explanation:** ΔH relates to the overall energy difference between reactants and products (thermodynamics) but not the energy barrier (activation energy) or path taken, which determines the rate (kinetics).

135. For the rate law, Rate = k[A]²[B], what is the overall order of the reaction?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** C. 3
    **Explanation:** The overall order is the sum of the individual orders of the reactants in the rate law. Overall order = (order of A) + (order of B) = 2 + 1 = 3.

136. A catalyst speeds up a reaction by...
    A. Increasing the temperature of the system
    B. Increasing the concentration of reactants
    C. Increasing the enthalpy change (ΔH)
    D. Providing an alternative reaction pathway with a lower activation energy
    **Answer:** D. Providing an alternative reaction pathway with a lower activation energy
    **Explanation:** A catalyst offers a different mechanism for the reaction. By lowering the activation energy barrier (E_a), a larger fraction of reactant molecules has sufficient energy to react, thus increasing the rate.

137. The minimum energy required for reactant molecules to form products is called...
    A. Kinetic energy
    B. Potential energy
    C. Activation energy
    D. Free energy
    **Answer:** C. Activation energy
    **Explanation:** Activation energy (E_a) is the energy barrier that must be overcome for a reaction to proceed.

138. If the half-life of a first-order reaction is constant, it means the half-life...
    A. Depends on temperature
    B. Depends on the initial concentration
    C. Is independent of the initial concentration
    D. Is equal to the rate constant
    **Answer:** C. Is independent of the initial concentration
    **Explanation:** A unique characteristic of first-order reactions is that their half-life (t_½ = 0.693/k) depends only on the rate constant (k) and not on the starting concentration of the reactant.

139. In a reaction mechanism, the rate-determining step is the...
    A. Fastest step
    B. Slowest step
    C. First step
    D. Last step
    **Answer:** B. Slowest step
    **Explanation:** The overall rate of a multi-step reaction is limited by the speed of its slowest step, much like a convoy's speed is determined by the slowest vehicle.

140. An enzyme is an example of a...
    A. Heterogeneous catalyst
    B. Biological catalyst
    C. Inhibitor
    D. Reaction intermediate
    **Answer:** B. Biological catalyst
    **Explanation:** Enzymes are proteins that act as highly specific catalysts for biochemical reactions in living organisms.

141. The Arrhenius equation relates the rate constant (k) to...
    A. Concentration and time
    B. Temperature and activation energy
    C. Enthalpy and entropy
    D. Pressure and volume
    **Answer:** B. Temperature and activation energy
    **Explanation:** The Arrhenius equation (k = Ae^(-Ea/RT)) mathematically describes how the rate constant changes with absolute temperature and the activation energy barrier.

142. Increasing the surface area of a solid reactant will...
    A. Decrease the reaction rate
    B. Increase the reaction rate
    C. Have no effect on the reaction rate
    D. Change the products of the reaction
    **Answer:** B. Increase the reaction rate
    **Explanation:** For heterogeneous reactions involving a solid, increasing the surface area (e.g., by grinding it into a powder) exposes more reactant particles to collision, thereby increasing the rate of reaction.

143. According to collision theory, for a reaction to occur, molecules must collide with...
    A. Sufficient energy only
    B. Correct orientation only
    C. Sufficient energy and correct orientation
    D. Any energy and any orientation
    **Answer:** C. Sufficient energy and correct orientation
    **Explanation:** Collision theory states that for a collision to be effective (lead to a reaction), the colliding particles must have energy equal to or greater than the activation energy AND they must have a proper spatial orientation for bond breaking and forming.

144. In the rate law, Rate = k[A], what are the units of the rate constant k?
    A. M s⁻¹
    B. M⁻¹ s⁻¹
    C. s⁻¹
    D. M⁻² s⁻¹
    **Answer:** C. s⁻¹
    **Explanation:** For a first-order reaction, Rate (M s⁻¹) = k [A] (M). To make the units on both sides equal, the units of k must be s⁻¹.

---

**Chapter 13: Chemical Equilibrium**

145. Chemical equilibrium is reached when...
    A. The concentrations of reactants and products are equal
    B. The reaction stops completely
    C. The rates of the forward and reverse reactions are equal
    D. All reactants have been converted to products
    **Answer:** C. The rates of the forward and reverse reactions are equal
    **Explanation:** Equilibrium is a dynamic state where the forward reaction (reactants to products) and the reverse reaction (products to reactants) are occurring at the same rate, resulting in no net change in concentrations.

146. For the reaction: N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g), increasing the pressure will...
    A. Shift the equilibrium to the left
    B. Shift the equilibrium to the right
    C. Have no effect on the equilibrium
    D. Decrease the reaction rate
    **Answer:** B. Shift the equilibrium to the right
    **Explanation:** According to Le Châtelier's principle, increasing the pressure will cause the equilibrium to shift in the direction that reduces the pressure, which is the side with fewer moles of gas. The reactant side has 1+3=4 moles of gas, and the product side has 2 moles of gas. Therefore, it shifts right.

147. Consider the endothermic reaction: A + B + Heat ⇌ C. Increasing the temperature will...
    A. Shift the equilibrium to the left and decrease K_c
    B. Shift the equilibrium to the right and increase K_c
    C. Shift the equilibrium to the left and increase K_c
    D. Shift the equilibrium to the right and decrease K_c
    **Answer:** B. Shift the equilibrium to the right and increase K_c
    **Explanation:** Treating heat as a reactant, adding more heat (increasing T) will shift the equilibrium to the right to consume the added heat. For an endothermic reaction, increasing the temperature increases the value of the equilibrium constant, K_c.

148. The equilibrium constant expression, K_c, for the reaction 2NO(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2NO₂(g) is...
    A. [NO₂]/([NO][O₂])
    B. [NO₂]²/([NO]²[O₂])
    C. ([NO]²[O₂])/[NO₂]²
    D. 2[NO₂]/(2[NO] + [O₂])
    **Answer:** B. [NO₂]²/([NO]²[O₂])
    **Explanation:** The K_c expression is the product of the concentrations of the products raised to their stoichiometric coefficients, divided by the product of the concentrations of the reactants raised to their coefficients.

149. If the equilibrium constant K_c for a reaction is very large (e.g., K_c = 10⁸), it means that at equilibrium...
    A. The concentration of reactants is much larger than products.
    B. The reaction has not started.
    C. The concentration of products is much larger than reactants.
    D. The concentrations of reactants and products are nearly equal.
    **Answer:** C. The concentration of products is much larger than reactants.
    **Explanation:** A large K_c value indicates that the ratio of [Products]/[Reactants] is very large, meaning the equilibrium lies far to the right and the reaction strongly favors the formation of products.

150. For the equilibrium: CaCO₃(s) ⇌ CaO(s) + CO₂(g), the expression for K_p is...
    A. K_p = P_CO₂
    B. K_p = (P_CaO * P_CO₂) / P_CaCO₃
    C. K_p = P_CaO * P_CO₂
    D. K_p = P_CO₂ / P_CaCO₃
    **Answer:** A. K_p = P_CO₂
    **Explanation:** In an equilibrium constant expression, the concentrations (or partial pressures) of pure solids and pure liquids are considered constant and are omitted. Therefore, only the gaseous product, CO₂, appears in the K_p expression.

151. What is the effect of adding a catalyst to a system at equilibrium?
    A. It shifts the equilibrium to the right.
    B. It shifts the equilibrium to the left.
    C. It increases the value of K_c.
    D. It has no effect on the equilibrium position.
    **Answer:** D. It has no effect on the aequilibrium position.
    **Explanation:** A catalyst speeds up the rates of both the forward and reverse reactions equally. It helps the system reach equilibrium faster but does not change the concentrations of species at equilibrium or the value of K_c.

152. The solubility product constant, K_sp, is the equilibrium constant for the...
    A. Reaction of an acid with a base
    B. Formation of a solid from its ions
    C. Dissolution of a sparingly soluble salt
    D. Combustion of a solid
    **Answer:** C. Dissolution of a sparingly soluble salt
    **Explanation:** K_sp represents the equilibrium established when a slightly soluble ionic solid dissolves in a solvent (usually water) to form its constituent ions.

153. Adding sodium chloride (NaCl) to a saturated solution of silver chloride (AgCl) will...
    A. Increase the solubility of AgCl
    B. Decrease the solubility of AgCl
    C. Have no effect on the solubility of AgCl
    D. Cause the AgCl to turn blue
    **Answer:** B. Decrease the solubility of AgCl
    **Explanation:** This is the common-ion effect. Adding Cl⁻ ions (from NaCl) to the equilibrium AgCl(s) ⇌ Ag⁺(aq) + Cl⁻(aq) will shift the equilibrium to the left, causing more AgCl(s) to precipitate and thus decreasing its solubility.

154. If the reaction quotient Q is greater than the equilibrium constant K, the reaction will...
    A. Proceed to the right to reach equilibrium.
    B. Proceed to the left to reach equilibrium.
    C. Is already at equilibrium.
    D. Shift towards the side with more moles of gas.
    **Answer:** B. Proceed to the left to reach equilibrium.
    **Explanation:** If Q > K, the current ratio of products to reactants is too high. To reach equilibrium, the reaction must shift to the left, consuming products and forming reactants, until Q decreases to the value of K.

155. For which of the following reactions will K_p be equal to K_c?
    A. N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g)
    B. 2SO₃(g) ⇌ 2SO₂(g) + O₂(g)
    C. H₂(g) + I₂(g) ⇌ 2HI(g)
    D. PCl₅(g) ⇌ PCl₃(g) + Cl₂(g)
    **Answer:** C. H₂(g) + I₂(g) ⇌ 2HI(g)
    **Explanation:** K_p = K_c(RT)^(Δn_gas). K_p will equal K_c when Δn_gas (moles of gaseous products - moles of gaseous reactants) is zero. In reaction C, Δn_gas = 2 - (1+1) = 0.

156. The Haber process for synthesizing ammonia is N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g), ΔH = -92 kJ. To maximize the yield of ammonia, the conditions should be...
    A. High temperature and high pressure
    B. Low temperature and high pressure
    C. High temperature and low pressure
    D. Low temperature and low pressure
    **Answer:** B. Low temperature and high pressure
    **Explanation:** The forward reaction is exothermic (ΔH < 0), so a low temperature will favor the products. The forward reaction reduces the number of gas moles (from 4 to 2), so a high pressure will favor the products. (Note: In practice, a moderately high temperature is used for a reasonable rate).

---

**Chapter 14: Redox Reactions and Electrochemistry**

157. In an electrochemical cell, oxidation always occurs at the...
    A. Cathode
    B. Anode
    C. Salt bridge
    D. Positive electrode
    **Answer:** B. Anode
    **Explanation:** By definition, the anode is the electrode where oxidation (loss of electrons) takes place, regardless of whether the cell is galvanic or electrolytic. (Mnemonic: An Ox).

158. A galvanic (voltaic) cell...
    A. Uses electricity to drive a non-spontaneous reaction
    B. Converts chemical energy into electrical energy
    C. Requires an external power source
    D. Is another name for an electrolytic cell
    **Answer:** B. Converts chemical energy into electrical energy
    **Explanation:** A galvanic cell utilizes a spontaneous redox reaction to produce an electric current.

159. In the electrolysis of molten sodium chloride (NaCl), sodium metal is formed at the...
    A. Anode, and it is a reduction process
    B. Cathode, and it is a reduction process
    C. Anode, and it is an oxidation process
    D. Cathode, and it is an oxidation process
    **Answer:** B. Cathode, and it is a reduction process
    **Explanation:** Na⁺ ions are positive and are attracted to the negative electrode (the cathode). At the cathode, they gain electrons (reduction) to form neutral Na metal: Na⁺ + e⁻ → Na.

160. What is the function of a salt bridge in a galvanic cell?
    A. To allow electrons to flow between half-cells
    B. To mix the solutions in the half-cells
    C. To provide a source of voltage
    D. To maintain charge neutrality by allowing ion flow
    **Answer:** D. To maintain charge neutrality by allowing ion flow
    **Explanation:** As electrons flow from the anode to the cathode, charge would build up in each half-cell. The salt bridge allows ions to migrate (anions to anode, cations to cathode) to neutralize this charge buildup and complete the electrical circuit.

161. In a galvanic cell, the electrode with the more positive standard reduction potential (E°) will be the...
    A. Anode
    B. Cathode
    C. Negative electrode
    D. Site of oxidation
    **Answer:** B. Cathode
    **Explanation:** A more positive E° indicates a greater tendency to be reduced. The site of reduction is the cathode.

162. Given the standard reduction potentials:
    Zn²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻ → Zn(s)  E° = -0.76 V
    Cu²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻ → Cu(s)  E° = +0.34 V
    What is the standard cell potential (E°_cell) for a cell made with these two half-cells?
    A. -1.10 V
    B. -0.42 V
    C. +0.42 V
    D. +1.10 V
    **Answer:** D. +1.10 V
    **Explanation:** The half-reaction with the more positive E° is the reduction site (cathode): Cu²⁺/Cu. The other half-reaction is the oxidation site (anode): Zn/Zn²⁺.
    E°_cell = E°_cathode - E°_anode = (+0.34 V) - (-0.76 V) = 0.34 V + 0.76 V = +1.10 V.

163. The process of coating one metal with another using electrolysis is called...
    A. Corrosion
    B. Galvanizing
    C. Electroplating
    D. Alloying
    **Answer:** C. Electroplating
    **Explanation:** Electroplating is an electrolytic process where the object to be coated is made the cathode, and metal ions in solution are reduced onto its surface.

164. During the rusting of iron, iron acts as the...
    A. Cathode
    B. Anode
    C. Electrolyte
    D. Oxidizing agent
    **Answer:** B. Anode
    **Explanation:** Corrosion is an electrochemical process. In the case of rusting, the iron metal is oxidized (Fe → Fe²⁺ + 2e⁻), which means it is the site of oxidation, the anode.

165. A battery that cannot be recharged is called a...
    A. Secondary cell
    B. Primary cell
    C. Fuel cell
    D. Lead-acid battery
    **Answer:** B. Primary cell
    **Explanation:** Primary cells (like common dry cells) use redox reactions that are not easily reversible, so they are discarded after use. Secondary cells (like car batteries or Li-ion batteries) are rechargeable.

166. In the cell notation: Mg(s) | Mg²⁺(aq) || Ag⁺(aq) | Ag(s), the species being oxidized is...
    A. Ag⁺
    B. Ag
    C. Mg²⁺
    D. Mg
    **Answer:** D. Mg
    **Explanation:** By convention, the anode (site of oxidation) is written on the left side of the cell notation. The reaction at the anode is Mg(s) → Mg²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻.

167. Faraday's constant (F) represents the charge of...
    A. One electron
    B. One proton
    C. One mole of electrons
    D. One mole of protons
    **Answer:** C. One mole of electrons
    **Explanation:** Faraday's constant (≈ 96,485 Coulombs per mole) is the magnitude of electric charge per mole of electrons.

168. To protect a steel pipeline from corrosion, it is often connected to a block of magnesium. This is an example of...
    A. Electroplating
    B. Galvanizing
    C. Sacrificial protection
    D. Passivation
    **Answer:** C. Sacrificial protection
    **Explanation:** Magnesium is more reactive (has a more negative E°) than iron (in steel). It will therefore act as the anode and corrode "sacrificially," protecting the steel pipeline which is forced to be the cathode.

---

**Chapter 15: Introduction to Organic Compounds**

169. The unique ability of carbon atoms to bond with each other to form long chains and rings is called...
    A. Isomerism
    B. Catenation
    C. Hybridization
    D. Aromaticity
    **Answer:** B. Catenation
    **Explanation:** Catenation is the ability of an element to form covalent bonds with itself, resulting in chain or ring structures. Carbon exhibits this property to a greater extent than any other element.

170. Which of the following is the functional group of an alcohol?
    A. -CHO
    B. -COOH
    C. -OH
    D. -CO-
    **Answer:** C. -OH
    **Explanation:** The hydroxyl group (-OH) attached to a saturated carbon atom is the functional group that defines a molecule as an alcohol.

171. What is the IUPAC name for the compound CH₃CH(CH₃)CH₂CH₃?
    A. Pentane
    B. 2,2-Dimethylbutane
    C. 2-Methylbutane
    D. 3-Methylbutane
    **Answer:** C. 2-Methylbutane
    **Explanation:** The longest continuous carbon chain has four carbons (butane). There is a methyl group on the second carbon when numbered from the left (the end closest to the branch), giving the name 2-Methylbutane.

172. Compounds that have the same molecular formula but different structural formulas are called...
    A. Allotropes
    B. Isotopes
    C. Isomers
    D. Polymers
    **Answer:** C. Isomers
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of isomers. They share the same types and numbers of atoms but differ in how those atoms are connected or arranged in space.

173. The general formula for alkanes is...
    A. C_n H_n
    B. C_n H_₂n
    C. C_n H_(2n-2)
    D. C_n H_(2n+2)
    **Answer:** D. C_n H_(2n+2)
    **Explanation:** This general formula applies to all acyclic (non-ring) saturated hydrocarbons (alkanes).

174. Benzene is best described as an...
    A. Aliphatic hydrocarbon
    B. Alkyne
    C. Alkene
    D. Aromatic hydrocarbon
    **Answer:** D. Aromatic hydrocarbon
    **Explanation:** Benzene is the parent compound for aromatic hydrocarbons, which are characterized by their special stability due to a delocalized pi electron system.

175. Ethanol (CH₃CH₂OH) and dimethyl ether (CH₃OCH₃) both have the formula C₂H₆O. They are examples of...
    A. Chain isomers
    B. Positional isomers
    C. Geometric isomers
    D. Functional group isomers
    **Answer:** D. Functional group isomers
    **Explanation:** Although they have the same molecular formula, they have different functional groups (alcohol vs. ether) and thus belong to different classes of compounds with very different properties.

176. Which of the following compounds can exhibit cis-trans isomerism?
    A. Propene
    B. But-1-ene
    C. But-2-ene
    D. 2-Methylpropene
    **Answer:** C. But-2-ene
    **Explanation:** Cis-trans isomerism in alkenes requires that each carbon of the double bond be attached to two *different* groups. In but-2-ene (CH₃-CH=CH-CH₃), each carbon of the C=C bond is attached to a -CH₃ group and an -H atom, allowing for cis and trans arrangements. The other options have at least one C=C carbon attached to two identical groups (e.g., two -H atoms).

177. Unsaturated hydrocarbons are characterized by the presence of...
    A. Only single bonds
    B. Carbon-carbon multiple bonds
    C. Oxygen atoms
    D. A ring structure
    **Answer:** B. Carbon-carbon multiple bonds
    **Explanation:** Unsaturated hydrocarbons (alkenes and alkynes) contain at least one carbon-carbon double (C=C) or triple (C≡C) bond.

178. The functional group R-COOH represents a(n)...
    A. Aldehyde
    B. Ester
    C. Carboxylic acid
    D. Amine
    **Answer:** C. Carboxylic acid
    **Explanation:** The -COOH group is known as the carboxyl group, the defining functional group of carboxylic acids.

179. A chiral carbon atom is a carbon atom that is bonded to...
    A. Four hydrogen atoms
    B. Four other carbon atoms
    C. Four different atoms or groups
    D. At least one double bond
    **Answer:** C. Four different atoms or groups
    **Explanation:** A chiral (or asymmetric) carbon center is a requirement for a molecule to exhibit optical isomerism (enantiomerism). It is an sp³ hybridized carbon with four unique substituents.

180. The common name for ethanoic acid is...
    A. Formic acid
    B. Acetic acid
    C. Butyric acid
    D. Citric acid
    **Answer:** B. Acetic acid
    **Explanation:** Ethanoic acid (CH₃COOH) is the main component of vinegar, and its common name is acetic acid.

---

**Chapter 16: Reactions of Organic Molecules**

181. The reaction of ethene (C₂H₄) with hydrogen (H₂) to form ethane (C₂H₆) is an example of...
    A. Substitution
    B. Addition
    C. Elimination
    D. Rearrangement
    **Answer:** B. Addition
    **Explanation:** A hydrogen molecule is added across the C=C double bond of ethene, breaking the pi bond and forming a saturated alkane. This is a classic addition reaction (hydrogenation).

182. When ethanol is heated with concentrated sulfuric acid, ethene gas is produced. This reaction is...
    A. Hydration
    B. Dehydration
    C. Hydrogenation
    D. Oxidation
    **Answer:** B. Dehydration
    **Explanation:** Concentrated sulfuric acid acts as a dehydrating agent, removing a molecule of water (an H from one carbon and the OH from the adjacent carbon) from ethanol to form a double bond. This is an elimination reaction.

183. A reaction mechanism involving species with unpaired electrons is called a...
    A. Nucleophilic mechanism
    B. Electrophilic mechanism
    C. Free radical mechanism
    D. Polar mechanism
