    **Answer:** C. Free radical mechanism
    **Explanation:** Free radicals are atoms or groups with an unpaired electron, and mechanisms that proceed via these highly reactive intermediates are known as free radical mechanisms.

184. The product of the oxidation of a secondary alcohol is a(n)...
    A. Aldehyde
    B. Ketone
    C. Carboxylic acid
    D. Ester
    **Answer:** B. Ketone
    **Explanation:** Secondary alcohols (R₂CHOH) are oxidized to ketones (R₂C=O) by removing the two hydrogens from the C-OH group.

185. The reaction between a carboxylic acid and an alcohol in the presence of an acid catalyst produces...
    A. An ether and water
    B. An aldehyde and water
    C. A ketone and water
    D. An ester and water
    **Answer:** D. An ester and water
    **Explanation:** This reaction is called Fischer esterification, where the -OH from the carboxylic acid and the -H from the alcohol are eliminated as water, forming an ester linkage (-COO-).

186. The monomer used to make the polymer poly(ethene) is...
    A. Ethane
    B. Ethene
    C. Ethyne
    D. Ethanol
    **Answer:** B. Ethene
    **Explanation:** Poly(ethene), or polyethylene, is an addition polymer formed from the polymerization of ethene monomers (CH₂=CH₂).

187. Nylon is an example of a...
    A. Addition polymer
    B. Polyester
    C. Polyamide
    D. Polyether
    **Answer:** C. Polyamide
    **Explanation:** Nylon is a condensation polymer characterized by repeating units joined by amide linkages (-CONH-).

188. In an S_N1 reaction, the rate-determining step involves the formation of a...
    A. Free radical
    B. Carbanion
    C. Carbocation
    D. Transition state only
    **Answer:** C. Carbocation
    **Explanation:** The S_N1 (Substitution Nucleophilic Unimolecular) mechanism is a two-step process where the first and slowest step is the departure of the leaving group to form a planar carbocation intermediate.

189. A positive Tollen's test (formation of a silver mirror) indicates the presence of...
    A. An alcohol
    B. A ketone
    C. An alkene
    D. An aldehyde
    **Answer:** D. An aldehyde
    **Explanation:** Tollen's reagent is a mild oxidizing agent that selectively oxidizes aldehydes to carboxylic acid anions, while the Ag⁺ ions in the reagent are reduced to metallic silver, forming a mirror. Ketones do not react.

190. Saponification is the alkaline hydrolysis of...
    A. An ether
    B. An ester
    C. An alkane
    D. An alcohol
    **Answer:** B. An ester
    **Explanation:** Saponification specifically refers to the hydrolysis of an ester using a strong base (like NaOH), which produces a carboxylate salt (soap, if starting from fats) and an alcohol.

191. The reaction CH₄ + Cl₂ --(UV light)--> CH₃Cl + HCl is an example of...
    A. Electrophilic addition
    B. Nucleophilic substitution
    C. Free radical substitution
    D. Elimination
    **Answer:** C. Free radical substitution
    **Explanation:** The reaction between an alkane and a halogen, initiated by UV light, proceeds via a free radical chain mechanism where a hydrogen atom is substituted by a halogen atom.

192. Condensation polymerization is characterized by...
    A. The formation of a polymer with the same empirical formula as the monomer
    B. The joining of monomers with no other products formed
    C. The joining of monomers with the elimination of a small molecule like water
    D. The use of only alkene monomers
    **Answer:** C. The joining of monomers with the elimination of a small molecule like water
    **Explanation:** This is the key difference between condensation and addition polymerization. In condensation, monomers with two functional groups react, and a small molecule (often water) is produced for each new bond formed.

---

**Chapter 17: Laboratory Techniques and Safety**

193. Which piece of laboratory apparatus is most suitable for accurately preparing 250.0 cm³ of a standard solution?
    A. A 250 cm³ beaker
    B. A 250 cm³ graduated cylinder
    C. A 250 cm³ conical flask
    D. A 250 cm³ volumetric flask
    **Answer:** D. A 250 cm³ volumetric flask
    **Explanation:** Volumetric flasks are calibrated to contain a very precise volume at a specific temperature and are the standard apparatus for preparing solutions of accurate concentrations.

194. When reading the volume of a liquid in a burette, the reading should be taken from the...
    A. Top of the meniscus
    B. Bottom of the meniscus
    C. Average of the top and bottom
    D. Mark closest to the liquid level
    **Answer:** B. Bottom of the meniscus
    **Explanation:** For most aqueous solutions, the surface of the liquid (meniscus) is concave. By convention, the volume is read from the bottom of this curve at eye level.

195. The correct procedure for diluting a concentrated acid is to...
    A. Add water to the acid
    B. Add acid to the water
    C. Mix acid and water simultaneously
    D. Cool the acid before adding water
    **Answer:** B. Add acid to the water
    **Explanation:** This is a crucial safety rule (AAA: Always Add Acid). The dilution of strong acids is highly exothermic. Adding acid to water allows the large volume of water to dissipate the heat safely. Adding water to acid can cause the water to boil and splash concentrated acid.

196. Which piece of personal protective equipment (PPE) is mandatory for all experiments in a chemistry laboratory?
    A. Gloves
    B. Fume hood
    C. Lab coat
    D. Safety goggles
    **Answer:** D. Safety goggles
    **Explanation:** Protecting the eyes from chemical splashes, fumes, and projectiles is the most critical and non-negotiable safety requirement in a chemistry lab.

197. A student is testing an unknown solution. Upon adding dilute hydrochloric acid, vigorous effervescence is observed, and the gas produced turns limewater milky. The anion present is likely...
    A. Sulfate (SO₄²⁻)
    B. Chloride (Cl⁻)
    C. Carbonate (CO₃²⁻)
    D. Nitrate (NO₃⁻)
    **Answer:** C. Carbonate (CO₃²⁻)
    **Explanation:** Acids react with carbonates to produce carbon dioxide gas (effervescence). CO₂ gas turns limewater (calcium hydroxide solution) milky due to the formation of a white precipitate of calcium carbonate.

198. A flame test on a sample produced an intense yellow flame. The cation present is most likely...
    A. K⁺
    B. Ca²⁺
    C. Na⁺
    D. Cu²⁺
    **Answer:** C. Na⁺
    **Explanation:** Sodium ions produce a characteristic and very strong yellow/orange color in a flame test. K⁺ is lilac, Ca²⁺ is brick-red, and Cu²⁺ is blue/green.

199. To separate two immiscible liquids, such as oil and water, the most appropriate apparatus to use is a...
    A. Filter funnel
    B. Separating funnel
    C. Burette
    D. Crucible
    **Answer:** B. Separating funnel
    **Explanation:** A separating funnel allows the two immiscible liquid layers to settle, and the stopcock at the bottom can then be used to drain off the bottom layer carefully, separating it from the top layer.

200. What does the GHS hazard pictogram showing a flame over a circle represent?
    A. Flammable
    B. Explosive
    C. Corrosive
    D. Oxidizing
    **Answer:** D. Oxidizing
    **Explanation:** This symbol indicates an oxidizing hazard. Oxidizers can cause or contribute to the combustion of other materials, usually by yielding oxygen. A simple flame symbol indicates a flammable hazard.

201. When using an electronic balance, the first step after ensuring it is clean is to...
    A. Place the substance on the pan
    B. Record the mass of the empty container
    C. Tare the balance
    D. Calibrate the balance with a standard weight
    **Answer:** C. Tare the balance
    **Explanation:** Taring (or zeroing) the balance, often with the weighing container already on it, ensures that the measurement displayed will be only the mass of the substance being added.

202. Which reagent is used to test for the presence of halide ions (Cl⁻, Br⁻, I⁻) in a solution?
    A. Barium chloride
    B. Sodium hydroxide
    C. Silver nitrate
    D. Dilute sulfuric acid
    **Answer:** C. Silver nitrate
    **Explanation:** After acidifying with nitric acid, adding silver nitrate solution (AgNO₃) will cause the precipitation of silver halides (AgCl, AgBr, AgI), which have characteristic colors and solubilities in ammonia.

203. Which test confirms the presence of ammonia gas?
    A. It relights a glowing splint.
    B. It extinguishes a burning splint.
    C. It turns damp red litmus paper blue.
    D. It turns limewater milky.
    **Answer:** C. It turns damp red litmus paper blue.
    **Explanation:** Ammonia is an alkaline gas. It dissolves in the moisture on the litmus paper to form ammonium hydroxide, a weak base, which turns the red litmus paper blue.

204. A student obtains titres of 25.50 cm³, 25.10 cm³, and 25.15 cm³. The average titre used for calculation should be...
    A. 25.25 cm³
    B. 25.13 cm³
    C. 25.33 cm³
    D. 25.10 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 25.13 cm³
    **Explanation:** In titration, you use only the concordant (closely agreeing) titres for the average. The first reading (25.50 cm³) is a rough titre and/or not in agreement with the other two. The average is calculated from the concordant results: (25.10 + 25.15) / 2 = 25.125 cm³. This is typically rounded to two decimal places, giving 25.13 cm³.

---

**Chapter 18: Experimental Investigations**

205. In a titration, the solution of known concentration is called the...
    A. Analyte
    B. Indicator
    C. Titrant
    D. Primary standard
    **Answer:** C. Titrant
    **Explanation:** The titrant (or standard solution) is the solution in the burette whose concentration is accurately known. The analyte is the solution of unknown concentration in the conical flask.

206. A primary standard used in acid-base titrations should NOT be...
    A. Highly pure
    B. Stable in air
    C. Hygroscopic
    D. Have a high molar mass
    **Answer:** C. Hygroscopic
    **Explanation:** A hygroscopic substance absorbs moisture from the air, which changes its mass and makes it impossible to weigh out an accurate amount. This is a disqualifying characteristic for a primary standard.

207. Which of the following is a suitable method for monitoring the rate of the reaction: Zn(s) + 2HCl(aq) → ZnCl₂(aq) + H₂(g)?
    A. Measuring the change in pH
    B. Using a colorimeter
    C. Collecting the gas evolved in a syringe over time
    D. Titrating samples with NaOH
    **Answer:** C. Collecting the gas evolved in a syringe over time
    **Explanation:** This reaction produces hydrogen gas at a measurable rate. Collecting the volume of gas at regular time intervals is a direct and effective way to monitor the reaction's progress.

208. In a calorimetry experiment, the main source of error is often...
    A. Inaccurate measurement of mass
    B. Heat loss to the surroundings
    C. Use of an incorrect indicator
    D. The reaction not going to completion
    **Answer:** B. Heat loss to the surroundings
    **Explanation:** No calorimeter is perfectly insulated. Unintended heat exchange with the laboratory environment is typically the most significant source of inaccuracy in simple calorimetry experiments.

209. The process of accurately determining the concentration of a solution by titrating it against a primary standard is called...
    A. Dilution
    B. Standardization
    C. Neutralization
    D. Calibration
    **Answer:** B. Standardization
    **Explanation:** Standardization is the specific term for this process of finding the exact concentration of a secondary standard (like HCl or NaOH) by using a reliable primary standard.

210. In a thermometric titration of NaOH with HCl, the equivalence point is indicated by...
    A. A sharp color change
    B. The maximum temperature reached
    C. The formation of a precipitate
    D. A constant pH reading
    **Answer:** B. The maximum temperature reached
    **Explanation:** Neutralization is exothermic. As the reaction proceeds, the temperature rises. The highest temperature is reached when the reaction is just complete (at the equivalence point), after which adding more cool titrant will cause the temperature to drop.

211. Which indicator would be most suitable for the titration of a weak acid (e.g., ethanoic acid) with a strong base (e.g., sodium hydroxide)?
    A. Methyl orange (pH range 3.1 - 4.4)
    B. Litmus (pH range 5.0 - 8.0)
    C. Bromothymol blue (pH range 6.0 - 7.6)
    D. Phenolphthalein (pH range 8.3 - 10.0)
    **Answer:** D. Phenolphthalein (pH range 8.3 - 10.0)
    **Explanation:** The salt formed from a weak acid and a strong base (e.g., sodium ethanoate) will hydrolyze water to produce a slightly alkaline solution at the equivalence point (pH > 7). An indicator that changes color in the alkaline range, like phenolphthalein, is therefore required.

212. A "clock reaction" is used in kinetics experiments to determine...
    A. The overall enthalpy change
    B. The initial rate of reaction
    C. The equilibrium constant
    D. The molecular formula of a product
    **Answer:** B. The initial rate of reaction
    **Explanation:** Clock reactions are designed so that after a certain amount of product has formed, a sudden observable change occurs. By measuring the time taken for this change under different starting concentrations, the initial rate (which is proportional to 1/time) can be determined, which helps in finding reaction orders.

213. When titrating iron(II) ions with potassium manganate(VII) solution, no external indicator is needed because...
    A. The reaction is neutral.
    B. Iron(II) ions are colored.
    C. The reaction is very slow.
    D. Potassium manganate(VII) acts as its own indicator.
    **Answer:** D. Potassium manganate(VII) acts as its own indicator.
    **Explanation:** Potassium manganate(VII) solution (KMnO₄) has an intense purple color. The manganate(VII) ion (MnO₄⁻) is consumed during the reaction and converted to the nearly colorless Mn²⁺ ion. The endpoint is reached when the first drop of excess KMnO₄ is added, imparting a permanent faint pink color to the solution.

214. In preparing 1.0 dm³ of 0.5 M NaCl solution, a student should dissolve... (Na=23, Cl=35.5)
    A. 58.5 g of NaCl in 1.0 dm³ of water.
    B. 29.25 g of NaCl in 1.0 dm³ of water.
    C. 58.5 g of NaCl and add water up to the 1.0 dm³ mark.
    D. 29.25 g of NaCl and add water up to the 1.0 dm³ mark.
    **Answer:** D. 29.25 g of NaCl and add water up to the 1.0 dm³ mark.
    **Explanation:** First, calculate the required mass. Molar mass of NaCl = 23 + 35.5 = 58.5 g/mol. Mass = Molarity × Volume(L) × Molar Mass = 0.5 mol/L × 1.0 L × 58.5 g/mol = 29.25 g. The correct procedure is to dissolve this mass in a volumetric flask and then add solvent up to the calibration mark to achieve the correct total volume of solution.

215. A possible source of error in a gas collection experiment could be...
    A. The gas syringe sticking
    B. Using an electronic balance
    C. Titrating too quickly
    D. Misreading the pH meter
    **Answer:** A. The gas syringe sticking
    **Explanation:** If the plunger of the gas syringe does not move smoothly, it can cause inaccurate and inconsistent readings of the volume of gas collected over time.

216. "Concordant results" in a titration are titres that...
    A. Are all identical
    B. Are within a specified small range of each other
    C. Increase with each trial
    D. Are obtained using different indicators
    **Answer:** B. Are within a specified small range of each other
    **Explanation:** Concordant titres are typically defined as those that agree within a certain margin, usually ±0.10 cm³ or ±0.20 cm³, indicating precision in the titration technique.

---
**Comprehensive & Mixed Questions (Ch 19-20 Style)**

217. The shape of the ammonia molecule (NH₃) is trigonal pyramidal. This is due to the presence of...
    A. Four bonding pairs of electrons
    B. Three bonding pairs and one lone pair of electrons
    C. Two bonding pairs and two lone pairs of electrons
    D. Resonance structures
    **Answer:** B. Three bonding pairs and one lone pair of electrons
    **Explanation:** The central nitrogen atom has 3 bonds to hydrogen and one non-bonding lone pair. These four electron domains give a tetrahedral electron geometry, but the lone pair results in a trigonal pyramidal molecular shape.

218. In the extraction of iron in a blast furnace, the role of limestone (CaCO₃) is to...
    A. Act as the main reducing agent
    B. Serve as fuel for the furnace
    C. Decompose to form a flux for removing impurities
    D. Catalyze the reduction of iron ore
    **Answer:** C. Decompose to form a flux for removing impurities
    **Explanation:** Limestone decomposes to calcium oxide (CaO), which is a basic oxide. CaO then reacts with acidic impurities like silicon dioxide (SiO₂) to form molten slag (calcium silicate, CaSiO₃), which can be removed.

219. The contact process for the industrial production of sulfuric acid uses ______ as a catalyst.
    A. Iron
    B. Nickel
    C. Vanadium(V) oxide (V₂O₅)
    D. Platinum
    **Answer:** C. Vanadium(V) oxide (V₂O₅)
    **Explanation:** Vanadium(V) oxide is the catalyst used for the key step in the Contact process: the oxidation of SO₂ to SO₃ (2SO₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2SO₃(g)).

220. Ethene can be distinguished from ethane by its reaction with...
    A. Sodium metal
    B. Aqueous bromine
    C. Concentrated sulfuric acid
    D. Dilute hydrochloric acid
    **Answer:** B. Aqueous bromine
    **Explanation:** Ethene is an alkene and will undergo an addition reaction with bromine water, decolorizing it from reddish-brown to colorless. Ethane is a saturated alkane and will not react with bromine water under normal lab conditions.

221. An atom X has an electron configuration of 1s²2s²2p⁶3s²3p⁴. Which of the following statements about X is correct?
    A. It is in Group 4 of the periodic table.
    B. It will form a positive ion with a +2 charge.
    C. It is in Period 3 and Group 16.
    D. It is a metal.
    **Answer:** C. It is in Period 3 and Group 16.
    **Explanation:** The highest principal energy level (n) is 3, so it is in Period 3. The number of valence electrons is 2+4=6. For p-block elements, Group Number = 10 + valence electrons = 10 + 6 = 16. An element in Group 16 (like Sulfur) is a non-metal and tends to gain 2 electrons to form a -2 ion.

222. Hardness in water is caused by the presence of dissolved...
    A. Na⁺ and K⁺ ions
    B. Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺ ions
    C. Cl⁻ and SO₄²⁻ ions
    D. H⁺ and OH⁻ ions
    **Answer:** B. Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺ ions
    **Explanation:** Hard water contains dissolved calcium and/or magnesium salts. These ions react with soap to form an insoluble scum, preventing it from lathering.

223. What is the oxidation number of chromium in the dichromate ion, Cr₂O₇²⁻?
    A. +3
    B. +4
    C. +6
    D. +7
    **Answer:** C. +6
    **Explanation:** Let the oxidation number of Cr be x. Oxygen is usually -2. The sum of oxidation numbers must equal the overall charge of the ion (-2).
    2(x) + 7(-2) = -2
    2x - 14 = -2
    2x = 12
    x = +6.

224. Which of the following processes decreases entropy?
    A. Melting of ice
    B. Boiling of water
    C. Condensation of steam
    D. Sublimation of iodine
    **Answer:** C. Condensation of steam
    **Explanation:** Condensation is the phase change from a highly disordered gas state (steam) to a more ordered liquid state (water). This decrease in disorder corresponds to a decrease in entropy.

225. A student mixed 25 cm³ of 0.2 M HCl with 25 cm³ of 0.2 M NaOH. The resulting solution would be...
    A. Acidic
    B. Basic
    C. Neutral
    D. A buffer
    **Answer:** C. Neutral
    **Explanation:** HCl is a strong acid and NaOH is a strong base. Mixing equal volumes of equimolar solutions results in complete neutralization. Moles of HCl = 0.025 L × 0.2 M = 0.005 mol. Moles of NaOH = 0.025 L × 0.2 M = 0.005 mol. The moles are equal, so the final solution contains only NaCl and water, which is neutral (pH=7).

226. What type of isomerism is shown by CH₃CH₂CH₂CH₃ and CH₃CH(CH₃)₂?
    A. Positional isomerism
    B. Geometric isomerism
    C. Functional group isomerism
    D. Chain isomerism
    **Answer:** D. Chain isomerism
    **Explanation:** Both compounds have the molecular formula C₄H₁₀. They differ in the arrangement of the carbon skeleton: one is a straight chain (n-butane) and the other is a branched chain (isobutane or 2-methylpropane).

227. Anode sludge is an important by-product in the electrolytic refining of...
    A. Iron
    B. Aluminum
    C. Copper
    D. Sodium
    **Answer:** C. Copper
    **Explanation:** During the electrolytic refining of copper, the impure copper anode dissolves. Less reactive metal impurities (like silver, gold, and platinum) do not dissolve and fall to the bottom of the cell as a valuable "anode sludge" or "anode mud".

228. Which of the following metals reacts with steam but not with cold water?
    A. Potassium
    B. Calcium
    C. Magnesium
    D. Copper
    **Answer:** C. Magnesium
    **Explanation:** Reactivity of metals with water decreases down the series. Potassium reacts violently with cold water. Calcium reacts readily. Magnesium reacts very slowly with cold water but readily with steam. Copper does not react with water or steam.

229. Fermentation of glucose produces...
    A. Ethanol and carbon dioxide
    B. Methanol and water
    C. Ethanoic acid and oxygen
    D. Ethene and hydrogen
    **Answer:** A. Ethanol and carbon dioxide
    **Explanation:** Fermentation is the anaerobic process, typically catalyzed by enzymes in yeast, that breaks down sugars like glucose into ethanol and carbon dioxide: C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2C₂H₅OH + 2CO₂.

230. A substance that absorbs moisture from the atmosphere without dissolving in it is said to be...
    A. Efflorescent
    B. Deliquescent
    C. Hygroscopic
    D. Anhydrous
    **Answer:** C. Hygroscopic
    **Explanation:** Hygroscopic substances absorb water vapor from the air. Deliquescent substances are similar but absorb enough water to dissolve completely. Efflorescent substances lose water of crystallization to the air.

231. The bond angle in a molecule with sp hybridization is...
    A. 109.5°
    B. 120°
    C. 180°
    D. 90°
    **Answer:** C. 180°
    **Explanation:** sp hybridization involves one s and one p orbital, resulting in two hybrid orbitals that arrange themselves linearly to be as far apart as possible, giving a 180° bond angle.

232. An element has isotopes with mass numbers 35 and 37 in the ratio of 3:1. The relative atomic mass of the element is...
    A. 35.0
    B. 35.5
    C. 36.0
    D. 37.0
    **Answer:** B. 35.5
    **Explanation:** The abundance of isotope 35 is 3/4 (or 75%), and for isotope 37 is 1/4 (or 25%).
    Relative Atomic Mass = (3/4 × 35) + (1/4 × 37) = 26.25 + 9.25 = 35.5.

233. Which of the following separation techniques is most suitable for separating a mixture of sand and ammonium chloride?
    A. Filtration
    B. Decantation
    C. Sublimation
    D. Fractional distillation
    **Answer:** C. Sublimation
    **Explanation:** Ammonium chloride is a substance that sublimes (turns from solid to gas) upon heating, while sand does not. By heating the mixture, the ammonium chloride will vaporize and can be collected by cooling the vapor elsewhere, leaving the sand behind.

234. An atom has an atomic number of 12 and a mass number of 24. It forms an ion by losing two electrons. The ion has...
    A. 12 protons and 10 electrons
    B. 12 protons and 12 electrons
    C. 10 protons and 12 electrons
    D. 12 protons and 14 electrons
    **Answer:** A. 12 protons and 10 electrons
    **Explanation:** The number of protons (12) never changes when an ion is formed. If the neutral atom (with 12 electrons) loses two electrons, it will have 12 - 2 = 10 electrons.

235. The main function of a fume hood in a laboratory is to...
    A. Provide good lighting for experiments
    B. Prevent fires and explosions
    C. Remove toxic or harmful vapors from the work area
    D. Store chemicals safely
    **Answer:** C. Remove toxic or harmful vapors from the work area
    **Explanation:** A fume hood is a ventilated enclosure that pulls air and airborne contaminants away from the user and out of the laboratory, providing protection from inhalation of hazardous fumes.

236. What volume of 0.5 M H₂SO₄ is required to completely neutralize 20 cm³ of 1.0 M NaOH?
    A. 10 cm³
    B. 20 cm³
    C. 40 cm³
    D. 50 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 20 cm³
    **Explanation:** Balanced equation: H₂SO₄ + 2NaOH → Na₂SO₄ + 2H₂O.
    Use the formula (MₐVₐ)/(MᵦVᵦ) = nₐ/nᵦ.
    (0.5 × Vₐ) / (1.0 × 20) = 1/2
    0.5 Vₐ / 20 = 0.5
    0.5 Vₐ = 10
    Vₐ = 20 cm³.

237. The correct electron configuration for the Fe³⁺ ion is... (Z of Fe = 26)
    A. [Ar] 4s² 3d³
    B. [Ar] 4s¹ 3d⁴
    C. [Ar] 3d⁵
    D. [Ar] 4s² 3d⁶
    **Answer:** C. [Ar] 3d⁵
    **Explanation:** First, write the configuration for neutral Fe: [Ar] 4s² 3d⁶. When forming a cation, electrons are removed from the outermost shell (highest n) first. So, the first two electrons are removed from the 4s orbital, and the third electron is removed from the 3d orbital. This leaves [Ar] 3d⁵.

238. Markovnikov's rule is used to predict the product of the addition of...
    A. H₂ to an alkene
    B. Br₂ to an alkene
    C. An unsymmetrical reagent like HBr to an unsymmetrical alkene
    D. A carboxylic acid to an alcohol
    **Answer:** C. An unsymmetrical reagent like HBr to an unsymmetrical alkene
    **Explanation:** Markovnikov's rule states that in the addition of a protic acid (like HX) to an unsymmetrical alkene, the acidic hydrogen attaches to the carbon with more hydrogen substituents, and the halide group attaches to the carbon with fewer hydrogen substituents (the more substituted carbon).

239. Which of the following oxides is amphoteric?
    A. Na₂O
    B. MgO
    C. Al₂O₃
    D. SO₂
    **Answer:** C. Al₂O₃
    **Explanation:** Amphoteric oxides can react with both acids and bases. Aluminum oxide reacts with acids (e.g., Al₂O₃ + 6HCl → 2AlCl₃ + 3H₂O) and with strong bases (e.g., Al₂O₃ + 2NaOH + 3H₂O → 2Na[Al(OH)₄]).

240. Which gas is responsible for the depletion of the ozone layer?
    A. Carbon dioxide
    B. Sulfur dioxide
    C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
    D. Methane
    **Answer:** C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
    **Explanation:** CFCs, when they reach the stratosphere, are broken down by UV radiation to produce chlorine free radicals. These radicals act as catalysts in the destruction of ozone molecules.

241. The bond type present in a sample of solid potassium is...
    A. Ionic
    B. Covalent
    C. Metallic
    D. Hydrogen bond
    **Answer:** C. Metallic
    **Explanation:** Potassium (K) is a metal. The atoms in a solid metal are held together by metallic bonds, which involve the attraction between positive potassium ions and a sea of delocalized electrons.

242. If the K_sp of AgCl is 1.8 x 10⁻¹⁰, which of the following will occur if [Ag⁺] = 1.0 x 10⁻⁵ M and [Cl⁻] = 1.0 x 10⁻⁵ M are mixed?
    A. A precipitate of AgCl will form.
    B. The solution will remain unsaturated.
    C. The solution will be at equilibrium.
    D. The AgCl will dissolve.
    **Answer:** B. The solution will remain unsaturated.
    **Explanation:** Calculate the ion product, Q_sp = [Ag⁺][Cl⁻] = (1.0 x 10⁻⁵)(1.0 x 10⁻⁵) = 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁰.
    Compare Q_sp to K_sp: 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁰ is less than 1.8 x 10⁻¹⁰. Since Q_sp < K_sp, the solution is unsaturated and no precipitate will form.

243. The process by which complex organic molecules are broken down into simpler ones by microorganisms is known as...
    A. Combustion
    B. Cracking
    C. Decay
    D. Neutralization
    **Answer:** C. Decay
    **Explanation:** Decay or decomposition is the general term for the breakdown of organic matter by bacteria and fungi.

244. Which of these separation techniques is based on the difference in boiling points?
    A. Filtration
    B. Fractional distillation
    C. Chromatography
    D. Crystallization
    **Answer:** B. Fractional distillation
    **Explanation:** Fractional distillation is used to separate a mixture of miscible liquids with different boiling points. The component with the lower boiling point vaporizes first, rises up the fractionating column, and is then condensed and collected.

245. The geometry around the carbon atom in an ethyne (acetylene) molecule is...
    A. Tetrahedral
    B. Trigonal planar
    C. Linear
    D. Bent
    **Answer:** C. Linear
    **Explanation:** The carbon atoms in ethyne (C₂H₂) are joined by a triple bond. Each carbon has two electron domains (one triple bond, one single bond), resulting in sp hybridization and a linear geometry with 180° bond angles.

246. What mass of water is produced when 8 g of hydrogen gas is burned completely in oxygen? (H=1, O=16)
    A. 18 g
    B. 36 g
    C. 72 g
    D. 144 g
    **Answer:** C. 72 g
    **Explanation:** Balanced equation: 2H₂(g) + O₂(g) → 2H₂O(l).
    Molar masses: H₂ = 2 g/mol, H₂O = 18 g/mol.
    Moles of H₂ = 8 g / 2 g/mol = 4 moles.
    Mole ratio of H₂ to H₂O is 2:2 or 1:1. So, 4 moles of H₂ will produce 4 moles of H₂O.
    Mass of H₂O = Moles × Molar Mass = 4 mol × 18 g/mol = 72 g.

247. The temporary hardness of water can be removed by...
    A. Adding sodium carbonate
    B. Boiling
    C. Using an ion-exchange resin
    D. Adding chlorine
    **Answer:** B. Boiling
    **Explanation:** Temporary hardness is caused by dissolved calcium or magnesium hydrogencarbonates. Boiling decomposes these salts into insoluble carbonates, which precipitate out, thus removing the hardness. Adding sodium carbonate and ion-exchange can remove both temporary and permanent hardness.

248. An oxidizing agent is a substance that...
    A. Donates electrons and is oxidized
    B. Donates electrons and is reduced
    C. Accepts electrons and is oxidized
    D. Accepts electrons and is reduced
    **Answer:** D. Accepts electrons and is reduced
    **Explanation:** An oxidizing agent causes another substance to be oxidized (lose electrons). To do this, it must accept those electrons, and in the process, it itself is reduced.

249. Which of the following pairs are allotropes?
    A. Ethene and Ethane
    B. Diamond and Graphite
    C. Water and Ice
    D. Isotopes of oxygen
    **Answer:** B. Diamond and Graphite
    **Explanation:** Allotropes are different structural forms of the same element in the same physical state. Diamond and graphite are both solid forms of the element carbon.

250. When a small amount of acid is added to a buffer solution made of CH₃COOH and CH₃COONa, the pH does not change significantly because the added H⁺ ions react with...
    A. CH₃COOH
    B. CH₃COO⁻
    C. Na⁺
    D. H₂O
    **Answer:** B. CH₃COO⁻
    **Explanation:** The function of the conjugate base (ethanoate ion, CH₃COO⁻) in the buffer is to neutralize any added acid. The H⁺ ions react with CH₃COO⁻ to form the weak acid CH₃COOH, thus preventing a large increase in the H⁺ concentration and a drop in pH.


Of course. Here is a second set of 250 WAEC-standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations, derived from the provided textbook content.

### WAEC Chemistry Practice Questions (Set 2)

**Chapter 1: Fundamentals of Atomic Structure**

251. Rutherford's gold foil experiment provided evidence for the existence of...
    A. Electrons in fixed orbits
    B. A small, dense, positively charged nucleus
    C. Neutrons within the nucleus
    D. The indivisibility of the atom
    **Answer:** B. A small, dense, positively charged nucleus
    **Explanation:** The deflection of some alpha particles at large angles and the bouncing back of a few indicated that the atom's positive charge and mass were concentrated in a tiny, dense central region, which he called the nucleus.

252. Which of the following particles has a mass that is considered negligible when calculating the mass number of an atom?
    A. Proton
    B. Neutron
    C. Electron
    D. Nucleon
    **Answer:** C. Electron
    **Explanation:** The mass of an electron is approximately 1/1836th the mass of a proton. Its contribution to the overall atomic mass is so small that it is usually ignored in mass number calculations.

253. An ion, X²⁻, has 18 electrons. How many protons does the atom X have?
    A. 16
    B. 18
    C. 20
    D. 14
    **Answer:** A. 16
    **Explanation:** The X²⁻ ion has a charge of -2, meaning it has gained 2 electrons compared to the neutral atom. If the ion has 18 electrons, the neutral atom had 18 - 2 = 16 electrons. In a neutral atom, the number of protons equals the number of electrons, so there are 16 protons.

254. Which of the following correctly describes the particles in a Tritium (³₁H) atom?
    A. 1 proton, 1 neutron, 1 electron
    B. 1 proton, 2 neutrons, 1 electron
    C. 1 proton, 3 neutrons, 1 electron
    D. 2 protons, 1 neutron, 2 electrons
    **Answer:** B. 1 proton, 2 neutrons, 1 electron
    **Explanation:** For ³₁H: The subscript (atomic number) is 1, so it has 1 proton. The superscript (mass number) is 3. Number of neutrons = Mass number - Atomic number = 3 - 1 = 2. A neutral atom has electrons equal to protons, so it has 1 electron.

255. The subshells present in the n=2 principal energy level are...
    A. s only
    B. s and p
    C. s, p, and d
    D. p and d
    **Answer:** B. s and p
    **Explanation:** For a given principal shell n, the possible values for the azimuthal quantum number (l) are 0 to n-1. For n=2, l can be 0 (the 's' subshell) and 1 (the 'p' subshell).

256. What is the electron configuration of a sodium ion, Na⁺? (Z of Na = 11)
    A. 1s² 2s² 2p⁶ 3s¹
    B. 1s² 2s² 2p⁶
    C. 1s² 2s² 2p⁵ 3s²
    D. 1s² 2s² 2p⁷
    **Answer:** B. 1s² 2s² 2p⁶
    **Explanation:** A neutral sodium atom has the configuration 1s² 2s² 2p⁶ 3s¹. To form the Na⁺ ion, it loses its single valence electron from the 3s orbital, leaving the stable configuration of Neon.

257. An orbital is defined as a region in space where...
    A. There is a 100% chance of finding an electron
    B. There is a high probability of finding an electron
    C. Two electrons can be found with the same spin
    D. The nucleus is located
    **Answer:** B. There is a high probability of finding an electron
    **Explanation:** Quantum mechanics describes electron locations in terms of probability distributions. An orbital represents the 3D space where there is a 90-95% probability of finding the electron.

258. The principal quantum number (n) primarily determines the...
    A. Shape of an orbital
    B. Orientation of an orbital
    C. Spin of an electron
    D. Energy level and size of an orbital
    **Answer:** D. Energy level and size of an orbital
    **Explanation:** The principal quantum number, n, designates the main energy shell. A larger 'n' value corresponds to a higher energy level and a larger average distance from the nucleus (larger orbital size).

259. The orbital diagram for a neutral oxygen atom (Z=8) shows that the 2p orbitals contain...
    A. Two fully filled orbitals
    B. Four electrons in two orbitals
    C. One fully filled orbital and two half-filled orbitals
    D. Two electrons paired in one orbital, and two empty orbitals
    **Answer:** C. One fully filled orbital and two half-filled orbitals
    **Explanation:** Oxygen has the configuration 1s²2s²2p⁴. According to Hund's Rule, the four 2p electrons will fill as: [↑↓] [↑ ] [↑ ]. This results in one orbital being fully occupied and two being half-occupied.

260. How many d orbitals are there in a d subshell?
    A. 1
    B. 3
    C. 5
    D. 7
    **Answer:** C. 5
    **Explanation:** An 's' subshell has 1 orbital, a 'p' subshell has 3 orbitals, a 'd' subshell has 5 orbitals, and an 'f' subshell has 7 orbitals.

261. The Pauli Exclusion Principle states that...
    A. Electrons fill the lowest energy orbitals first.
    B. Orbitals must be half-filled before they are fully filled.
    C. No two electrons in an atom can have the same four quantum numbers.
    D. The position and momentum of an electron cannot be known simultaneously.
    **Answer:** C. No two electrons in an atom can have the same four quantum numbers.
    **Explanation:** This principle implies that if two electrons are in the same orbital (same n, l, and m_l), they must have opposite spins (different m_s).

262. An atom X has 20 protons and 20 neutrons. Another atom Y has 20 protons and 22 neutrons. X and Y are...
    A. Allotropes
    B. Isobars
    C. Isomers
    D. Isotopes
    **Answer:** D. Isotopes
    **Explanation:** Both atoms have the same number of protons (20), so they are atoms of the same element (Calcium). They have different numbers of neutrons (20 vs 22), which makes them isotopes of that element.

---

**Chapter 2: The Periodic Table and Periodicity**

263. The element in Period 3 and Group 17 is...
    A. Fluorine
    B. Sulfur
    C. Chlorine
    D. Argon
    **Answer:** C. Chlorine
    **Explanation:** Period 3 means the highest electron shell is n=3. Group 17 means it is a halogen with 7 valence electrons. This describes Chlorine (Cl), with configuration [Ne] 3s² 3p⁵.

264. Which of the following has the smallest atomic radius?
    A. Na
    B. Mg
    C. Si
    D. Cl
    **Answer:** D. Cl
    **Explanation:** All elements are in Period 3. Atomic radius decreases across a period from left to right due to increasing effective nuclear charge. Chlorine is the furthest to the right (of these options), so it will be the smallest.

265. Electron affinity is the...
    A. Energy required to remove an electron
    B. Energy change when an electron is added to a gaseous atom
    C. Tendency to attract electrons in a bond
    D. Force holding the nucleus together
    **Answer:** B. Energy change when an electron is added to a gaseous atom
    **Explanation:** Electron affinity measures the energy change (often released) when a neutral atom in the gas phase gains an electron to form a negative ion.

266. Which of the following is an alkaline earth metal?
    A. Li
    B. K
    C. Ca
    D. Sc
    **Answer:** C. Ca
    **Explanation:** Alkaline earth metals are the elements in Group 2 of the periodic table. Calcium (Ca) is in Group 2.

267. The tendency for elements to be non-metallic...
    A. Increases down a group and increases across a period.
    B. Decreases down a group and increases across a period.
    C. Increases down a group and decreases across a period.
    D. Decreases down a group and decreases across a period.
    **Answer:** B. Decreases down a group and increases across a period.
    **Explanation:** Non-metallic character relates to the ability to gain electrons. This ability increases across a period (increasing electronegativity) and decreases down a group (increasing atomic size).

268. Which of the following species are isoelectronic?
    A. Na⁺, Mg²⁺, F⁻
    B. Na⁺, K⁺, Li⁺
    C. Cl⁻, Br⁻, I⁻
    D. O, S, Se
    **Answer:** A. Na⁺, Mg²⁺, F⁻
    **Explanation:** Isoelectronic species have the same number of electrons. Na (11p) as Na⁺ has 10e⁻. Mg (12p) as Mg²⁺ has 10e⁻. F (9p) as F⁻ has 10e⁻. All have 10 electrons.

269. The "staircase" line in the periodic table separates...
    A. s-block and p-block elements
    B. Metals and non-metals
    C. Transition metals and main group elements
    D. Halogens and noble gases
    **Answer:** B. Metals and non-metals
    **Explanation:** The diagonal line separates the metals on the left from the non-metals on the right. Elements located along this line are metalloids.

270. Why is the second ionization energy of Sodium (Na) much larger than its first ionization energy?
    A. The second electron is in a different orbital.
    B. After losing one electron, the nucleus pulls the remaining electrons more strongly.
    C. The second electron is removed from a stable, noble-gas-like core.
    D. The atom is larger after losing the first electron.
    **Answer:** C. The second electron is removed from a stable, noble-gas-like core.
    **Explanation:** The first electron is the lone 3s¹ valence electron. Removing it leaves the Na⁺ ion with the stable electron configuration of Neon ([Ne]). Removing a second electron would require breaking up this very stable, full valence shell, which requires a huge amount of energy.

271. An element with the electron configuration [Ar] 4s¹ is...
    A. A halogen
    B. A noble gas
    C. An alkali metal
    D. An alkaline earth metal
    **Answer:** C. An alkali metal
    **Explanation:** This is the configuration for Potassium (K), Z=19. It has one valence electron in the outermost s-orbital, which is characteristic of the Group 1 alkali metals.

272. A d-block element is one which has its highest energy electron in a...
    A. d orbital
    B. s orbital
    C. p orbital
    D. f orbital
    **Answer:** A. d orbital
    **Explanation:** The blocks of the periodic table are named after the type of orbital being filled with the valence or highest energy electrons. For transition metals, this is the d orbital.

273. Döbereiner's classification of elements was based on...
    A. Octaves
    B. Triads
    C. Atomic number
    D. Electron configuration
    **Answer:** B. Triads
    **Explanation:** Johann Döbereiner observed that some groups of three elements (triads) had similar properties, and the atomic weight of the middle element was the average of the other two.

274. In a given period, the element with the highest electronegativity is typically a...
    A. Alkali metal
    B. Noble gas
    C. Halogen
    D. Transition metal
    **Answer:** C. Halogen
    **Explanation:** Electronegativity increases across a period. Halogens (Group 17) are at the far right and have a strong tendency to attract electrons. Noble gases (Group 18) are usually not assigned electronegativity values as they rarely form bonds.

---

**Chapter 3: Ionic and Covalent Bonding**

275. A coordinate (dative) covalent bond is formed when...
    A. Electrons are transferred between atoms.
    B. Electrons are shared equally between atoms.
    C. One atom provides both electrons for the shared pair.
    D. Two atoms each provide one electron for the shared pair.
    **Answer:** C. One atom provides both electrons for the shared pair.
    **Explanation:** In a coordinate bond, the shared pair of electrons originates from a single atom (the Lewis base) which donates them to another atom (the Lewis acid).

276. Which of the following would be the most brittle?
    A. Copper wire
    B. Graphite sheet
    C. Potassium chloride crystal
    D. A block of wax
    **Answer:** C. Potassium chloride crystal
    **Explanation:** Ionic compounds like KCl are hard but brittle. A strong force can shift the layers of the crystal lattice, bringing like charges next to each other, which causes strong repulsion and shatters the crystal.

277. The bond in a hydrogen chloride (HCl) molecule is best described as...
    A. Ionic
    B. Nonpolar covalent
    C. Polar covalent
    D. Metallic
    **Answer:** C. Polar covalent
    **Explanation:** Chlorine is significantly more electronegative than hydrogen. This leads to unequal sharing of the bonding electrons, creating a dipole with a partial negative charge on Cl and a partial positive charge on H.

278. An example of a compound that is an exception to the octet rule with an incomplete octet is...
    A. CH₄
    B. H₂O
    C. BF₃
    D. PCl₅
    **Answer:** C. BF₃
    **Explanation:** In Boron Trifluoride (BF₃), the central boron atom forms single bonds with three fluorine atoms. It only has 6 valence electrons, an incomplete octet, but it is a stable molecule. PCl₅ has an expanded octet.

279. The electrical conductivity of metals is due to...
    A. The movement of ions
    B. The mobility of valence electrons
    C. The strength of the metallic bonds
    D. The high melting points of metals
    **Answer:** B. The mobility of valence electrons
    **Explanation:** In the "electron sea" model, the delocalized valence electrons are free to move throughout the metallic lattice and can carry an electric current when a potential difference is applied.

280. How many total valence electrons are in the carbonate ion, CO₃²⁻?
    A. 22
    B. 24
    C. 26
    D. 32
    **Answer:** B. 24
    **Explanation:** Carbon (Group 14) has 4 valence e⁻. Oxygen (Group 16) has 6 valence e⁻. The -2 charge means there are 2 extra electrons. Total = 4 + (3 × 6) + 2 = 4 + 18 + 2 = 24.

281. The strength of bonds in the order single, double, triple is...
    A. Triple > Double > Single
    B. Single > Double > Triple
    C. Double > Triple > Single
    D. Single > Triple > Double
    **Answer:** A. Triple > Double > Single
    **Explanation:** A double bond (one sigma, one pi) is stronger than a single bond, and a triple bond (one sigma, two pi) is stronger still, as more electrons are holding the two nuclei together.

282. Steel is an example of an interstitial alloy because...
    A. Zinc atoms replace iron atoms.
    B. Small carbon atoms fit into the spaces between larger iron atoms.
    C. Iron atoms are covalently bonded to carbon.
    D. Iron ions are surrounded by a sea of carbon electrons.
    **Answer:** B. Small carbon atoms fit into the spaces between larger iron atoms.
    **Explanation:** In steel, the smaller carbon atoms occupy the interstices (holes) in the iron crystal lattice, which makes the metal harder and stronger. This defines an interstitial alloy.

283. In the Lewis structure of O₂, the two oxygen atoms are joined by...
    A. a single bond
    B. a double bond
    C. a triple bond
    D. an ionic bond
    **Answer:** B. a double bond
    **Explanation:** To satisfy the octet rule for both oxygen atoms, they must share two pairs of electrons, forming a double covalent bond (O=O).

284. Which pair of elements is most likely to form an ionic compound?
    A. Carbon and Oxygen
    B. Hydrogen and Chlorine
    C. Potassium and Bromine
    D. Silicon and Hydrogen
    **Answer:** C. Potassium and Bromine
    **Explanation:** Ionic compounds are typically formed between a metal (which readily loses electrons) and a non-metal (which readily gains electrons). Potassium (K) is a Group 1 alkali metal, and Bromine (Br) is a Group 17 halogen, making them ideal candidates for forming an ionic bond.

285. In general, a chemical bond forms when the process...
    A. releases energy, leading to a more stable system.
    B. absorbs energy, leading to a more stable system.
    C. releases energy, leading to a less stable system.
    D. absorbs energy, leading to a less stable system.
    **Answer:** A. releases energy, leading to a more stable system.
    **Explanation:** Bond formation is an exothermic process because the resulting bonded arrangement is at a lower potential energy (is more stable) than the separate atoms. The excess energy is released.

286. What is the electronegativity difference range for a typical polar covalent bond?
    A. 0.0 to 0.4
    B. 0.4 to 1.7
    C. 1.7 to 2.0
    D. Greater than 2.0
    **Answer:** B. 0.4 to 1.7
    **Explanation:** While a continuum, a difference of 0 to ~0.4 is generally considered nonpolar. A difference of ~0.4 to ~1.7 is considered polar covalent. A difference greater than ~1.7 is generally considered ionic.

---

**Chapter 4: Molecular Geometry and VSEPR Theory**

287. The electron domain geometry of a molecule with 3 bonding pairs and 1 lone pair on the central atom is...
    A. Trigonal planar
    B. Trigonal pyramidal
    C. Tetrahedral
    D. T-shaped
    **Answer:** C. Tetrahedral
    **Explanation:** Electron domain geometry is determined by the total number of electron domains (bonding pairs + lone pairs). 3 + 1 = 4 domains. The arrangement that minimizes repulsion for 4 domains is tetrahedral. (The molecular geometry is trigonal pyramidal).

288. A molecule with the shape of a see-saw has...
    A. 5 bonding pairs, 0 lone pairs
    B. 4 bonding pairs, 1 lone pair
    C. 3 bonding pairs, 2 lone pairs
    D. 2 bonding pairs, 3 lone pairs
    **Answer:** B. 4 bonding pairs, 1 lone pair
    **Explanation:** A central atom with 5 electron domains has a trigonal bipyramidal electron geometry. If one of these is a lone pair (which occupies an equatorial position), the resulting molecular shape is a see-saw (e.g., SF₄).

289. Which of the following can form hydrogen bonds?
    A. H₂S
    B. PH₃
    C. HF
    D. CH₄
    **Answer:** C. HF
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen bonding occurs when hydrogen is covalently bonded to a highly electronegative atom: Nitrogen (N), Oxygen (O), or Fluorine (F). HF meets this condition.

290. What is the main reason for the abnormally high boiling point of water?
    A. Its low molar mass
    B. Its covalent bonds
    C. The V-shape of the molecule
    D. Extensive hydrogen bonding between molecules
    **Answer:** D. Extensive hydrogen bonding between molecules
    **Explanation:** The strong hydrogen bonds in water require a large amount of thermal energy to overcome, resulting in a much higher boiling point than other molecules of similar size (like H₂S or CH₄) which lack hydrogen bonding.

291. A molecule of XeF₄ is...
    A. Tetrahedral and nonpolar
    B. Square planar and nonpolar
    C. See-saw and polar
    D. Octahedral and polar
    **Answer:** B. Square planar and nonpolar
    **Explanation:** The central Xenon atom has 4 bonding pairs and 2 lone pairs, for a total of 6 electron domains (octahedral electron geometry). The two lone pairs position themselves on opposite sides (axial positions) to minimize repulsion, leaving the four fluorine atoms in a square planar arrangement. This symmetrical shape causes the bond dipoles to cancel, making the molecule nonpolar.

292. The hybridization of the central atom in BeCl₂ is...
    A. sp³
    B. sp²
    C. sp
    D. No hybridization
    **Answer:** C. sp
    **Explanation:** BeCl₂ has two electron domains and is a linear molecule. This corresponds to sp hybridization of the central Beryllium atom.

293. Which intermolecular force is most significant in liquid bromine (Br₂)?
    A. Hydrogen bonding
    B. Dipole-dipole forces
    C. Ionic bonding
    D. London dispersion forces
    **Answer:** D. London dispersion forces
    **Explanation:** Br₂ is a nonpolar molecule. The only intermolecular forces present between nonpolar molecules are London dispersion forces.

294. A sigma (σ) bond is generally ______ a pi (π) bond.
    A. Weaker than
    B. Stronger than
    C. Equal in strength to
    D. Of the same type as
    **Answer:** B. Stronger than
    **Explanation:** A sigma bond involves direct, head-on overlap of orbitals, which is a more effective and stronger overlap than the sideways overlap that forms a pi bond.

295. Viscosity of a liquid generally...
    A. Increases with increasing temperature
    B. Decreases with increasing temperature
    C. Is not affected by temperature
    D. Is highest at the boiling point
    **Answer:** B. Decreases with increasing temperature
    **Explanation:** Viscosity is resistance to flow, caused by intermolecular forces. Increasing the temperature gives the molecules more kinetic energy to overcome these forces, so the liquid flows more easily (viscosity decreases).

296. A molecule containing a central atom with 5 electron domains has a ______ electron domain geometry.
    A. Tetrahedral
    B. Octahedral
    C. Trigonal planar
    D. Trigonal bipyramidal
    **Answer:** D. Trigonal bipyramidal
    **Explanation:** This is the standard VSEPR geometry for 5 electron domains, with angles of 90° and 120°.

297. How many sigma (σ) and pi (π) bonds are in a molecule of ethyne (C₂H₂)?
    A. 2 σ, 2 π
    B. 3 σ, 1 π
    C. 3 σ, 2 π
    D. 2 σ, 3 π
    **Answer:** C. 3 σ, 2 π
    **Explanation:** The structure is H-C≡C-H. There are single bonds between H-C and H-C (2 σ bonds). The triple bond between the carbons consists of one σ bond and two π bonds. Total = 3 σ bonds and 2 π bonds.

298. Dipole-dipole forces are attractions between...
    A. Ions
    B. Nonpolar molecules
    C. Polar molecules
    D. Atoms in a covalent bond
    **Answer:** C. Polar molecules
    **Explanation:** These forces are the electrostatic attractions between the permanent partial positive (δ⁺) end of one polar molecule and the permanent partial negative (δ⁻) end of another.

---

**Chapter 5 & 6: Stoichiometry & Reactions**

299. In the reaction: 2Al(s) + 6HCl(aq) → 2AlCl₃(aq) + 3H₂(g), what mass of aluminum is required to produce 3.0 moles of hydrogen gas? (Al=27)
    A. 13.5 g
    B. 27.0 g
    C. 54.0 g
    D. 81.0 g
    **Answer:** C. 54.0 g
    **Explanation:** Mole ratio of Al to H₂ is 2:3.
    Moles of Al needed = (3.0 moles H₂) × (2 moles Al / 3 moles H₂) = 2.0 moles Al.
    Mass of Al = Moles × Molar Mass = 2.0 mol × 27 g/mol = 54.0 g.

300. The oxidation number of sulfur in H₂S is...
    A. +2
    B. -2
    C. +6
    D. 0
    **Answer:** B. -2
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen usually has an oxidation number of +1. In a neutral compound, the sum must be zero. (2 × +1) + S = 0.  2 + S = 0. So, S = -2.

301. The type of reaction represented by A + BC → AC + B is...
    A. Synthesis
    B. Decomposition
    C. Single displacement
    D. Double displacement
    **Answer:** C. Single displacement
    **Explanation:** Element A displaces element B from the compound BC. This is a single replacement or displacement reaction.

302. What volume of 2.0 M HCl is needed to prepare 100 cm³ of 0.5 M HCl solution?
    A. 20 cm³
    B. 25 cm³
    C. 40 cm³
    D. 50 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 25 cm³
    **Explanation:** Use the dilution formula M₁V₁ = M₂V₂.
    (2.0 M) × V₁ = (0.5 M) × (100 cm³)
    V₁ = (0.5 × 100) / 2.0 = 50 / 2.0 = 25 cm³.

303. If 10 g of magnesium (Mg=24) reacts with 10 g of oxygen (O=16), what is the limiting reactant? (Reaction: 2Mg + O₂ → 2MgO)
    A. Mg
    B. O₂
    C. MgO
    D. There is no limiting reactant
    **Answer:** A. Mg
    **Explanation:** Moles of Mg = 10 g / 24 g/mol ≈ 0.417 mol.
    Moles of O₂ = 10 g / 32 g/mol ≈ 0.313 mol.
    To react with 0.417 mol of Mg, you need (0.417 / 2) = 0.2085 mol of O₂. You have 0.313 mol of O₂, so O₂ is in excess. Mg is the limiting reactant.

304. Molarity, molality, and mole fraction are all measures of...
    A. Volume
    B. Mass
    C. Temperature
    D. Concentration
    **Answer:** D. Concentration
    **Explanation:** These are all different ways to express the amount of solute present in a given amount of solvent or solution.

305. The balanced half-reaction for the reduction of MnO₄⁻ to Mn²⁺ in acidic solution is...
    A. MnO₄⁻ + 8H⁺ → Mn²⁺ + 4H₂O + 5e⁻
    B. MnO₄⁻ + 8H⁺ + 5e⁻ → Mn²⁺ + 4H₂O
    C. MnO₄⁻ → Mn²⁺ + 4O²⁻ + 5e⁻
    D. MnO₄⁻ + 4H₂O + 5e⁻ → Mn²⁺ + 8OH⁻
    **Answer:** B. MnO₄⁻ + 8H⁺ + 5e⁻ → Mn²⁺ + 4H₂O
    **Explanation:** Mn is balanced. Balance O by adding 4H₂O to the right. Balance H by adding 8H⁺ to the left. Balance charge: Left is (-1 + 8) = +7, Right is +2. Add 5e⁻ to the left side to balance the charge (+7 - 5 = +2).

306. A reactant that is not completely used up in a reaction is called the...
    A. Limiting reactant
    B. Spectator ion
    C. Excess reactant
    D. Catalyst
    **Answer:** C. Excess reactant
    **Explanation:** The limiting reactant is completely consumed. Any other reactant that is left over is said to be in excess.

307. When copper is added to a solution of silver nitrate, the reaction is Zn(s) + 2AgNO₃(aq) → Zn(NO₃)₂(aq) + 2Ag(s). What type of reaction is this?  (Wait, there's a typo in the question. Let's assume it should be Zn)
    Okay, assuming the intended question is `Zn(s) + 2AgNO₃(aq) → Zn(NO₃)₂(aq) + 2Ag(s)`.
    A. Combustion
    B. Single Displacement
    C. Neutralization
    D. Decomposition
    **Answer:** B. Single Displacement
    **Explanation:** The more reactive metal, Zinc (Zn), displaces the less reactive metal, Silver (Ag), from its salt solution. This is a single displacement reaction.

308. In the reaction CaCO₃(s) + 2HCl(aq) → CaCl₂(aq) + H₂O(l) + CO₂(g), which species is a spectator ion?
    A. Ca²⁺
    B. CO₃²⁻
    C. H⁺
    D. Cl⁻
    **Answer:** D. Cl⁻
    **Explanation:** The full ionic equation is CaCO₃(s) + 2H⁺(aq) + 2Cl⁻(aq) → Ca²⁺(aq) + 2Cl⁻(aq) + H₂O(l) + CO₂(g). The chloride ion (Cl⁻) appears unchanged on both sides of the equation, making it a spectator ion.

309. Which concentration unit is temperature-independent?
    A. Molarity
    B. Molality
    C. Mass/Volume percent
    D. Volume/Volume percent
    **Answer:** B. Molality
    **Explanation:** Molality is defined as moles of solute per *mass* (kg) of solvent. Mass does not change with temperature, whereas volume does, making molarity and other volume-based concentrations temperature-dependent.

310. What is the mole ratio of H₂O to O₂ in the balanced equation for the combustion of propane: C₃H₈ + 5O₂ → 3CO₂ + 4H₂O?
    A. 4:3
    B. 3:4
    C. 4:5
    D. 5:4
    **Answer:** C. 4:5
    **Explanation:** The coefficients in the balanced equation give the mole ratios. The coefficient for H₂O is 4 and for O₂ is 5, so the ratio is 4:5.

---

**Chapter 7: Gases and Gas Laws**

311. If the temperature of a gas in a rigid container is doubled from 100 K to 200 K, the pressure will...
    A. Be halved
    B. Be doubled
    C. Remain the same
    D. Be quadrupled
    **Answer:** B. Be doubled
    **Explanation:** For a gas in a rigid container (constant volume), pressure is directly proportional to absolute temperature (Gay-Lussac's Law, P ∝ T). Doubling the Kelvin temperature will double the pressure.

312. A sample of gas occupies 10 L at 1 atm. What pressure is needed to compress it to 2.5 L at constant temperature?
    A. 0.25 atm
    B. 2.0 atm
    C. 4.0 atm
    D. 25.0 atm
    **Answer:** C. 4.0 atm
    **Explanation:** Use Boyle's Law: P₁V₁ = P₂V₂.
    (1 atm)(10 L) = P₂(2.5 L)
    P₂ = (1 × 10) / 2.5 = 10 / 2.5 = 4.0 atm.

313. The relationship between average kinetic energy of gas particles and temperature is...
    A. Inverse
    B. Logarithmic
    C. Direct and linear
    D. Exponential
    **Answer:** C. Direct and linear
    **Explanation:** One of the main postulates of the Kinetic Molecular Theory is that the average kinetic energy of gas particles is directly proportional to the absolute temperature in Kelvin.

314. The term `(V - nb)` in the van der Waals equation corrects for...
    A. Gas particle attractions
    B. The non-spherical shape of particles
    C. The volume occupied by the gas particles themselves
    D. The inelastic nature of collisions
    **Answer:** C. The volume occupied by the gas particles themselves
    **Explanation:** It corrects the total container volume (V) by subtracting the volume excluded by the particles, providing the "free volume" for motion.

315. Which of the following gases will diffuse most rapidly at the same temperature? (H=1, C=12, O=16, S=32)
    A. CH₄
    B. O₂
    C. H₂
    D. SO₂
    **Answer:** C. H₂
    **Explanation:** According to Graham's Law of Diffusion, the rate of diffusion is inversely proportional to the square root of the molar mass. The gas with the lowest molar mass will diffuse the fastest. H₂ (2 g/mol) is much lighter than CH₄ (16), O₂ (32), or SO₂ (64).

316. In a mixture of gases, the total pressure is the sum of the individual pressures of each gas. This is a statement of...
    A. Boyle's Law
    B. Avogadro's Law
    C. Henry's Law
    D. Dalton's Law
    **Answer:** D. Dalton's Law
    **Explanation:** This is Dalton's Law of Partial Pressures.

317. What is the volume of 2 moles of N₂ gas at STP?
    A. 11.2 L
    B. 22.4 L
    C. 44.8 L
    D. 56.0 L
    **Answer:** C. 44.8 L
    **Explanation:** At STP, 1 mole of any ideal gas occupies 22.4 L. Therefore, 2 moles will occupy 2 × 22.4 L = 44.8 L.

318. A gas is most likely to behave ideally under which conditions?
    A. High P, high T
    B. Low P, high T
    C. High P, low T
    D. Low P, low T
    **Answer:** B. Low P, high T
    **Explanation:** Under these conditions, the particles are far apart (low pressure) and moving very fast (high temperature), which minimizes the effects of intermolecular forces and particle volume, making the ideal gas assumptions most valid.

319. The "universal gas constant", R, has a value of 0.08206 when the units for pressure and volume are...
    A. Pascals and m³
    B. Atmospheres and L
    C. torr and mL
    D. psi and L
    **Answer:** B. Atmospheres and L
    **Explanation:** The numerical value of R depends on the units used. 0.08206 L·atm/(mol·K) is the value for pressure in atmospheres and volume in liters.

320. Which instrument is used to measure atmospheric pressure?
    A. Manometer
    B. Barometer
    C. Thermometer
    D. Calorimeter
    **Answer:** B. Barometer
    **Explanation:** A barometer, invented by Torricelli, is used to measure the pressure exerted by the atmosphere. A manometer is used to measure the pressure of an enclosed gas sample.

321. The escape of gas molecules through a tiny hole in a container is known as...
    A. Diffusion
    B. Sublimation
    C. Effusion
    D. Condensation
    **Answer:** C. Effusion
    **Explanation:** This is the specific definition of effusion, which is related to diffusion but refers to movement through a small opening rather than mixing in open space.

322. At constant volume and number of moles, what happens to the pressure of a gas if its Celsius temperature changes from 0°C to 100°C?
    A. It increases by a factor of 100
    B. It increases by a factor of 1.37
    C. It stays the same
    D. It doubles
    **Answer:** B. It increases by a factor of 1.37
    **Explanation:** Use Gay-Lussac's Law (P₁/T₁ = P₂/T₂) with temperatures in Kelvin. T₁ = 0 + 273 = 273 K. T₂ = 100 + 273 = 373 K.
    P₂ = P₁ (T₂/T₁) = P₁ (373/273) ≈ P₁ (1.366). The pressure increases by a factor of about 1.37.

---

**Chapter 8: Liquids and Solids**

323. Which of the following is a molecular solid?
    A. NaCl
    B. SiO₂ (Quartz)
    C. Fe
    D. I₂ (Iodine)
    **Answer:** D. I₂ (Iodine)
    **Explanation:** Solid iodine consists of discrete I₂ molecules held together in a crystal lattice by weak London dispersion forces. This is characteristic of a molecular solid. NaCl is ionic, SiO₂ is covalent network, Fe is metallic.

324. A liquid's resistance to flow is called...
    A. Surface tension
    B. Vapor pressure
    C. Viscosity
    D. Volatility
    **Answer:** C. Viscosity
    **Explanation:** Viscosity is a measure of a fluid's internal friction, which determines how easily it flows.

325. At pressures below the triple point, a substance will undergo which phase transition when heated?
    A. Melting
    B. Boiling
    C. Condensation
    D. Sublimation
    **Answer:** D. Sublimation
    **Explanation:** The triple point is the minimum pressure at which a liquid phase can exist. Below this pressure, the solid will transition directly to the gas phase upon heating.

326. The unique properties of water, such as its high boiling point and ability to act as a universal solvent for polar substances, are mainly due to...
    A. its low molecular mass.
    B. covalent bonding.
    C. its linear shape.
    D. hydrogen bonding.
    **Answer:** D. hydrogen bonding.
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen bonding is a strong intermolecular force that significantly influences nearly all of water's distinctive physical properties.

327. The number of nearest neighbors to an atom in a crystal lattice is its...
    A. Unit cell number
    B. Bravais number
    C. Coordination number
    D. Packing number
    **Answer:** C. Coordination number
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of the coordination number in crystallography.

328. A substance that has a definite melting point is most likely...
    A. an amorphous solid.
    B. a liquid.
    C. a crystalline solid.
    D. a gas.
    **Answer:** C. a crystalline solid.
    **Explanation:** Crystalline solids have a regular, ordered internal structure. This means all the bonds have similar strength, and they break at the same specific temperature, resulting in a sharp, well-defined melting point. Amorphous solids soften over a range of temperatures.

329. How many atoms are there per unit cell in a body-centered cubic (BCC) structure?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** B. 2
    **Explanation:** A BCC unit cell has 1/8 of an atom at each of the 8 corners (8 × 1/8 = 1 atom) and one full atom at the center of the body. Total = 1 + 1 = 2 atoms.

330. Glass is considered an amorphous solid because...
    A. it is transparent.
    B. it is made from sand.
    C. its atoms lack long-range order.
    D. it is a good electrical insulator.
    **Answer:** C. its atoms lack long-range order.
    **Explanation:** The key distinction of an amorphous solid is its disordered, non-repeating internal structure, which is more like that of a frozen liquid than a crystal.

331. The temperature above which a substance cannot be liquefied, no matter the pressure, is its...
    A. Boiling point
    B. Triple point
    C. Critical temperature
    D. Eutectic temperature
    **Answer:** C. Critical temperature
    **Explanation:** At the critical temperature, the kinetic energy of the particles is so high that no amount of pressure can force them into a distinct liquid phase.

332. The process of deposition is the phase change from...
    A. Solid to gas
    B. Gas to solid
    C. Liquid to gas
    D. Gas to liquid
    **Answer:** B. Gas to solid
    **Explanation:** Deposition is the reverse of sublimation. An example is the formation of frost from water vapor in the air.

333. A liquid in an open beaker will boil at a lower temperature at the top of a high mountain than at sea level because...
    A. the air is colder.
    B. there is less oxygen.
    C. the atmospheric pressure is lower.
    D. the gravitational pull is weaker.
    **Answer:** C. the atmospheric pressure is lower.
    **Explanation:** Boiling occurs when a liquid's vapor pressure equals the external pressure. At high altitudes, the atmospheric pressure is lower, so the liquid's vapor pressure needs to reach a lower value to boil, which occurs at a lower temperature.

334. In the phase diagram of water, the solid-liquid equilibrium line has a negative slope. This indicates that...
    A. Ice is denser than liquid water.
    B. Liquid water is denser than ice.
    C. The boiling point of water is 100°C.
    D. Water cannot be sublimated.
    **Answer:** B. Liquid water is denser than ice.
    **Explanation:** A negative slope on the S-L line means that as pressure increases, the melting point decreases. This happens for substances where the liquid phase is more dense than the solid phase (like water), as applying pressure favors the formation of the denser phase.

---

**Chapter 9 & 10: Solutions & Acids/Bases**

335. A supersaturated solution...
    A. is very stable.
    B. contains more solute than normally possible and is unstable.
    C. can dissolve more solute.
    D. is always very concentrated.
    **Answer:** B. contains more solute than normally possible and is unstable.
    **Explanation:** A supersaturated solution holds more solute than its equilibrium saturation point, making it a metastable (unstable) state. Adding a seed crystal will cause the excess solute to crystallize out.

336. Which of the following ionic compounds is expected to be insoluble in water?
    A. NaNO₃
    B. K₂SO₄
    C. (NH₄)₂CO₃
    D. BaSO₄
    **Answer:** D. BaSO₄
    **Explanation:** According to solubility rules, most sulfates are soluble, but barium sulfate (BaSO₄) is a major exception and is highly insoluble.

337. The hydrolysis of the salt sodium ethanoate (CH₃COONa) in water will produce a solution that is...
    A. Acidic
    B. Basic
    C. Neutral
    D. A buffer
    **Answer:** B. Basic
    **Explanation:** Sodium ethanoate is the salt of a strong base (NaOH) and a weak acid (CH₃COOH). The ethanoate ion (CH₃COO⁻) is the conjugate base of a weak acid and will react with water (hydrolyze) to produce OH⁻ ions: CH₃COO⁻ + H₂O ⇌ CH₃COOH + OH⁻. This makes the solution basic.

338. A 0.001 M solution of HCl has a pH of...
    A. 1
    B. 3
    C. 11
    D. 13
    **Answer:** B. 3
    **Explanation:** HCl is a strong acid, so it dissociates completely. [H⁺] = 0.001 M = 1 x 10⁻³ M.
    pH = -log[H⁺] = -log(1 x 10⁻³) = 3.

339. The relationship K_a × K_b = K_w applies to...
    A. Any acid and any base
    B. A conjugate acid-base pair
    C. A strong acid and its conjugate base
    D. A buffer solution
    **Answer:** B. A conjugate acid-base pair
    **Explanation:** For any weak acid and its corresponding conjugate base (or a weak base and its conjugate acid), the product of their dissociation constants is equal to the ion-product constant for water, K_w.

340. What is the conjugate acid of the bicarbonate ion, HCO₃⁻?
    A. H₂O
    B. CO₃²⁻
    C. CO₂
    D. H₂CO₃
    **Answer:** D. H₂CO₃
    **Explanation:** A conjugate acid is formed when a base accepts a proton (H⁺). When the base HCO₃⁻ accepts a proton, it forms carbonic acid, H₂CO₃.

341. An Arrhenius acid is defined as a...
    A. proton donor.
    B. proton acceptor.
    C. substance that increases H⁺ concentration in water.
    D. substance that increases OH⁻ concentration in water.
    **Answer:** C. substance that increases H⁺ concentration in water.
    **Explanation:** This is the core of the Arrhenius definition, which is specific to behavior in aqueous solutions.

342. A solution contains 10 g of NaOH in 500 cm³ of solution. What is its molarity? (Na=23, O=16, H=1)
    A. 0.25 M
    B. 0.50 M
    C. 1.0 M
    D. 2.0 M
    **Answer:** B. 0.50 M
    **Explanation:** Molar mass of NaOH = 23+16+1 = 40 g/mol.
    Moles of NaOH = 10 g / 40 g/mol = 0.25 mol.
    Volume = 500 cm³ = 0.5 dm³ (or 0.5 L).
    Molarity = Moles / Volume = 0.25 mol / 0.5 L = 0.50 M.

343. In a titration, 25.0 cm³ of 0.1 M HCl is neutralized by 20.0 cm³ of NaOH solution. What is the concentration of the NaOH solution?
    A. 0.08 M
    B. 0.10 M
    C. 0.125 M
    D. 0.20 M
    **Answer:** C. 0.125 M
    **Explanation:** Reaction is HCl + NaOH → NaCl + H₂O (mole ratio 1:1).
    Use M_aV_a = M_bV_b.
    (0.1 M)(25.0 cm³) = M_b (20.0 cm³)
    M_b = (0.1 × 25.0) / 20.0 = 2.5 / 20.0 = 0.125 M.

344. The pH of a buffer solution can be calculated using the...
    A. Ideal gas law
    B. Nernst equation
    C. Arrhenius equation
    D. Henderson-Hasselbalch equation
    **Answer:** D. Henderson-Hasselbalch equation
    **Explanation:** The Henderson-Hasselbalch equation (pH = pKa + log([base]/[acid])) specifically relates the pH of a buffer to the pKa of the weak acid and the ratio of the concentrations of the conjugate base to the weak acid.

345. Which of the following is NOT a strong base?
    A. NaOH
    B. KOH
    C. NH₃
    D. Ca(OH)₂
    **Answer:** C. NH₃
    **Explanation:** Ammonia (NH₃) is a classic example of a weak base. It reacts with water only partially to produce hydroxide ions. The others are hydroxides of alkali or alkaline earth metals and are considered strong bases.

346. An amphoteric substance...
    A. can act as either an acid or a base.
    B. can conduct electricity.
    C. resists changes in pH.
    D. contains both ionic and covalent bonds.
    **Answer:** A. can act as either an acid or a base.
    **Explanation:** An amphoteric (or amphiprotic) substance, like water, can donate a proton (act as an acid) or accept a proton (act as a base) depending on the chemical environment.

---

**Chapter 11 & 12: Thermodynamics & Kinetics**

347. In a spontaneous endothermic process...
    A. ΔH is positive, and ΔS must be positive and large.
    B. ΔH is negative, and ΔS must be positive.
    C. ΔH is positive, and ΔS must be negative.
    D. ΔH and ΔS must both be zero.
    **Answer:** A. ΔH is positive, and ΔS must be positive and large.
    **Explanation:** For a process to be spontaneous, ΔG must be negative. In ΔG = ΔH - TΔS, if the process is endothermic (ΔH is positive), the only way for ΔG to be negative is if the TΔS term is positive and larger in magnitude than ΔH. This requires ΔS to be positive.

348. For the reaction A → B, the rate law is Rate = k[A]. If the concentration of A is tripled, the rate of reaction will...
    A. Triple
    B. Be one-third
    C. Increase by a factor of nine
    D. Stay the same
    **Answer:** A. Triple
    **Explanation:** This is a first-order reaction with respect to A. The rate is directly proportional to the concentration of A. Therefore, if [A] is tripled, the rate will also triple.

349. Which of the following conditions must be met for a collision to be effective in producing a chemical reaction?
    A. The molecules must be gases.
    B. The collision must involve a catalyst.
    C. The molecules must have sufficient kinetic energy.
    D. The molecules must be in an aqueous solution.
    **Answer:** C. The molecules must have sufficient kinetic energy.
    **Explanation:** According to collision theory, an effective collision requires two things: sufficient energy (to overcome the activation energy) and correct orientation. Of the options provided, sufficient energy is a necessary condition.

350. The units for a second-order rate constant are typically...
    A. s⁻¹
    B. M s⁻¹
    C. M⁻¹ s⁻¹
    D. M⁻² s⁻¹
    **Answer:** C. M⁻¹ s⁻¹
    **Explanation:** For a second-order reaction, Rate = k[A]². The units are:
    (M s⁻¹) = k (M)².
    Solving for k gives units of M⁻¹ s⁻¹.

351. The specific heat capacity of a substance is the amount of heat required to...
    A. Melt one gram of the substance.
    B. Raise the temperature of one gram of the substance by one degree Celsius.
    C. Raise the temperature of one mole of the substance by one degree Celsius.
    D. Boil one gram of the substance.
    **Answer:** B. Raise the temperature of one gram of the substance by one degree Celsius.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of specific heat capacity (c) in the equation q = mcΔT.

352. For a reaction with an activation energy of 50 kJ/mol, increasing the temperature from 300K to 310K will...
    A. Significantly increase the reaction rate.
    B. Significantly decrease the reaction rate.
    C. Have a very small effect on the reaction rate.
    D. Reverse the direction of the reaction.
    **Answer:** A. Significantly increase the reaction rate.
    **Explanation:** The Arrhenius equation shows an exponential relationship between the rate constant and temperature. A common rule of thumb is that a 10°C (or 10K) rise in temperature approximately doubles the rate of many chemical reactions.

353. A reaction intermediate differs from a transition state in that...
    A. An intermediate is at a higher energy than a transition state.
    B. A transition state is more stable than an intermediate.
    C. An intermediate is a real species that can potentially be isolated, while a transition state cannot.
    D. A transition state has fully formed bonds, while an intermediate has partial bonds.
    **Answer:** C. An intermediate is a real species that can potentially be isolated, while a transition state cannot.
    **Explanation:** An intermediate (like a carbocation) exists in a potential energy valley between two transition states and has a finite, albeit short, lifetime. A transition state is the peak of the energy barrier and is a fleeting arrangement of atoms that cannot be isolated.

354. In the free radical chlorination of methane, Cl• + CH₄ → HCl + •CH₃ is an example of a(n)...
    A. Initiation step
    B. Propagation step
    C. Termination step
    D. Rearrangement step
    **Answer:** B. Propagation step
    **Explanation:** In a propagation step, a radical reacts with a stable molecule to produce a new stable molecule and a new radical, allowing the chain reaction to continue.

355. Gibbs free energy (G) is defined as...
    A. G = H + TS
    B. G = U + PV
    C. G = q + w
    D. G = H - TS
    **Answer:** D. G = H - TS
    **Explanation:** This is the defining equation for Gibbs free energy, relating it to enthalpy (H), entropy (S), and absolute temperature (T).

356. What does a bomb calorimeter measure directly?
    A. The change in enthalpy (ΔH)
    B. The change in internal energy (ΔU)
    C. The change in entropy (ΔS)
    D. The change in Gibbs free energy (ΔG)
    **Answer:** B. The change in internal energy (ΔU)
    **Explanation:** A bomb calorimeter operates at a constant volume. The heat flow measured under constant volume conditions (q_v) is equal to the change in internal energy (ΔU).

357. Homogeneous catalysis involves...
    A. A catalyst in a different phase from the reactants.
    B. A catalyst in the same phase as the reactants.
    C. Only solid catalysts.
    D. Catalysts that are biological enzymes.
    **Answer:** B. A catalyst in the same phase as the reactants.
    **Explanation:** In homogeneous catalysis, both the catalyst and reactants exist in a single phase, for example, all as gases or all dissolved in a single liquid solution.

358. If a reaction has a rate law of Rate = k, the reaction is...
    A. First order
    B. Second order
    C. Zero order
    D. Not possible
    **Answer:** C. Zero order
    **Explanation:** A zero-order rate law means the rate is independent of the concentration of the reactants (Rate ∝ [A]⁰ = 1). The rate is simply equal to the rate constant, k.

---

**Chapter 13 & 14: Equilibrium & Electrochemistry**

359. A reversible reaction has a ΔH° = +40 kJ/mol. An increase in temperature will...
    A. Increase the value of K and shift the equilibrium to the right.
    B. Decrease the value of K and shift the equilibrium to the left.
    C. Increase the value of K and shift the equilibrium to the left.
    D. Have no effect on the value of K.
    **Answer:** A. Increase the value of K and shift the equilibrium to the right.
    **Explanation:** A positive ΔH° indicates an endothermic reaction. According to Le Chatelier's principle, increasing the temperature will favor the endothermic (forward) direction to absorb the added heat, thus shifting the equilibrium to the right.
