    C. Mass of the component to total mass of the solution
    D. Moles of the component to volume of the solution
    **Answer:** B. Moles of the component to total moles of all components
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of mole fraction (χ). The sum of the mole fractions of all components in a solution is always 1.

533. In the activity series of metals, a metal will displace any other metal...
    A. below it in the series from its salt solution.
    B. above it in the series from its salt solution.
    C. in the same period from its salt solution.
    D. with a higher atomic mass from its salt solution.
    **Answer:** A. below it in the series from its salt solution.
    **Explanation:** The activity series ranks metals by their reactivity (ease of oxidation). A more reactive metal (higher in the series) can displace a less reactive metal (lower in the series) from a solution of its ions.

534. How many oxygen atoms are in one formula unit of aluminum sulfate, Al₂(SO₄)₃?
    A. 3
    B. 4
    C. 7
    D. 12
    **Answer:** D. 12
    **Explanation:** There are 3 sulfate (SO₄) ions. Each sulfate ion contains 4 oxygen atoms. So, the total number of oxygen atoms is 3 × 4 = 12.

535. When solid ammonium chloride (NH₄Cl) is heated, it decomposes into ammonia (NH₃) and hydrogen chloride (HCl) gas. This is a...
    A. Neutralization reaction
    B. Redox reaction
    C. Reversible thermal decomposition
    D. Precipitation reaction
    **Answer:** C. Reversible thermal decomposition
    **Explanation:** This is a classic example of thermal decomposition that is reversible. Upon cooling, the ammonia and hydrogen chloride gases recombine to form solid ammonium chloride.

536. An element forms an oxide that dissolves in water to produce an acidic solution. The element is likely...
    A. Sodium
    B. Magnesium
    C. Aluminum
    D. Sulfur
    **Answer:** D. Sulfur
    **Explanation:** Oxides of non-metals (like sulfur dioxide, SO₂) typically dissolve in water to form acids (e.g., sulfurous acid, H₂SO₃). Oxides of active metals are basic, and some (like aluminum) are amphoteric.

---

**Chapter 7 & 8: States of Matter**

537. The process by which gas molecules spread throughout a space is...
    A. Sublimation
    B. Effusion
    C. Condensation
    D. Diffusion
    **Answer:** D. Diffusion
    **Explanation:** Diffusion describes the mixing of gases due to their random molecular motion.

538. Which of the following substances has the weakest intermolecular forces?
    A. H₂O (l)
    B. NaCl (s)
    C. He (g)
    D. Fe (s)
    **Answer:** C. He (g)
    **Explanation:** Helium is a monatomic noble gas at room temperature, indicating extremely weak intermolecular (interatomic, in this case) forces. The only forces present are very weak London dispersion forces. The other substances are liquid, ionic solid, and metallic solid, all with much stronger forces holding them together.

539. At standard temperature and pressure (STP), what is the mass of 5.6 L of CO₂ gas? (C=12, O=16)
    A. 5.6 g
    B. 11.0 g
    C. 22.0 g
    D. 44.0 g
    **Answer:** B. 11.0 g
    **Explanation:**
    1.  At STP, 1 mole of any gas occupies 22.4 L.
    2.  Moles of CO₂ = 5.6 L / 22.4 L/mol = 0.25 mol.
    3.  Molar mass of CO₂ = 12 + 2(16) = 44 g/mol.
    4.  Mass of CO₂ = Moles × Molar Mass = 0.25 mol × 44 g/mol = 11.0 g.

540. A liquid with a high vapor pressure is described as...
    A. Viscous
    B. Dense
    C. Volatile
    D. Stable
    **Answer:** C. Volatile
    **Explanation:** Volatility refers to the tendency of a liquid to evaporate easily. This occurs when a liquid has weak intermolecular forces, which also leads to a high vapor pressure.

541. The regular three-dimensional arrangement of particles in a solid is called a...
    A. Unit cell
    B. Crystal lattice
    C. Amorphous structure
    D. Molecular solid
    **Answer:** B. Crystal lattice
    **Explanation:** A crystal lattice is the ordered array of points representing the positions of particles (atoms, ions, or molecules) in a crystalline solid. A unit cell is the smallest repeating unit of this lattice.

542. Water can exist as a solid, liquid, and gas simultaneously...
    A. At its critical point
    B. At its triple point
    C. At 0°C and 1 atm
    D. Only in a vacuum
    **Answer:** B. At its triple point
    **Explanation:** The triple point is the specific temperature and pressure where all three phases of a substance are in thermodynamic equilibrium.

543. Metals are malleable because...
    A. The atoms are arranged in a rigid lattice.
    B. The bonds are very weak.
    C. Layers of positive ions can slide past each other within the delocalized electron sea.
    D. They have high densities.
    **Answer:** C. Layers of positive ions can slide past each other within the delocalized electron sea.
    **Explanation:** The non-directional nature of metallic bonding allows the layers of metal ions to be displaced without breaking the bonds entirely, as the "sea" of electrons can adjust to the new arrangement. This allows the metal to be hammered into sheets (malleability).

544. A gas exerts pressure because its particles...
    A. are heavy.
    B. have strong attractions for each other.
    C. are in constant motion and collide with container walls.
    D. have a very small volume.
    **Answer:** C. are in constant motion and collide with container walls.
    **Explanation:** According to the Kinetic Molecular Theory, the macroscopic pressure of a gas is the result of the cumulative force of countless collisions of its fast-moving particles with the surfaces of the container.

545. For an ideal gas at constant pressure, volume is directly proportional to...
    A. Moles
    B. The square of the temperature
    C. The absolute temperature (in Kelvin)
    D. The reciprocal of the temperature
    **Answer:** C. The absolute temperature (in Kelvin)
    **Explanation:** This is a statement of Charles's Law (V ∝ T), which holds for a fixed amount of gas at constant pressure.

546. Which solid type is held together by weak intermolecular forces?
    A. Ionic
    B. Molecular
    C. Metallic
    D. Covalent network
    **Answer:** B. Molecular
    **Explanation:** In molecular solids (e.g., ice, dry ice, wax), the lattice points are occupied by discrete molecules, and the forces holding these molecules together are the relatively weak IMFs (LDF, dipole-dipole, H-bonds).

547. Which of the following is NOT an assumption of the Kinetic Molecular Theory for ideal gases?
    A. Gas particles are in constant, random motion.
    B. There are significant attractive forces between gas particles.
    C. The volume of the gas particles is negligible compared to the container volume.
    D. Collisions are perfectly elastic.
    **Answer:** B. There are significant attractive forces between gas particles.
    **Explanation:** The ideal gas model specifically assumes that there are *no* attractive or repulsive forces between the particles. This is one of the main ways real gases deviate from ideal behavior.

548. The "normal boiling point" of a liquid is its boiling point at what pressure?
    A. 0 atm
    B. The triple point pressure
    C. 1 atm
    D. The critical pressure
    **Answer:** C. 1 atm
    **Explanation:** The normal boiling point is a standard reference point, defined as the boiling temperature when the external pressure is exactly 1 standard atmosphere (101.325 kPa or 760 torr).

---

**Chapter 9 & 10: Solutions & Acids/Bases**

549. Henry's Law relates the solubility of a gas to its...
    A. Temperature
    B. Molar mass
    C. Partial pressure
    D. Molecular shape
    **Answer:** C. Partial pressure
    **Explanation:** Henry's Law states that the amount of gas that dissolves in a liquid is directly proportional to the partial pressure of that gas above the liquid.

550. When a strong acid is titrated with a weak base, the pH at the equivalence point will be...
    A. Less than 7
    B. Equal to 7
    C. Greater than 7
    D. Dependent on the indicator used
    **Answer:** A. Less than 7
    **Explanation:** At the equivalence point, the salt formed is from a strong acid and a weak base (e.g., NH₄Cl). The cation (e.g., NH₄⁺) is the conjugate acid of a weak base and will hydrolyze water to produce H₃O⁺ ions, making the solution acidic.

551. An aqueous solution of which of the following salts will be neutral?
    A. NH₄Cl
    B. KCN
    C. NaNO₃
    D. CH₃COONa
    **Answer:** C. NaNO₃
    **Explanation:** Sodium nitrate (NaNO₃) is the salt of a strong acid (HNO₃) and a strong base (NaOH). Neither the Na⁺ nor the NO₃⁻ ion will hydrolyze water, so the solution remains neutral (pH=7).

552. A solution that resists changes in pH upon addition of small amounts of acid or base is called a...
    A. Neutral solution
    B. Saturated solution
    C. Colligative solution
    D. Buffer solution
    **Answer:** D. Buffer solution
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of a buffer, which typically consists of a weak acid/base and its conjugate partner.

553. According to the Brønsted-Lowry theory, an acid is a...
    A. Proton donor
    B. Proton acceptor
    C. Electron pair donor
    D. Electron pair acceptor
    **Answer:** A. Proton donor
    **Explanation:** The Brønsted-Lowry definition focuses on the transfer of a proton (H⁺). An acid is the species that donates the proton.

554. If the pOH of a solution is 10, the solution is...
    A. Acidic
    B. Basic
    C. Neutral
    D. A buffer
    **Answer:** A. Acidic
    **Explanation:** If pOH = 10, then pH = 14 - pOH = 14 - 10 = 4. A pH of 4 is acidic.

555. Reverse osmosis is used in...
    A. Preparing buffer solutions
    B. The carbonation of soft drinks
    C. The desalination of seawater
    D. The titration of acids and bases
    **Answer:** C. The desalination of seawater
    **Explanation:** In reverse osmosis, a pressure greater than the osmotic pressure is applied to a salt solution (seawater), forcing pure water molecules across a semipermeable membrane, leaving the dissolved salts behind.

556. A solution has [H⁺] = 2.5 x 10⁻⁵ M. What is its pH?
    A. 4.6
    B. 5.0
    C. 2.5
    D. 9.4
    **Answer:** A. 4.6
    **Explanation:** pH = -log[H⁺] = -log(2.5 x 10⁻⁵) = -(log(2.5) + log(10⁻⁵)) = -(0.4 - 5) = -(-4.6) = 4.6.

557. Which pair could form a buffer solution?
    A. HCl and NaOH
    B. H₂SO₄ and HSO₄⁻
    C. HNO₃ and KNO₃
    D. HF and NaF
    **Answer:** D. HF and NaF
    **Explanation:** A buffer requires a weak acid and its conjugate base. HF is a weak acid, and NaF provides its conjugate base, F⁻. HCl and HNO₃ are strong acids, and their conjugate bases have negligible basicity.

558. In the reaction Al(OH)₃ + OH⁻ → [Al(OH)₄]⁻, Al(OH)₃ is acting as a...
    A. Brønsted-Lowry base
    B. Lewis acid
    C. Brønsted-Lowry acid
    D. Arrhenius base
    **Answer:** B. Lewis acid
    **Explanation:** Al(OH)₃ is accepting an electron pair from the OH⁻ ion to form a new bond. A species that accepts an electron pair is a Lewis acid. It can also be considered a Brønsted-Lowry acid here, by donating the whole Al(OH)3 to the OH-. A more direct action is the Lewis definition. Let's see if we can find a better Brønsted example. Al(OH)₃ + 3H⁺ -> Al³⁺ + 3H₂O. Here it is a Brønsted base. Since it reacts with both acid and base, it is amphoteric. In its reaction with OH⁻, it is acting as an acid. A Lewis acid is the most general and correct term for this behavior.

559. Antifreeze works by...
    A. increasing the vapor pressure of the coolant.
    B. increasing the specific heat capacity of water.
    C. elevating the boiling point and depressing the freezing point of the coolant.
    D. catalyzing the flow of coolant.
    **Answer:** C. elevating the boiling point and depressing the freezing point of the coolant.
    **Explanation:** Antifreeze (like ethylene glycol) acts as a solute that utilizes colligative properties. It lowers the freezing point, preventing the coolant from freezing in winter, and raises the boiling point, preventing it from boiling over in summer.

560. What happens to the pH of water when a non-metal oxide like SO₃ is dissolved in it?
    A. It increases.
    B. It decreases.
    C. It remains 7.
    D. It becomes 14.
    **Answer:** B. It decreases.
    **Explanation:** Non-metal oxides are acidic anhydrides. They react with water to form acids. SO₃(g) + H₂O(l) → H₂SO₄(aq). The formation of sulfuric acid increases the [H⁺] concentration and thus lowers the pH.

---

**Chapter 11 & 12: Thermodynamics & Kinetics**

561. The study of reaction rates is called...
    A. Thermodynamics
    B. Stoichiometry
    C. Kinetics
    D. Electrochemistry
    **Answer:** C. Kinetics
    **Explanation:** Chemical kinetics is the branch of chemistry that deals with the speed or rate at which chemical reactions occur and the mechanisms by which they proceed.

562. If the rate law for a reaction is Rate = k[X]², what happens to the rate if the concentration of X is halved?
    A. It is halved.
    B. It is doubled.
    C. It decreases by a factor of 4.
    D. It increases by a factor of 4.
    **Answer:** C. It decreases by a factor of 4.
    **Explanation:** The reaction is second order with respect to X. If [X] becomes [X]/2, the rate will change by a factor of (1/2)² = 1/4. It decreases by a factor of 4.

563. The step in a reaction mechanism with the highest activation energy is the...
    A. overall reaction.
    B. transition state.
    C. fastest step.
    D. rate-determining step.
    **Answer:** D. rate-determining step.
    **Explanation:** The slowest step in a mechanism is the one with the highest energy barrier (activation energy). This step acts as the bottleneck and determines the overall rate of the reaction.

564. For a process to be spontaneous, ΔG must be...
    A. Positive
    B. Negative
    C. Zero
    D. Equal to ΔH
    **Answer:** B. Negative
    **Explanation:** A negative value for the change in Gibbs free energy (ΔG) indicates that a process is thermodynamically favorable and will proceed spontaneously under the given conditions.

565. According to the Arrhenius equation, the natural log of the rate constant (ln k) is inversely proportional to...
    A. Temperature (T)
    B. Activation energy (Ea)
    C. The frequency factor (A)
    D. Absolute Temperature (T)
    **Answer:** D. Absolute Temperature (T)
    **Explanation:** The linear form of the Arrhenius equation is ln(k) = ln(A) - (Ea/RT). Plotting ln(k) vs 1/T gives a straight line. This means ln(k) is proportional to -1/T, or inversely proportional to T.

566. An increase in temperature increases the rate of reaction primarily because it...
    A. increases the concentration of reactants.
    B. increases the frequency and energy of collisions.
    C. lowers the activation energy.
    D. makes the reaction more exothermic.
    **Answer:** B. increases the frequency and energy of collisions.
    **Explanation:** Higher temperature means reactant particles move faster. This leads to more frequent collisions, and more importantly, a larger fraction of these collisions have energy greater than the activation energy, making them effective. A catalyst lowers Ea.

5-67. The heat change that occurs when one mole of a compound is formed from its elements in their standard states is the...
    A. Standard enthalpy of combustion.
    B. Standard enthalpy of formation.
    C. Lattice energy.
    D. Activation energy.
    **Answer:** B. Standard enthalpy of formation.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of the standard enthalpy of formation, ΔH°_f.

568. If a reaction has a ΔH = +100 kJ and a ΔS = +250 J/K, at what temperature will it become spontaneous? (Assume ΔH and ΔS are constant with T)
    A. At temperatures above 400 K
    B. At temperatures below 400 K
    C. At all temperatures
    D. At no temperature
    **Answer:** A. At temperatures above 400 K
    **Explanation:** The reaction becomes spontaneous when ΔG < 0. We find the equilibrium temperature where ΔG = 0.
    ΔG = ΔH - TΔS = 0 => T = ΔH / ΔS.
    First, convert units to be consistent: ΔS = 250 J/K = 0.250 kJ/K.
    T = (100 kJ) / (0.250 kJ/K) = 400 K.
    Since both ΔH and ΔS are positive, the TΔS term will overcome the ΔH term at temperatures *above* 400 K, making ΔG negative.

569. An enzyme is a highly specific biological...
    A. Reactant
    B. Substrate
    C. Catalyst
    D. Inhibitor
    **Answer:** C. Catalyst
    **Explanation:** Enzymes are proteins that catalyze specific biochemical reactions in living systems with remarkable efficiency and specificity.

570. In the equation ΔU = q + w, 'w' represents...
    A. work done BY the system.
    B. work done ON the system.
    C. the heat capacity.
    D. the wavelength of light.
    **Answer:** B. work done ON the system.
    **Explanation:** This is the standard chemistry convention for the first law of thermodynamics. A positive 'w' means the surroundings do work on the system (e.g., compression), increasing its internal energy.

571. Which statement about the transition state of a reaction is true?
    A. It is a stable intermediate.
    B. It corresponds to a minimum on the energy profile.
    C. It is the highest energy point along the reaction coordinate.
    D. Its concentration can be measured directly.
    **Answer:** C. It is the highest energy point along the D.action coordinate.
    **Explanation:** The transition state (or activated complex) is the unstable, high-energy arrangement of atoms at the peak of the activation energy barrier.

572. Using a coffee-cup calorimeter, you are measuring...
    A. ΔU, the change in internal energy
    B. ΔH, the change in enthalpy
    C. ΔG, the change in free energy
    D. ΔS, the change in entropy
    **Answer:** B. ΔH, the change in enthalpy
    **Explanation:** A coffee-cup calorimeter operates at constant atmospheric pressure. The heat flow measured at constant pressure (q_p) is equal to the enthalpy change, ΔH.

---

**Chapter 13 & 14: Equilibrium & Electrochemistry**

573. In the reaction N₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2NO(g), removing some NO gas from the system at equilibrium will cause...
    A. A shift to the left.
    B. A shift to the right.
    C. An increase in the pressure.
    D. No change in the equilibrium position.
    **Answer:** B. A shift to the right.
    **Explanation:** According to Le Chatelier's principle, removing a product will cause the system to shift in the direction that replenishes that product. The equilibrium will shift to the right.

574. In a galvanic cell made of Zinc and Copper half-cells, the electrons flow...
    A. from the copper electrode to the zinc electrode.
    B. from the zinc electrode to the copper electrode.
    C. through the salt bridge.
    D. from the solution to the electrodes.
    **Answer:** B. from the zinc electrode to the copper electrode.
    **Explanation:** Zinc is more reactive than copper and has a more negative E° value. Therefore, Zinc is the anode (oxidation) and Copper is the cathode (reduction). Electrons are released at the anode (Zn) and flow through the external wire to the cathode (Cu).

575. Which statement is correct about the salt bridge?
    A. Electrons flow through it.
    B. It allows the two solutions to mix completely.
    C. It must be made of metal.
    D. Anions from the salt bridge flow towards the anode.
    **Answer:** D. Anions from the salt bridge flow towards the anode.
    **Explanation:** To maintain charge neutrality, negative ions (anions) must flow into the anode compartment to balance the positive charge created by the formation of cations during oxidation. Positive ions (cations) flow into the cathode compartment.

576. Which process requires electrolysis?
    A. The reaction in a car battery to start the engine.
    B. The charging of a mobile phone battery.
    C. The rusting of iron.
    D. The reaction of zinc with acid.
    **Answer:** B. The charging of a mobile phone battery.
    **Explanation:** Charging a rechargeable battery involves using an external power source to force the non-spontaneous reverse reaction to occur. This is an electrolytic process. All the other options are spontaneous galvanic processes.

577. The K_sp expression for magnesium fluoride, MgF₂, is...
    A. K_sp = [Mg²⁺][F⁻]
    B. K_sp = [Mg²⁺][F⁻]²
    C. K_sp = [Mg²⁺][2F⁻]²
    D. K_sp = [MgF₂] / ([Mg²⁺][F⁻]²)
    **Answer:** B. K_sp = [Mg²⁺][F⁻]²
    **Explanation:** The dissolution equilibrium is MgF₂(s) ⇌ Mg²⁺(aq) + 2F⁻(aq). The Ksp expression is the product of the ion concentrations raised to their stoichiometric coefficients. The solid is omitted.

578. The purpose of galvanizing iron is to...
    A. Coat it with a more reactive metal to act as a sacrificial anode.
    B. Coat it with a less reactive metal to block air and water.
    C. Make it stronger and harder.
    D. Change its color.
    **Answer:** A. Coat it with a more reactive metal to act as a sacrificial anode.
    **Explanation:** Galvanizing involves coating iron with zinc. Since zinc is more reactive than iron, if the coating is scratched, the zinc will corrode preferentially (sacrificial protection), protecting the iron underneath.

579. An increase in the temperature of the endothermic reaction 2H₂O(g) ⇌ 2H₂(g) + O₂(g) will result in...
    A. an increase in the concentration of H₂O(g).
    B. a decrease in the value of the equilibrium constant.
    C. a shift in the equilibrium position to the right.
    D. a decrease in the total pressure of the system.
    **Answer:** C. a shift in the equilibrium position to the right.
    **Explanation:** Since the forward reaction is endothermic, adding heat (increasing T) will favor the forward reaction to absorb the stress, causing the equilibrium to shift right.

580. The Standard Hydrogen Electrode (SHE) is assigned a potential of...
    A. +1.00 V
    B. -1.00 V
    C. 0.00 V
    D. 1.66 V
    **Answer:** C. 0.00 V
    **Explanation:** By international convention, the SHE is the reference standard against which all other standard electrode potentials are measured, and its potential is defined as exactly 0.00 V under standard conditions.

581. In the electrolysis of molten Al₂O₃, the product at the cathode is...
    A. O₂ gas
    B. Al metal
    C. CO₂ gas
    D. F₂ gas
    **Answer:** B. Al metal
    **Explanation:** The cathode is the negative electrode. Positive Al³⁺ ions are attracted to it, where they gain electrons (are reduced) to form molten aluminum metal: Al³⁺ + 3e⁻ → Al(l).

582. If the reaction quotient, Q_sp, for a salt is less than its K_sp value, then...
    A. a precipitate will form.
    B. the solution is saturated.
    C. the solution is supersaturated.
    D. the solution is unsaturated.
    **Answer:** D. the solution is unsaturated.
    **Explanation:** Q_sp < K_sp means the current concentration of ions is below the equilibrium (saturation) level. More solid can dissolve, and no precipitation will occur.

583. Which is the strongest oxidizing agent?
    (E° values: F₂+2e⁻→2F⁻ (+2.87V); Cl₂+2e⁻→2Cl⁻ (+1.36V); I₂+2e⁻→2I⁻ (+0.54V))
    A. F₂
    B. Cl₂
    C. I₂
    D. F⁻
    **Answer:** A. F₂
    **Explanation:** A stronger oxidizing agent is a species that is more easily reduced. The species with the most positive standard reduction potential (E°) has the greatest tendency to be reduced. F₂ has the highest E° value.

584. During the discharge of a lead-acid (car) battery, what happens to the concentration of the sulfuric acid electrolyte?
    A. It increases.
    B. It decreases.
    C. It remains constant.
    D. It turns into lead sulfate.
    **Answer:** B. It decreases.
    **Explanation:** In the overall discharge reaction (Pb + PbO₂ + 2H₂SO₄ → 2PbSO₄ + 2H₂O), sulfuric acid (H₂SO₄) is a reactant. As the battery discharges, H₂SO₄ is consumed, and water is produced, causing the density and concentration of the electrolyte to decrease.

---

**Chapter 15 & 16: Organic Chemistry**

585. The reaction type most characteristic of benzene is...
    A. Addition
    B. Elimination
    C. Free radical substitution
    D. Electrophilic substitution
    **Answer:** D. Electrophilic substitution
    **Explanation:** Due to the stability of its aromatic ring, benzene does not readily undergo addition reactions. Instead, it undergoes substitution reactions where an electrophile replaces a hydrogen atom, preserving the stable delocalized pi system.

586. The dehydration of butan-2-ol could produce...
    A. But-1-ene only
    B. But-2-ene only
    C. Both but-1-ene and but-2-ene
    D. Butane
    **Answer:** C. Both but-1-ene and but-2-ene
    **Explanation:** Dehydration involves removing the -OH group and a hydrogen from an adjacent carbon. Butan-2-ol has two different adjacent carbons (C1 and C3). Removing H from C1 gives but-1-ene. Removing H from C3 gives but-2-ene (which exists as cis/trans isomers). But-2-ene is often the major product (Zaitsev's rule).

587. Which of the following is the functional group of a ketone?
    A. R-OH
    B. R-CHO
    C. R-CO-R'
    D. R-COOH
    **Answer:** C. R-CO-R'
    **Explanation:** A ketone contains a carbonyl group (C=O) bonded to two other carbon atoms (alkyl or aryl groups, R and R').

588. Polyesters are formed by the condensation reaction between a dicarboxylic acid and a...
    A. Diamine
    B. Dialdehyde
    C. Diol
    D. Dialkene
    **Answer:** C. Diol
    **Explanation:** A polyester is formed by creating repeating ester linkages. This is achieved by reacting a monomer with two carboxylic acid groups and a monomer with two alcohol (hydroxyl) groups, a diol.

5-89. What is the product when propanal is gently warmed with Tollen's reagent?
    A. Propane
    B. Propan-1-ol
    C. Propanone
    D. Propanoate ion and silver metal
    **Answer:** D. Propanoate ion and silver metal
    **Explanation:** Tollen's reagent is a mild oxidizing agent. It oxidizes the aldehyde (propanal) to a carboxylate ion (propanoate). Simultaneously, the silver ions (Ag⁺) in the reagent are reduced to metallic silver (Ag), which forms a silver mirror.

590. The monomers of starch are molecules of...
    A. Fructose
    B. Amino acids
    C. Glucose
    D. Ethene
    **Answer:** C. Glucose
    **Explanation:** Starch and cellulose are natural polymers (polysaccharides) made up of many glucose monosaccharide units linked together by glycosidic bonds.

591. The reaction between an alcohol and a carboxylic acid is known as...
    A. Saponification
    B. Esterification
    C. Hydration
    D. Decarboxylation
    **Answer:** B. Esterification
    **Explanation:** This is the name for the reaction that produces an ester and water.

592. The formula CH₃(CH₂)₄COOH represents...
    A. Butanoic acid
    B. Pentanoic acid
    C. Hexanoic acid
    D. Heptanoic acid
    **Answer:** C. Hexanoic acid
    **Explanation:** The formula has one carbon in the CH₃ group, four carbons in the (CH₂)₄ group, and one carbon in the COOH group. Total carbons = 1 + 4 + 1 = 6. A six-carbon carboxylic acid is hexanoic acid.

593. A Grignard reagent (RMgX) is a strong...
    A. Electrophile
    B. Nucleophile and base
    C. Oxidizing agent
    D. Acid
    **Answer:** B. Nucleophile and base
    **Explanation:** The carbon-magnesium bond is highly polarized, making the carbon atom strongly nucleophilic (and basic). It is a key reagent for forming new carbon-carbon bonds by attacking electrophilic centers like carbonyl carbons.

594. An example of a tertiary alcohol is...
    A. propan-1-ol
    B. propan-2-ol
    C. 2-methylpropan-2-ol
    D. butan-2-ol
    **Answer:** C. 2-methylpropan-2-ol
    **Explanation:** A tertiary (3°) alcohol has the -OH group attached to a carbon atom that is itself bonded to three other carbon atoms. In 2-methylpropan-2-ol, the central carbon is bonded to three methyl groups and the -OH group.

595. Free radical reactions are often initiated by...
    A. An acid catalyst
    B. A base
    C. Water
    D. UV light or heat
    **Answer:** D. UV light or heat
    **Explanation:** Energy in the form of UV light or high heat is required to cause the homolytic cleavage of a covalent bond to form the initial free radicals.

596. Which compound would NOT be considered a hydrocarbon?
    A. Methane (CH₄)
    B. Benzene (C₆H₆)
    C. Ethene (C₂H₄)
    D. Ethanol (C₂H₅OH)
    **Answer:** D. Ethanol (C₂H₅OH)
    **Explanation:** Hydrocarbons are organic compounds containing *only* carbon and hydrogen. Ethanol also contains an oxygen atom, making it a derivative of a hydrocarbon (an alcohol), but not a hydrocarbon itself.

---

**Chapter 17 & 18: Laboratory & Experimental**

597. In a titration, the purpose of rinsing the burette with the titrant solution before filling it is to...
    A. Make sure the stopcock is not blocked.
    B. Remove any water droplets that would dilute the titrant.
    C. Coat the inside with a layer of titrant.
    D. Sterilize the burette.
    **Answer:** B. Remove any water droplets that would dilute the titrant.
    **Explanation:** If the burette is rinsed with water and not dried completely, the remaining water will dilute the standard solution (titrant) as it's filled, leading to an inaccurate concentration and incorrect titration results.

598. Which test can be used to distinguish between a saturated and an unsaturated hydrocarbon?
    A. Reaction with litmus paper
    B. Reaction with sodium metal
    C. Reaction with acidified potassium manganate(VII)
    D. Flame test
    **Answer:** C. Reaction with acidified potassium manganate(VII)
    **Explanation:** An unsaturated hydrocarbon (containing a C=C or C≡C bond) will decolorize the purple potassium manganate(VII) solution as the multiple bond is oxidized. A saturated hydrocarbon will not react under these mild conditions. This is an alternative to the bromine water test.

599. What piece of apparatus is used to accurately measure out 25.00 cm³ of a solution to be placed in the conical flask for titration?
    A. Burette
    B. Pipette
    C. Graduated cylinder
    D. Beaker
    **Answer:** B. Pipette
    **Explanation:** A 25.00 cm³ volumetric pipette is designed to deliver a single, highly accurate and precise volume of liquid, which is essential for the analyte in a titration.

600. The point in a titration where the moles of acid are stoichiometrically equal to the moles of base is the...
    A. Endpoint
    B. Equivalence point
    C. Neutralization point
    D. Half-equivalence point
    **Answer:** B. Equivalence point
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of the equivalence point. The endpoint is the observed color change of the indicator, which is chosen to be as close as possible to the equivalence point.

601. In qualitative analysis, the presence of the SO₄²⁻ ion is confirmed by adding acidified barium chloride. The acid is added first to...
    A. speed up the reaction.
    B. act as a catalyst.
    C. prevent the precipitation of other barium salts like BaCO₃ or BaSO₃.
    D. dissolve any existing BaSO₄ precipitate.
    **Answer:** C. prevent the precipitation of other barium salts like BaCO₃ or BaSO₃.
    **Explanation:** Carbonate and sulfite ions would also form white precipitates with barium chloride. However, barium carbonate and barium sulfite are soluble in acid, while barium sulfate is not. Adding acid first removes these interfering ions.

602. An ATTP (Alternative to Practical) question asks you to describe how to investigate the effect of concentration on reaction rate. The most important aspect of the experimental design is to...
    A. Use a wide range of different chemicals.
    B. Change the temperature for each trial.
    C. Keep all variables constant except for the concentration being varied.
    D. Ensure the reaction goes to completion as fast as possible.
    **Answer:** C. Keep all variables constant except for the concentration being varied.
    **Explanation:** This is the principle of a fair test. To isolate the effect of one variable (the independent variable, in this case, concentration), all other factors that could affect the rate (temperature, catalyst, total volume, etc.) must be kept constant (controlled variables).

603. To obtain a sample of pure water from an inky blue solution, the best technique would be...
    A. Simple distillation
    B. Filtration
    C. Crystallization
    D. Chromatography
    **Answer:** A. Simple distillation
    **Explanation:** Distillation separates a volatile solvent (water) from a non-volatile solute (ink). The water is boiled, the steam is condensed back into pure liquid water, and the ink is left behind.

604. A student records a burette reading as 24.5 cm³. The reading is likely inaccurate because...
    A. Burettes cannot measure this volume.
    B. The reading should be estimated to one more decimal place.
    C. Burettes should be read from the bottom up.
    D. This reading is too high.
    **Answer:** B. The reading should be estimated to one more decimal place.
    **Explanation:** Standard burettes are graduated in 0.1 cm³ increments. By convention and for accuracy, readings should be recorded to ±0.05 cm³, meaning to two decimal places (e.g., 24.50 cm³ or 24.55 cm³). A reading with only one decimal place is not precise enough.

605. Which error would cause the calculated molarity of an acid analyte to be higher than its true value in a titration with a standard base?
    A. Adding extra water to the conical flask.
    B. Rinsing the burette with water instead of the base solution.
    C. Overshooting the endpoint (adding too much base).
    D. Rinsing the pipette with water instead of the acid solution.
    **Answer:** C. Overshooting the endpoint (adding too much base).
    **Explanation:** Overshooting the endpoint means a larger-than-required volume of base (titrant) is recorded. In the calculation (Mₐ = (MᵦVᵦ)/Vₐ), a larger Vᵦ will lead to a larger calculated Mₐ, making the acid concentration appear higher than it actually is.

606. A Bunsen flame is hottest when the air hole is...
    A. Closed, producing a yellow flame.
    B. Half open, producing a blue-yellow flame.
    C. Fully open, producing a roaring blue flame.
    D. Closed, and the gas pressure is low.
    **Answer:** C. Fully open, producing a roaring blue flame.
    **Explanation:** A fully open air hole allows for a sufficient supply of oxygen, leading to complete combustion of the gas. This releases the maximum amount of energy and produces a hot, non-luminous blue flame.

607. When making a standard solution, the final step before use is to...
    A. heat the solution to ensure it is dissolved.
    B. stopper the volumetric flask and invert it several times.
    C. add one more drop of water to go over the mark.
    D. filter the solution to remove impurities.
    **Answer:** B. stopper the volumetric flask and invert it several times.
    **Explanation:** After carefully adding water up to the calibration mark, the solution must be thoroughly mixed by inverting the stoppered flask repeatedly to ensure the concentration is uniform throughout.

608. In the reaction CaCO₃(s) + 2HCl(aq) → CaCl₂(aq) + H₂O(l) + CO₂(g), the rate could be measured by monitoring...
    A. The change in color of the solution.
    B. The change in pH of the solution.
    C. The formation of the precipitate CaCl₂.
    D. The thermal conductivity of the flask.
    **Answer:** B. The change in pH of the solution.
    **Explanation:** As the reaction proceeds, the acid (HCl) is consumed, causing the [H⁺] to decrease and the pH to rise. Monitoring this pH change over time is a valid way to measure the reaction rate. Measuring the mass loss or gas volume would also work.

---
**Comprehensive & Mixed Questions**

609. An element Z has atomic number 16. It belongs to which block in the periodic table?
    A. s-block
    B. p-block
    C. d-block
    D. f-block
    **Answer:** B. p-block
    **Explanation:** The element with Z=16 is Sulfur. Its electron configuration is [Ne] 3s² 3p⁴. The highest energy electrons are filling the p-orbitals, so it is a p-block element.

610. The main pollutant from the exhaust of a car engine that is not CO or an oxide of nitrogen is...
    A. SO₂
    B. Unburnt hydrocarbons
    C. O₃
    D. Pb
    **Answer:** B. Unburnt hydrocarbons
    **Explanation:** Incomplete combustion in a car engine can release unburnt fuel (hydrocarbons) into the exhaust. Catalytic converters are designed to oxidize these, along with CO and NOx.

611. What is the IUPAC name for CH₃COOCH₂CH₃?
    A. Propanoic acid
    B. Ethyl ethanoate
    C. Butan-2-one
    D. Diethyl ether
    **Answer:** B. Ethyl ethanoate
    **Explanation:** This is an ester. The part of the name from the alcohol is "ethyl" (from ethanol). The part from the carboxylic acid is "ethanoate" (from ethanoic acid). Hence, ethyl ethanoate.

612. Which of the following best explains why a lump of sugar dissolves faster in hot tea than in iced tea?
    A. The tea is less dense when hot.
    B. Hot tea has a lower pH.
    C. Solvent particles move faster at higher temperatures, increasing collision frequency and energy with the solute.
    D. Sugar molecules are smaller when hot.
    **Answer:** C. Solvent particles move faster at higher temperatures, increasing collision frequency and energy with the solute.
    **Explanation:** The process of dissolving involves solvent molecules surrounding and carrying away solute particles. At higher temperatures, the solvent (water) molecules have more kinetic energy, leading to more frequent and forceful collisions with the sugar crystal, which breaks it apart and dissolves it faster.

613. The electrolysis of brine (concentrated aqueous NaCl) using inert electrodes produces...
    A. H₂(g) at the cathode, Cl₂(g) at the anode.
    B. Na(s) at the cathode, Cl₂(g) at the anode.
    C. H₂(g) at the cathode, O₂(g) at the anode.
    D. Na(s) at the cathode, O₂(g) at the anode.
    **Answer:** A. H₂(g) at the cathode, Cl₂(g) at the anode.
    **Explanation:** At the cathode, H₂O is more easily reduced than Na⁺ (H₂ is produced). At the anode, Cl⁻ is more easily oxidized than H₂O in a *concentrated* solution (Cl₂ is produced). This leaves a solution of NaOH.

614. Given ΔH° for the reaction N₂ + 3H₂ ⇌ 2NH₃ is -92 kJ, what is ΔH° for the reaction 2NH₃ ⇌ N₂ + 3H₂?
    A. -92 kJ
    B. +92 kJ
    C. -46 kJ
    D. +46 kJ
    **Answer:** B. +92 kJ
    **Explanation:** When a thermochemical equation is reversed, the sign of the enthalpy change (ΔH) is also reversed.

615. A substance has a giant molecular structure and a very high melting point. It could be...
    A. Ice
    B. Iodine
    C. Sand (SiO₂)
    D. Copper
    **Answer:** C. Sand (SiO₂)
    **Explanation:** Sand (silicon dioxide) is a classic example of a covalent network solid, which is described as having a giant molecular structure. These solids have very high melting points due to the need to break strong covalent bonds throughout the structure.

616. Temporary hardness of water can be removed by the addition of...
    A. Dilute HCl
    B. Sodium sulfate
    C. Calcium hydroxide (Clark's method)
    D. Chlorine gas
    **Answer:** C. Calcium hydroxide (Clark's method)
    **Explanation:** Adding a calculated amount of calcium hydroxide reacts with the soluble calcium hydrogencarbonate to precipitate insoluble calcium carbonate, thus removing the hardness. Boiling also removes temporary hardness.

617. An oxidation process involves...
    A. An increase in oxidation number
    B. A decrease in oxidation number
    C. A gain of electrons
    D. A reaction with oxygen only
    **Answer:** A. An increase in oxidation number
    **Explanation:** Oxidation is defined as a loss of electrons. This loss corresponds to an increase (becoming more positive) in the oxidation number of the species.

618. A functional group that can be both a hydrogen bond donor and acceptor is...
    A. An alkene C=C
    B. An ether R-O-R'
    C. An alcohol R-OH
    D. An alkane C-C
    **Answer:** C. An alcohol R-OH
    **Explanation:** An alcohol can donate the hydrogen from its -OH group to form a hydrogen bond, and the lone pairs on its oxygen atom can accept a hydrogen bond from another molecule.

619. Which statement is correct for the reaction at equilibrium: 2SO₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2SO₃(g)?
    A. The concentration of SO₂ must be equal to the concentration of SO₃.
    B. Adding more O₂ will cause the concentration of SO₃ to decrease.
    C. Removing the catalyst will cause the equilibrium to shift left.
    D. The rate of formation of SO₃ is equal to the rate of its decomposition.
    **Answer:** D. The rate of formation of SO₃ is equal to the rate of its decomposition.
    **Explanation:** This statement correctly describes the dynamic nature of equilibrium: the forward rate equals the reverse rate.

620. What is the empirical formula of a compound containing 40.0% C, 6.7% H, and 53.3% O? (C=12, H=1, O=16)
    A. C₂H₆O
    B. CH₂O
    C. CH₄O
    D. C₂H₄O
    **Answer:** B. CH₂O
    **Explanation:** Assume 100g sample.
    Moles C = 40.0/12.0 = 3.33.
    Moles H = 6.7/1.0 = 6.7.
    Moles O = 53.3/16.0 = 3.33.
    Divide all by the smallest (3.33): C = 1, H ≈ 2, O = 1. Empirical formula is CH₂O.

621. An atom of sodium has a mass number of 23. The number of neutrons in its nucleus is... (Atomic number of Na = 11)
    A. 11
    B. 12
    C. 23
    D. 34
    **Answer:** B. 12
    **Explanation:** Number of neutrons = Mass number - Atomic number = 23 - 11 = 12.

622. A student wants to separate sand from a solution of salt water. The best methods in order are...
    A. Filtration then distillation
    B. Distillation then filtration
    C. Evaporation then chromatography
    D. Chromatography then filtration
    **Answer:** A. Filtration then distillation
    **Explanation:** Filtration is used first to separate the insoluble sand. Then, distillation can be used to separate the pure water (solvent) from the dissolved salt (solute).

623. Which gas turns moist blue litmus paper red?
    A. Ammonia (NH₃)
    B. Hydrogen (H₂)
    C. Oxygen (O₂)
    D. Hydrogen Chloride (HCl)
    **Answer:** D. Hydrogen Chloride (HCl)
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen chloride gas dissolves in the moisture on the litmus paper to form hydrochloric acid, which is acidic and will turn blue litmus red. Ammonia is basic.

624. When but-1-ene reacts with hydrogen bromide (HBr), the major product is...
    A. 1-bromobutane
    B. 2-bromobutane
    C. 1,2-dibromobutane
    D. 2,2-dibromobutane
    **Answer:** B. 2-bromobutane
    **Explanation:** This is an electrophilic addition reaction that follows Markovnikov's rule. The H⁺ adds to the carbon with more hydrogens (C1), and the Br⁻ adds to the carbon with fewer hydrogens (the more substituted carbocation at C2).

625. What type of reaction occurs when an acid chloride reacts with an alcohol?
    A. S_N2 substitution
    B. Esterification
    C. Oxidation
    D. Elimination
    **Answer:** B. Esterification
    **Explanation:** This is a very efficient way to form an ester. The alcohol acts as a nucleophile, attacking the carbonyl carbon of the acid chloride and displacing the chloride ion. It's a type of nucleophilic acyl substitution, and the overall result is the formation of an ester.

626. The half-life of a radioactive isotope is 20 days. What fraction of the original sample will remain after 60 days?
    A. 1/2
    B. 1/4
    C. 1/8
    D. 1/16
    **Answer:** C. 1/8
    **Explanation:** 60 days is equal to 60/20 = 3 half-lives.
    After 1 half-life: 1/2 remains.
    After 2 half-lives: 1/4 remains.
    After 3 half-lives: 1/8 remains.

627. What is the geometry of the CCl₄ molecule?
    A. Linear
    B. Trigonal planar
    C. Tetrahedral
    D. Square planar
    **Answer:** C. Tetrahedral
    **Explanation:** The central carbon atom has 4 bonding pairs and 0 lone pairs. VSEPR theory predicts a tetrahedral geometry with 109.5° bond angles to minimize repulsion.

628. The Haber process is a reversible reaction shown by the equation N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g). Which of the following will NOT affect the position of equilibrium?
    A. Increase in pressure
    B. Decrease in temperature
    C. Addition of a catalyst
    D. Removal of ammonia
    **Answer:** C. Addition of a catalyst
    **Explanation:** A catalyst speeds up the forward and reverse reactions equally, allowing the system to reach equilibrium faster, but it does not change the concentrations of the species at equilibrium.

629. A cation is formed when a neutral atom...
    A. gains a proton.
    B. loses a proton.
    C. gains an electron.
    D. loses an electron.
    **Answer:** D. loses an electron.
    **Explanation:** Losing a negatively charged electron leaves the atom with more protons than electrons, resulting in a net positive charge (a cation).

630. Which element is essential for the vulcanization of rubber?
    A. Nitrogen
    B. Chlorine
    C. Carbon
    D. Sulfur
    **Answer:** D. Sulfur
    **Explanation:** Vulcanization involves heating natural rubber with sulfur to create cross-links between polymer chains, improving the rubber's mechanical properties.

631. In a Daniell cell (Zn/Zn²⁺ || Cu/Cu²⁺), the mass of the copper electrode...
    A. increases.
    B. decreases.
    C. remains constant.
    D. first increases then decreases.
    **Answer:** A. increases.
    **Explanation:** The copper electrode is the cathode, where reduction occurs. Cu²⁺ ions from the solution gain electrons and deposit as solid copper metal onto the electrode: Cu²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻ → Cu(s).

632. When chlorine gas is bubbled through an aqueous solution of potassium iodide, the solution turns brown because...
    A. potassium chloride is formed.
    B. chlorine is a brown gas.
    C. iodine is displaced.
    D. potassium iodide is oxidized.
    **Answer:** C. iodine is displaced.
    **Explanation:** Chlorine is more reactive than iodine, so it displaces iodine from the KI solution: Cl₂(g) + 2KI(aq) → 2KCl(aq) + I₂(aq). The displaced iodine (I₂) dissolves slightly in the aqueous solution to give a brown color.

633. The primary reason noble gases are unreactive is that they...
    A. are all gases at room temperature.
    B. have a low density.
    C. have a stable, full valence electron shell.
    D. have high ionization energies only.
    **Answer:** C. have a stable, full valence electron shell.
    **Explanation:** Having a complete octet (or duet for helium) in their outermost electron shell is an extremely stable configuration, meaning there is very little tendency to lose, gain, or share electrons to form chemical bonds.

634. A solution of soap in water has a pH...
    A. equal to 7.
    B. less than 7.
    C. greater than 7.
    D. equal to its pKa.
    **Answer:** C. greater than 7.
    **Explanation:** Soap is the salt of a strong base (NaOH) and a weak fatty acid. The fatty acid anion (the soap molecule) is a weak base that will hydrolyze water to produce OH⁻ ions, making the solution slightly alkaline (basic).

635. What is the approximate percentage of oxygen in the air?
    A. 78%
    B. 50%
    C. 21%
    D. 1%
    **Answer:** C. 21%
    **Explanation:** Dry air is composed of approximately 78% nitrogen, 21% oxygen, and about 1% other gases like argon and carbon dioxide.

636. Anhydrous Calcium Chloride is often used in a desiccator because it is...
    A. Deliquescent
    B. Efflorescent
    C. Insoluble
    D. Volatile
    **Answer:** A. Deliquescent
    **Explanation:** A desiccator needs a drying agent. Calcium chloride is deliquescent, meaning it strongly absorbs moisture from the air to the point of dissolving in it, making it an effective drying agent.

637. What type of bonding exists in solid Argon?
    A. Covalent bonds
    B. Ionic bonds
    C. Metallic bonds
    D. London dispersion forces
    **Answer:** D. London dispersion forces
    **Explanation:** Argon is a noble gas. In its solid state (at very low temperatures), the only forces holding the neutral, nonpolar atoms together are the weak, temporary dipole attractions known as London dispersion forces.

638. A gas occupies 30.0 cm³ at 1.0 atm. If the pressure is increased to 1.5 atm at constant temperature, the new volume is...
    A. 20.0 cm³
    B. 30.0 cm³
    C. 45.0 cm³
    D. 60.0 cm³
    **Answer:** A. 20.0 cm³
    **Explanation:** Use Boyle's Law: P₁V₁ = P₂V₂.
    (1.0 atm)(30.0 cm³) = (1.5 atm)(V₂)
    V₂ = (1.0 × 30.0) / 1.5 = 30.0 / 1.5 = 20.0 cm³.

639. When one mole of ethanol undergoes complete combustion, how many moles of oxygen gas are consumed?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** C. 3
    **Explanation:** First, write the balanced equation: C₂H₅OH + O₂ → CO₂ + H₂O.
    Balance C: C₂H₅OH + O₂ → 2CO₂ + H₂O.
    Balance H: C₂H₅OH + O₂ → 2CO₂ + 3H₂O.
    Balance O: Left side has 1 + 2 = 3 O. Right side has 2(2) + 3(1) = 7 O.
    We need 6 more O on the left, so we need 3O₂.
    Balanced equation: C₂H₅OH + 3O₂ → 2CO₂ + 3H₂O.
    So, 3 moles of O₂ are consumed.

640. Which factor does NOT influence the position of a chemical equilibrium?
    A. Concentration
    B. Temperature
    C. Pressure
    D. A catalyst
    **Answer:** D. A catalyst
    **Explanation:** A catalyst affects the rate at which equilibrium is reached but does not alter the equilibrium position itself. The other three factors can cause the equilibrium to shift according to Le Chatelier's principle.

641. The reaction between an alkene and a halogen is a(n)...
    A. Substitution reaction
    B. Addition reaction
    C. Elimination reaction
    D. Hydrolysis reaction
    **Answer:** B. Addition reaction
    **Explanation:** The double bond in the alkene breaks, and the halogen atoms add across it. This is a characteristic electrophilic addition reaction.

642. The element with the electron configuration 1s²2s²2p⁶3s²3p¹ is...
    A. Sodium
    B. Magnesium
    C. Aluminum
    D. Silicon
    **Answer:** C. Aluminum
    **Explanation:** The configuration has a total of 2+2+6+2+1 = 13 electrons. The element with atomic number 13 is Aluminum (Al).

643. Which pair represents structural isomers?
    A. O₂ and O₃
    B. ¹²C and ¹⁴C
    C. cis-but-2-ene and trans-but-2-ene
    D. Butan-1-ol and Butan-2-ol
    **Answer:** D. Butan-1-ol and Butan-2-ol
    **Explanation:** Structural isomers have the same molecular formula but different connectivity. Butan-1-ol and butan-2-ol are positional isomers, which is a type of structural isomerism. O₂ and O₃ are allotropes. ¹²C and ¹⁴C are isotopes. Cis/trans isomers are stereoisomers.

644. If ΔG for a reaction is positive, the reaction is...
    A. Spontaneous
    B. At equilibrium
    C. Non-spontaneous
    D. Exothermic
    **Answer:** C. Non-spontaneous
    **Explanation:** A positive ΔG indicates that the forward reaction is not thermodynamically favorable under the given conditions and will not proceed on its own. The reverse reaction would be spontaneous.

645. A burette is used in a titration to...
    A. Measure a precise, fixed volume of the analyte.
    B. Measure the temperature change of the reaction.
    C. Accurately dispense a variable volume of the titrant.
    D. Hold the indicator solution.
    **Answer:** C. Accurately dispense a variable volume of the titrant.
    **Explanation:** The key features of a burette are its graduation marks along its length and a stopcock, which allow for the precise measurement and controlled delivery of variable volumes of liquid.

646. A white precipitate is formed when BaCl₂ solution is added to a solution of salt X. The precipitate is insoluble in dilute HCl. Salt X likely contains the anion...
    A. CO₃²⁻
    B. SO₃²⁻
    C. SO₄²⁻
    D. NO₃⁻
    **Answer:** C. SO₄²⁻
    **Explanation:** BaSO₄ is a white precipitate that is famously insoluble in strong acids. Both BaCO₃ and BaSO₃ are also white precipitates, but they would dissolve in HCl.

647. Which substance is used to make the anode in the Hall-Héroult cell for aluminum extraction?
    A. Graphite (carbon)
    B. Platinum
    C. Steel
    D. Aluminum
    **Answer:** A. Graphite (carbon)
    **Explanation:** Large carbon blocks are used as the anodes. They are consumed during the process because the oxygen produced at the anode (from the electrolysis of the oxide ions) reacts with the hot carbon to form CO and CO₂.

648. The pH of a solution can be determined accurately using a...
    A. Litmus paper
    B. Universal indicator paper
    C. pH meter
    D. Conductance meter
    **Answer:** C. pH meter
    **Explanation:** While indicators give a range or an approximate pH, a pH meter is an electronic instrument that provides a direct and precise numerical measurement of the pH.

649. Which of the following is an example of an addition polymer?
    A. Nylon
    B. PET (Terylene)
    C. Polystyrene
    D. Protein
    **Answer:** C. Polystyrene
    **Explanation:** Polystyrene is formed by the addition polymerization of styrene (phenylethene) monomers across their C=C double bonds. The others are all condensation polymers.

650. The reaction Mg(s) + 2H⁺(aq) → Mg²⁺(aq) + H₂(g) is an example of...
    A. Single displacement and redox reaction.
    B. Double displacement and neutralization.
    C. Decomposition reaction.
    D. Combustion reaction.
    **Answer:** A. Single displacement and redox reaction.
    **Explanation:** Mg displaces H⁺, making it a single displacement reaction. Mg is also oxidized (0 to +2) and H⁺ is reduced (+1 to 0), so it is a redox reaction.

651. Saponification is the process of...
    A. a neutralisation reaction.
    B. acid hydrolysis of an ester.
    C. alkaline hydrolysis of a fat or oil.
    D. the reaction between an acid and a base.
    **Answer:** C. alkaline hydrolysis of a fat or oil.
    **Explanation:** Saponification specifically refers to the hydrolysis of an ester (like a fat or oil) using a strong alkali (like NaOH) to produce soap and glycerol.

652. A molecule that has the same molecular and empirical formula is...
    A. Benzene (C₆H₆)
    B. Glucose (C₆H₁₂O₆)
    C. Ethene (C₂H₄)
    D. Water (H₂O)
    **Answer:** D. Water (H₂O)
    **Explanation:** The ratio of atoms in H₂O is 2:1, which cannot be simplified further. Therefore, its molecular formula (H₂O) is the same as its empirical formula. The others can all be simplified (CH, CH₂O, CH₂).

653. In an experiment to find the rate of reaction between magnesium ribbon and hydrochloric acid, which of the following is NOT a suitable method?
    A. Measuring the volume of gas produced over time.
    B. Measuring the change in mass of the flask and contents over time.
    C. Measuring the change in color of the solution over time.
    D. Measuring the time taken for the magnesium to disappear.
    **Answer:** C. Measuring the change in color of the solution over time.
    **Explanation:** All reactants (Mg, HCl) and products (MgCl₂, H₂) in this reaction are colorless. There is no color change to monitor with a colorimeter. The other methods are all valid ways to measure the rate.

654. The process by which nitrogen gas from the atmosphere is converted into ammonia is called...
    A. Denitrification
    B. Nitrification
    C. Nitrogen fixation
    D. Ammonification
    **Answer:** C. Nitrogen fixation
    **Explanation:** Nitrogen fixation is the general term for any process (biological or industrial, like the Haber process) that converts inert atmospheric nitrogen (N₂) into a more usable form like ammonia (NH₃).

655. An electron transition from n=4 to n=2 in an atom will result in the...
    A. absorption of energy.
    B. emission of a photon.
    C. formation of a cation.
    D. formation of an anion.
    **Answer:** B. emission of a photon.
    **Explanation:** When an electron falls from a higher energy level (n=4) to a lower energy level (n=2), the excess energy is released, often in the form of a discrete packet of light energy called a photon.

656. In the Daniell cell, what happens at the anode?
    A. Cu atoms are oxidized to Cu²⁺ ions.
    B. Cu²⁺ ions are reduced to Cu atoms.
    C. Zn atoms are oxidized to Zn²⁺ ions.
    D. Zn²⁺ ions are reduced to Zn atoms.
    **Answer:** C. Zn atoms are oxidized to Zn²⁺ ions.
    **Explanation:** Zinc is more reactive than copper, so it acts as the anode. At the anode, oxidation occurs: Zn(s) → Zn²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻.

657. Which separation technique is most appropriate for obtaining petrol from crude oil?
    A. Filtration
    B. Sublimation
    C. Chromatography
    D. Fractional distillation
    **Answer:** D. Fractional distillation
    **Explanation:** Crude oil is a complex mixture of hydrocarbons with different chain lengths and boiling points. Fractional distillation is the industrial process used to separate this mixture into useful fractions like petrol, kerosene, and diesel based on their boiling point ranges.

658. In the reaction of methane with chlorine in the presence of sunlight, the first step is...
    A. CH₄ + Cl• → CH₃• + HCl
    B. Cl₂ → 2Cl•
    C. CH₃• + Cl₂ → CH₃Cl + Cl•
    D. CH₃• + CH₃• → C₂H₆
    **Answer:** B. Cl₂ → 2Cl•
    **Explanation:** This is a free radical reaction. The very first step must be the formation of radicals from a stable molecule. This is the initiation step, where UV light causes the homolytic cleavage of the Cl-Cl bond to form two chlorine radicals.

659. The chemical formula for calcium hydrogencarbonate is...
    A. CaHCO₃
    B. Ca(HCO₃)₂
    C. Ca₂(HCO₃)
    D. CaCO₃
    **Answer:** B. Ca(HCO₃)₂
    **Explanation:** The calcium ion has a +2 charge (Ca²⁺). The hydrogencarbonate (bicarbonate) ion has a -1 charge (HCO₃⁻). To form a neutral compound, two hydrogencarbonate ions are needed to balance the charge of one calcium ion.

660. What mass of solute is needed to prepare 500 cm³ of a 0.2 M solution of KCl? (K=39, Cl=35.5)
    A. 7.45 g
    B. 14.9 g
    C. 3.73 g
    D. 37.3 g
    **Answer:** A. 7.45 g
    **Explanation:**
    1. Molar mass of KCl = 39 + 35.5 = 74.5 g/mol.
    2. Volume = 500 cm³ = 0.5 dm³.
    3. Moles needed = Molarity × Volume = 0.2 mol/dm³ × 0.5 dm³ = 0.1 mol.
    4. Mass needed = Moles × Molar Mass = 0.1 mol × 74.5 g/mol = 7.45 g.

661. In the reaction of concentrated tetraoxosulphate(VI) acid with ethanol, what two roles can the acid play?
    A. Catalyst and reducing agent
    B. Dehydrating agent and catalyst
    C. Oxidizing agent and reducing agent
    D. Catalyst and base
    **Answer:** B. Dehydrating agent and catalyst
    **Explanation:** In the dehydration of ethanol to ethene, the acid acts as a dehydrating agent and a catalyst. In the esterification of ethanol with a carboxylic acid, it acts as a catalyst. These are the main roles for this reaction combination.

662. Which of the following elements can form an ion with a charge of +3?
    A. Sodium
    B. Calcium
    C. Aluminum
    D. Chlorine
    **Answer:** C. Aluminum
    **Explanation:** Aluminum is in Group 13 and has 3 valence electrons ([Ne] 3s² 3p¹). It readily loses these three electrons to form the stable Al³⁺ ion.

663. When ammonium chloride is dissolved in water, the solution feels cold. This is because the dissolving process is...
    A. Exothermic
    B. Endothermic
    C. A neutralization reaction
    D. Very slow
    **Answer:** B. Endothermic
    **Explanation:** A process that makes the surroundings feel cold is one that is absorbing heat from the surroundings. This is an endothermic process (ΔH_solution is positive).

664. What is the pH of a 0.05 M solution of H₂SO₄? (Assume complete dissociation)
    A. 1.3
    B. 1.0
    C. 0.05
    D. 0.1
    **Answer:** B. 1.0
    **Explanation:** H₂SO₄ is a strong acid that is diprotic. It dissociates to produce two H⁺ ions for every one molecule: H₂SO₄ → 2H⁺ + SO₄²⁻.
    Therefore, [H⁺] = 2 × 0.05 M = 0.1 M.
    pH = -log[H⁺] = -log(0.1) = -log(10⁻¹) = 1.

665. The primary constituent of limestone is...
    A. Calcium oxide
    B. Calcium sulfate
    C. Calcium carbonate
    D. Silicon dioxide
    **Answer:** C. Calcium carbonate
    **Explanation:** Limestone, marble, and chalk are all naturally occurring forms of calcium carbonate (CaCO₃).

666. Anode sludge obtained during the refining of copper may contain...
    A. Zinc and iron
    B. Sodium and potassium
    C. Silver and gold
    D. Aluminum and magnesium
    **Answer:** C. Silver and gold
    **Explanation:** During electrolytic refining, less reactive metals than copper (like the precious metals Ag, Au, Pt) do not get oxidized at the anode. They simply fall off as the anode dissolves and collect at the bottom as a valuable sludge.

667. What is the molecular formula of a compound with the empirical formula CH and a molar mass of 78 g/mol? (C=12, H=1)
    A. C₂H₂
    B. C₄H₄
    C. C₆H₆
    D. C₇H₆
    **Answer:** C. C₆H₆
    **Explanation:** Mass of empirical formula CH = 12 + 1 = 13 g/mol.
    Ratio n = Molar Mass / Empirical Mass = 78 / 13 = 6.
    Molecular formula = (CH)₆ = C₆H₆. This is benzene.

668. Ethene polymerises to form poly(ethene). This is an example of...
    A. Hydrolysis
    B. Addition reaction
    C. Condensation reaction
    D. Cracking
    **Answer:** B. Addition reaction
    **Explanation:** Ethene monomers add to each other across their double bonds to form the long polymer chain with no loss of any atoms.

669. A solution has a hydrogen ion concentration of 1.0 × 10⁻¹⁰ mol dm⁻³. The solution is...
    A. Strongly acidic
    B. Weakly acidic
    C. Neutral
    D. Basic
    **Answer:** D. Basic
    **Explanation:** A hydrogen ion concentration of 1.0 × 10⁻¹⁰ M corresponds to a pH of 10. Any pH greater than 7 is basic.

670. The substance that is reduced in the reaction 2FeCl₃ + H₂S → 2FeCl₂ + 2HCl + S is...
    A. FeCl₃
    B. H₂S
    C. HCl
    D. S
    **Answer:** A. FeCl₃
    **Explanation:** The oxidation state of Fe in FeCl₃ is +3. In FeCl₂, it is +2. The oxidation number of Fe has decreased, meaning FeCl₃ has been reduced. (The oxidation state of S in H₂S is -2, and in S it is 0, so H₂S has been oxidized).

671. What volume of CO₂ at s.t.p. is produced on heating 20g of CaCO₃? [Ca = 40, C=12, O=16]
    A. 2.24 dm³
    B. 4.48 dm³
    C. 11.2 dm³
    D. 22.4 dm³
    **Answer:** B. 4.48 dm³
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Reaction: CaCO₃(s) → CaO(s) + CO₂(g)
    2.  Molar mass CaCO₃ = 40 + 12 + 3(16) = 100 g/mol.
    3.  Moles CaCO₃ = 20 g / 100 g/mol = 0.2 mol.
    4.  Mole ratio CaCO₃ to CO₂ is 1:1. So, 0.2 mol of CO₂ is produced.
    5.  Volume of CO₂ at STP = Moles × Molar Volume = 0.2 mol × 22.4 dm³/mol = 4.48 dm³.

672. What are the two main steps in the purification of municipal water?
    A. Aeration and filtration
    B. Coagulation and chlorination
    C. Sedimentation and distillation
    D. Coagulation/sedimentation/filtration and chlorination
    **Answer:** D. Coagulation/sedimentation/filtration and chlorination
    **Explanation:** Water purification is a multi-step process. First, suspended particles are removed by adding a coagulant (like alum), allowing particles to clump and settle (sedimentation), followed by filtration. Second, the water is disinfected to kill harmful microorganisms, most commonly by adding chlorine.

673. Which of the following compounds exhibits optical isomerism?
    A. CH₃CHClCH₃
    B. CH₃CH₂CH₂OH
    C. CH₃CH(OH)COOH
    D. CCl₂F₂
    **Answer:** C. CH₃CH(OH)COOH
    **Explanation:** Optical isomerism requires a chiral center (a carbon atom bonded to four different groups). In CH₃CH(OH)COOH (lactic acid), the central carbon is bonded to: -CH₃, -H, -OH, and -COOH. These are four different groups, making it chiral.

674. During the electrolysis of water acidified with H₂SO₄, the gas collected at the anode is...
    A. Hydrogen
    B. Oxygen
    C. Sulfur dioxide
    D. Hydrogen sulfide
    **Answer:** B. Oxygen
    **Explanation:** At the anode (positive electrode), negative ions are oxidized. H₂SO₄ is the electrolyte, providing SO₄²⁻ and H₂O. Water is more easily oxidized than the sulfate ion. The oxidation of water (2H₂O → O₂ + 4H⁺ + 4e⁻) produces oxygen gas.

675. A reaction is at equilibrium when the...
    A. container is sealed.
    B. opposing reactions cease.
    C. rates of opposing reactions are equal.
    D. concentrations of reactants are equal.
    **Answer:** C. rates of opposing reactions are equal.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of a dynamic chemical equilibrium. The forward and reverse reactions continue to occur, but at the same speed, so there is no net change.

676. The general formula for alkyl groups is...
    A. C_n H_(2n)
    B. C_n H_(2n+1)
    C. C_n H_(2n+2)
    D. C_n H_(2n-1)
    **Answer:** B. C_n H_(2n+1)
    **Explanation:** An alkyl group is an alkane missing one hydrogen atom. Since the formula for an alkane is C_n H_(2n+2), the alkyl group formula is C_n H_(2n+1).

677. What is the major product formed when propene reacts with water in the presence of an acid?
    A. Propan-1-ol
    B. Propan-2-ol
    C. Propane
    D. Propanal
    **Answer:** B. Propan-2-ol
    **Explanation:** This is the hydration of an unsymmetrical alkene. According to Markovnikov's rule, the H⁺ adds to the carbon with more hydrogens (C1), and the OH⁻ (from water) adds to the more substituted carbon (C2), forming propan-2-ol.

678. The atomicity of ozone is...
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** C. 3
    **Explanation:** Atomicity refers to the number of atoms in one molecule of an element. Ozone has the chemical formula O₃, so its atomicity is 3. The atomicity of oxygen gas (O₂) is 2.

679. Which of the following elements does NOT exist as a diatomic molecule at room temperature?
    A. Nitrogen
    B. Oxygen
    C. Fluorine
    D. Helium
    **Answer:** D. Helium
    **Explanation:** Helium is a noble gas (Group 18). Noble gases exist as individual, unbonded atoms (they are monatomic). Nitrogen (N₂), Oxygen (O₂), and Fluorine (F₂) are all diatomic.

680. What quantity of electricity is required to deposit 1 mole of Aluminum from a solution of Al³⁺? (F = Faraday constant)
    A. 1 F
    B. 2 F
    C. 3 F
    D. 6 F
    **Answer:** C. 3 F
    **Explanation:** The half-reaction for the deposition is Al³⁺ + 3e⁻ → Al. To deposit 1 mole of Al atoms, 3 moles of electrons are required. The charge of one mole of electrons is one Faraday (F). Therefore, 3F of electricity is needed.

681. Which of the following statements is true for a neutral solution at 25°C?
    A. [H⁺] > [OH⁻]
    B. [H⁺] < [OH⁻]
    C. [H⁺] = [OH⁻] = 1.0 x 10⁻⁷ M
    D. pH + pOH = 7
    **Answer:** C. [H⁺] = [OH⁻] = 1.0 x 10⁻⁷ M
    **Explanation:** In a neutral solution, the concentrations of hydrogen ions and hydroxide ions are equal. At 25°C, they both have a value of 1.0 x 10⁻⁷ M, corresponding to a pH and pOH of 7.

682. The common industrial method for producing ethene is...
    A. fermentation of glucose.
    B. catalytic cracking of alkanes.
    C. hydrogenation of ethyne.
    D. dehydration of sucrose.
    **Answer:** B. catalytic cracking of alkanes.
    **Explanation:** Ethene is a crucial starting material for the polymer industry. It is produced on a massive scale by the thermal or catalytic cracking of long-chain hydrocarbons found in crude oil fractions like naphtha.

683. An atom of ¹³¹₅₃I contains...
    A. 53 protons, 78 neutrons, and 53 electrons
    B. 53 protons, 131 neutrons, and 53 electrons
    C. 78 protons, 53 neutrons, and 78 electrons
    D. 131 protons, 53 neutrons, and 131 electrons
    **Answer:** A. 53 protons, 78 neutrons, and 53 electrons
    **Explanation:** Atomic number Z = 53 (protons). Mass number A = 131. Neutrons = A - Z = 131 - 53 = 78. In a neutral atom, electrons = protons = 53.

684. In the periodic table, the period to which an element belongs indicates the...
    A. number of valence electrons.
    B. number of protons.
    C. principal quantum number of the outermost shell.
    D. total number of electrons.
    **Answer:** C. principal quantum number of the outermost shell.
    **Explanation:** The period number (1, 2, 3...) corresponds directly to the value of 'n' for the highest energy electron shell that is occupied in that element.

685. A mixture of petrol and water can be separated using...
    A. a separating funnel.
    B. filtration.
    C. fractional distillation.
    D. sublimation.
    **Answer:** A. a separating funnel.
    **Explanation:** Petrol and water are immiscible (they do not mix) and have different densities. They will form two distinct layers in a separating funnel, which can then be drained off one at a time.

686. Which of the following is an example of a chemical change?
    A. Dissolving sugar in water
    B. Melting an ice cube
    C. Burning a candle
    D. Grinding salt into a powder
    **Answer:** C. Burning a candle
    **Explanation:** Burning (combustion) is a chemical reaction where the wax (a hydrocarbon) reacts with oxygen to form new substances (carbon dioxide and water), releasing heat and light. The other options are physical changes.

687. What is the maximum number of covalent bonds a carbon atom can form?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** D. 4
    **Explanation:** Carbon has 4 valence electrons. To achieve a stable octet, it forms four covalent bonds.

688. The most suitable indicator for the titration between NaOH and CH₃COOH would have a pH range of...
    A. 3 - 5
    B. 6 - 8
    C. 8 - 10
    D. 1 - 2
    **Answer:** C. 8 - 10
    **Explanation:** This is a strong base-weak acid titration. The resulting salt (CH₃COONa) hydrolyzes to make the solution basic at the equivalence point (pH > 7). An indicator like phenolphthalein, which changes color in the 8-10 pH range, is appropriate.

689. If a reaction is exothermic and there is an increase in entropy, the reaction will be...
    A. spontaneous only at high temperatures.
    B. spontaneous only at low temperatures.
    C. spontaneous at all temperatures.
    D. non-spontaneous at all temperatures.
    **Answer:** C. spontaneous at all temperatures.
    **Explanation:** The condition for spontaneity is ΔG < 0. From ΔG = ΔH - TΔS, if ΔH is negative (exothermic) and ΔS is positive (increase in entropy), then ΔG will be negative regardless of the temperature.

690. When heated with an oxidizing agent, propan-2-ol will produce...
    A. propanal.
    B. propanoic acid.
    C. propanone.
    D. propene.
    **Answer:** C. propanone.
    **Explanation:** Propan-2-ol is a secondary alcohol. Oxidation of a secondary alcohol yields a ketone. The ketone corresponding to propan-2-ol is propanone (acetone).

691. Which of the following has a giant ionic structure?
    A. Diamond
    B. Sodium chloride
    C. Iodine
    D. Carbon dioxide
    **Answer:** B. Sodium chloride
    **Explanation:** Sodium chloride consists of a vast, repeating 3D lattice of positively charged Na⁺ ions and negatively charged Cl⁻ ions held together by strong electrostatic forces (ionic bonds).

692. Avogadro's law states that equal volumes of gases at the same temperature and pressure contain an equal number of...
    A. atoms.
    B. molecules.
    C. ions.
    D. protons.
    **Answer:** B. molecules.
    **Explanation:** This is the precise statement of Avogadro's law. It applies to molecules (or atoms, for monatomic gases).

693. What volume of a 0.5M stock solution is needed to prepare 100 cm³ of a 0.1M solution?
    A. 10 cm³
    B. 20 cm³
    C. 50 cm³
    D. 200 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 20 cm³
    **Explanation:** Use the dilution equation M₁V₁ = M₂V₂.
    (0.5 M)(V₁) = (0.1 M)(100 cm³)
    V₁ = (0.1 × 100) / 0.5 = 10 / 0.5 = 20 cm³.

694. An aqueous solution of copper(II) sulfate is electrolyzed using copper electrodes. The reaction at the anode is...
    A. Cu²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻ → Cu(s)
    B. 2H₂O(l) → O₂(g) + 4H⁺(aq) + 4e⁻
    C. Cu(s) → Cu²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻
    D. SO₄²⁻(aq) → S₂O₈²⁻(aq) + 2e⁻
    **Answer:** C. Cu(s) → Cu²⁺(aq) + 2e⁻
    **Explanation:** When using an active electrode (like copper) during the electrolysis of a solution of its own salt, the anode itself is more easily oxidized than water or the sulfate ion. The copper anode dissolves to replenish the Cu²⁺ ions in solution.

695. Which of the following affects the value of the equilibrium constant, K_c?
    A. Change in concentration of reactants
    B. Change in pressure
    C. Change in temperature
    D. Addition of a catalyst
    **Answer:** C. Change in temperature
    **Explanation:** The value of K_c is constant for a given reaction at a specific temperature. Only a change in temperature will change the numerical value of K_c. The other factors can shift the equilibrium position but do not change K_c.

696. The general formula for a carboxylic acid is...
    A. R-OH
    B. R-O-R'
    C. R-CHO
    D. R-COOH
    **Answer:** D. R-COOH
    **Explanation:** The defining functional group for a carboxylic acid is the carboxyl group, -COOH.

697. How many moles are present in 4.9g of H₂SO₄? (H=1, S=32, O=16)
    A. 0.05 mol
    B. 0.10 mol
    C. 0.50 mol
    D. 1.0 mol
    **Answer:** A. 0.05 mol
    **Explanation:** Molar mass of H₂SO₄ = 2(1) + 32 + 4(16) = 98 g/mol.
    Moles = mass / molar mass = 4.9 g / 98 g/mol = 0.05 mol.

698. A reducing agent...
    A. gains electrons and is oxidized.
    B. donates electrons and is oxidized.
    C. gains electrons and is reduced.
    D. donates electrons and is reduced.
    **Answer:** B. donates electrons and is oxidized.
    **Explanation:** A reducing agent *causes* reduction in another substance by donating electrons to it. In the process of donating electrons, it itself is oxidized.

699. Which of the following is used to test for a non-reducing sugar?
    A. Add Benedict's solution and heat.
    B. Add dilute acid, heat, neutralize, then add Benedict's solution and heat.
    C. Add iodine solution.
    D. Add Tollen's reagent and warm.
    **Answer:** B. Add dilute acid, heat, neutralize, then add Benedict's solution and heat.
    **Explanation:** Non-reducing sugars (like sucrose) do not react with Benedict's solution directly. They must first be hydrolyzed (by boiling with acid) into their monosaccharide components (reducing sugars). After neutralization, these will then give a positive test with Benedict's solution (a color change from blue to green/yellow/orange/red).

700. In a chemical reaction, if the theoretical yield is 'T' and the actual yield is 'A', the percentage yield is given by...
    A. (T/A) x 100
    B. (A/T) x 100
    C. (T - A) / T x 100
    D. A x T x 100
    **Answer:** B. (A/T) x 100
    **Explanation:** Percentage yield is a measure of the efficiency of a reaction, calculated as the ratio of the amount of product actually obtained to the maximum amount that was theoretically possible.

701. Which separation technique would be most suitable to obtain pure salt from an aqueous solution of salt contaminated with mud?
    A. Distillation
    B. Evaporation
    C. Decantation, followed by crystallization
    D. Filtration, followed by evaporation/crystallization
    **Answer:** D. Filtration, followed by evaporation/crystallization
    **Explanation:** First, the insoluble mud must be removed by filtration. Then, the pure salt can be recovered from the resulting clear salt solution (the filtrate) by either evaporating all the water or by evaporating some water and then cooling to allow pure salt crystals to form (crystallization).

702. In the Lewis structure for N₂, there is/are...
    A. A single bond and 6 lone pair electrons.
    B. A double bond and 4 lone pair electrons.
    C. A triple bond and 2 lone pair electrons.
    D. Three single bonds.
    **Answer:** C. A triple bond and 2 lone pair electrons.
    **Explanation:** To satisfy the octet rule for both nitrogen atoms, they must share 6 electrons (a triple bond). Each nitrogen atom also has one lone pair of non-bonding electrons.

703. The element Chlorine (Cl) will most likely...
    A. lose one electron to form a Cl⁺ ion.
    B. lose two electrons to form a Cl²⁺ ion.
    C. gain one electron to form a Cl⁻ ion.
    D. gain two electrons to form a Cl²⁻ ion.
    **Answer:** C. gain one electron to form a Cl⁻ ion.
    **Explanation:** Chlorine is in Group 17 and has 7 valence electrons. It has a high electronegativity and will most readily gain one electron to achieve a stable octet, forming the chloride ion, Cl⁻.

704. An endothermic process...
    A. has a negative ΔH value.
    B. releases heat to the surroundings.
    C. feels warm to the touch.
    D. absorbs heat from the surroundings.
    **Answer:** D. absorbs heat from the surroundings.
    **Explanation:** In an endothermic reaction, heat flows from the surroundings into the system, causing the surroundings to cool down. The process has a positive ΔH value.

705. The chemical that is present in the greatest proportion in a solution is known as the...
    A. solute
    B. solvent
    C. concentrate
    D. titrant
    **Answer:** B. solvent
    **Explanation:** By definition, the solvent is the component of a solution that is present in the largest amount. The solute is the substance that is dissolved in the solvent.

706. A characteristic reaction of alkenes is...
    A. Free-radical substitution
    B. Electrophilic substitution
    C. Electrophilic addition
    D. Nucleophilic addition
    **Answer:** C. Electrophilic addition
    **Explanation:** The electron-rich pi bond of the C=C double bond in an alkene is susceptible to attack by electrophiles (electron-loving species), leading to an addition reaction.

707. What type of bond holds the atoms together within a water molecule?
    A. Ionic bond
    B. Hydrogen bond
    C. Polar covalent bond
    D. Metallic bond
    **Answer:** C. Polar covalent bond
    **Explanation:** The bond between Oxygen and Hydrogen atoms *within* the molecule is a polar covalent bond due to the difference in their electronegativities. Hydrogen bonds are the forces *between* separate water molecules.

708. What is the concentration in mol/dm³ of a solution containing 11.7g of NaCl in 200cm³ of solution? (Na=23, Cl=35.5)
    A. 0.5 M
    B. 1.0 M
    C. 1.5 M
    D. 2.0 M
    **Answer:** B. 1.0 M
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Molar Mass of NaCl = 23 + 35.5 = 58.5 g/mol.
    2.  Moles of NaCl = 11.7g / 58.5 g/mol = 0.2 mol.
    3.  Volume = 200 cm³ = 0.2 dm³.
