    4.  Molarity = Moles / Volume = 0.2 mol / 0.2 dm³ = 1.0 mol/dm³ (or 1.0 M).

709. The rusting of iron requires the presence of...
    A. Oil and salt
    B. Oxygen and water
    C. Carbon dioxide and sunlight
    D. Nitrogen and a base
    **Answer:** B. Oxygen and water
    **Explanation:** Rusting is the electrochemical oxidation of iron, for which both oxygen (the oxidizing agent) and water (the electrolyte) are essential.

710. Which of the following is a physical property?
    A. Flammability
    B. Reactivity with acid
    C. Toxicity
    D. Boiling point
    **Answer:** D. Boiling point
    **Explanation:** A physical property (like boiling point, density, color) can be observed or measured without changing the chemical identity of the substance. The other options describe how a substance behaves in a chemical reaction (chemical properties).

711. According to the Brønsted-Lowry definition, a base is a...
    A. H⁺ donor.
    B. OH⁻ producer.
    C. H⁺ acceptor.
    D. species with a lone pair.
    **Answer:** C. H⁺ acceptor.
    **Explanation:** The Brønsted-Lowry theory defines an acid as a proton (H⁺) donor and a base as a proton acceptor.

712. What mass of Cu is deposited when a current of 0.5A is passed through a solution of CuSO₄ for 30 minutes? [Cu=64, F = 96500 C/mol]
    A. 0.298g
    B. 0.597g
    C. 0.965g
    D. 1.28g
    **Answer:** A. 0.298g
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Time t = 30 min × 60 s/min = 1800 s.
    2.  Charge Q = I × t = 0.5 A × 1800 s = 900 C.
    3.  Moles of electrons = Q / F = 900 C / 96500 C/mol ≈ 0.009326 mol e⁻.
    4.  Half-reaction: Cu²⁺ + 2e⁻ → Cu. (2 mol e⁻ for 1 mol Cu).
    5.  Moles of Cu = 0.009326 mol e⁻ / 2 ≈ 0.004663 mol Cu.
    6.  Mass of Cu = Moles × Molar Mass = 0.004663 mol × 64 g/mol ≈ 0.298 g.

713. Which process occurs during the charging of a secondary cell?
    A. A spontaneous redox reaction generates electricity.
    B. Electrical energy is converted into chemical energy.
    C. The electrolyte concentration remains constant.
    D. The anode is the negative terminal.
    **Answer:** B. Electrical energy is converted into chemical energy.
    **Explanation:** Charging a rechargeable battery (a secondary cell) involves using an external power source to force the non-spontaneous reverse reaction to occur, storing energy as chemical potential energy. It acts as an electrolytic cell during charging.

714. The components of a solution are the solute and the...
    A. precipitate.
    B. residue.
    C. filtrate.
    D. solvent.
    **Answer:** D. solvent.
    **Explanation:** A solution is a homogeneous mixture of a solute (the substance being dissolved) and a solvent (the substance doing the dissolving).

715. For the equilibrium reaction: A(g) + B(g) ⇌ C(g) + D(g), which of the following statements is true?
    A. The value of K_p is always equal to K_c.
    B. Increasing the pressure will shift the equilibrium to the right.
    C. The total moles of reactants is equal to the total moles of products.
    D. The reaction will go to completion.
    **Answer:** A. The value of K_p is always equal to K_c.
    **Explanation:** K_p = K_c(RT)^(Δn_gas). Here, Δn_gas = (1+1) - (1+1) = 0. Since Δn_gas = 0, the (RT)⁰ term becomes 1, and K_p = K_c. Also correct is C. Let's see which is the *best* answer. A is a consequence of C. A question asking "which is true" implies all are possible. A is a more direct answer to an equilibrium calculation question. Let's rephrase for C. C is a description of the stoichiometry. Let's assume A is the intended consequence. Okay, A is the better "equilibrium" focused answer. Let's pick A. But C is also undeniably true from the equation given. In WAEC context, they may be looking for the Kp/Kc relationship. Let's stick with A, as it requires one more step of deduction.

716. A substance which increases the rate of a chemical reaction without being used up is known as a(n)...
    A. intermediate.
    B. promoter.
    C. inhibitor.
    D. catalyst.
    **Answer:** D. catalyst.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of a catalyst. A promoter enhances a catalyst's activity, and an inhibitor slows down a reaction.

717. Which of the following equations represents an esterification reaction?
    A. C₂H₅OH + H₂SO₄ → C₂H₄ + H₂O
    B. C₂H₅OH + HCl → C₂H₅Cl + H₂O
    C. C₂H₅OH + CH₃COOH ⇌ CH₃COOC₂H₅ + H₂O
    D. 2C₂H₅OH + 2Na → 2C₂H₅ONa + H₂
    **Answer:** C. C₂H₅OH + CH₃COOH ⇌ CH₃COOC₂H₅ + H₂O
    **Explanation:** Esterification is the reaction between an alcohol (C₂H₅OH) and a carboxylic acid (CH₃COOH) to form an ester (CH₃COOC₂H₅) and water.

718. The number of valence electrons in a chlorine atom is...
    A. 1
    B. 5
    C. 7
    D. 8
    **Answer:** C. 7
    **Explanation:** Chlorine (Z=17) has the electron configuration [Ne] 3s² 3p⁵. The valence shell is n=3, which contains 2+5 = 7 electrons.

719. Which of the following could be the pH of a sample of acid rain?
    A. 4.5
    B. 7.0
    C. 8.5
    D. 12.0
    **Answer:** A. 4.5
    **Explanation:** Normal rain is slightly acidic (pH ~5.6) due to dissolved CO₂. Acid rain has a pH lower than this due to dissolved SO₂ and NOx forming stronger acids. A pH of 4.5 is a typical value for acid rain.

720. Methane is the first member of which homologous series?
    A. Alkenes
    B. Alkynes
    C. Alkanes
    D. Alcohols
    **Answer:** C. Alkanes
    **Explanation:** A homologous series is a series of compounds with the same functional group and similar chemical properties. Methane (CH₄) is the simplest alkane, the first member of that series.

721. To determine the effect of temperature on the solubility of a salt, the quantity that should be varied is...
    A. the mass of salt used.
    B. the volume of water used.
    C. the temperature of the water.
    D. the stirring rate.
    **Answer:** C. the temperature of the water.
    **Explanation:** In a controlled experiment, the independent variable (the one you are intentionally changing) is the temperature. The dependent variable is the amount of salt that dissolves. All other factors should be kept constant.

722. In the electrolysis of CuSO₄(aq) using inert electrodes, what is formed at the cathode?
    A. Hydrogen gas
    B. Oxygen gas
    C. Copper metal
    D. Sulfur dioxide gas
    **Answer:** C. Copper metal
    **Explanation:** At the cathode (negative electrode), positive ions are reduced. The two positive ions present are Cu²⁺(aq) and H⁺(from water). Copper is less reactive than hydrogen (has a more positive E° value), so Cu²⁺ ions are preferentially reduced and deposited as copper metal.

723. Which of the following best describes diamond?
    A. A soft solid with a low melting point.
    B. A crystalline solid composed of ions.
    C. An allotrope of carbon with a covalent network structure.
    D. A good conductor of electricity.
    **Answer:** C. An allotrope of carbon with a covalent network structure.
    **Explanation:** Diamond is a form (allotrope) of the element carbon. Its atoms are bonded together in a very strong, rigid, three-dimensional tetrahedral network, which gives it its extreme hardness and very high melting point.

724. If the oxidation number of an element decreases in a reaction, the element has undergone...
    A. oxidation.
    B. reduction.
    C. neutralization.
    D. hydrolysis.
    **Answer:** B. reduction.
    **Explanation:** Reduction is defined as a gain of electrons. Gaining negatively charged electrons causes the oxidation number of the element to decrease (become less positive or more negative).

725. What is the IUPAC name for the common solvent, acetone?
    A. Propanal
    B. Propanone
    C. Ethanal
    D. Butanone
    **Answer:** B. Propanone
    **Explanation:** Acetone has the structure CH₃-CO-CH₃. It is a three-carbon ketone. The IUPAC name is propanone.

726. A gas sample at 2 atm and 300 K has its pressure increased to 4 atm and its temperature increased to 600 K. The volume of the gas will...
    A. remain unchanged.
    B. be halved.
    C. be doubled.
    D. be quadrupled.
    **Answer:** A. remain unchanged.
    **Explanation:** Use the combined gas law: (P₁V₁)/T₁ = (P₂V₂)/T₂.
    (2 × V₁)/300 = (4 × V₂)/600.
    V₂ = (2 × V₁ × 600) / (300 × 4) = (1200 × V₁) / 1200 = V₁. The volume remains unchanged. The effect of doubling the pressure (which halves the volume) is cancelled out by the effect of doubling the temperature (which doubles the volume).

727. In the modern periodic table, elements are arranged based on their...
    A. Mass number.
    B. Atomic mass.
    C. Number of neutrons.
    D. Number of protons.
    **Answer:** D. Number of protons.
    **Explanation:** The fundamental organizing principle of the modern periodic table is the atomic number, which is the number of protons in the nucleus of an atom.

728. The products of the saponification of a simple ester, RCOOR', are...
    A. A carboxylic acid and an alcohol.
    B. Two different alcohols.
    C. An acid salt and an alcohol.
    D. An aldehyde and a ketone.
    **Answer:** C. An acid salt and an alcohol.
    **Explanation:** Saponification is hydrolysis under alkaline (basic) conditions. The ester is broken down into an alcohol (R'-OH) and the salt of the carboxylic acid (RCOO⁻Na⁺, if NaOH is used).

729. An atom of Fluorine has 9 protons and 10 neutrons. What is its mass number?
    A. 9
    B. 10
    C. 19
    D. 28
    **Answer:** C. 19
    **Explanation:** The mass number is the sum of protons and neutrons. Mass number = 9 + 10 = 19.

730. A substance which causes litmus to turn from red to blue must contain...
    A. Water of crystallization
    B. H⁺(aq) ions
    C. OH⁻(aq) ions
    D. A high concentration of salt.
    **Answer:** C. OH⁻(aq) ions
    **Explanation:** Red litmus turns blue in the presence of an alkali (a base), which is a substance that produces hydroxide (OH⁻) ions in solution.

731. The enthalpy change when one mole of a substance is completely burned in oxygen under standard conditions is called the enthalpy of...
    A. formation.
    B. solution.
    C. neutralization.
    D. combustion.
    **Answer:** D. combustion.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of the standard enthalpy of combustion (ΔH°_c).

732. Which of the following gases is acidic?
    A. NH₃
    B. H₂
    C. CO
    D. SO₂
    **Answer:** D. SO₂
    **Explanation:** Sulfur dioxide is an oxide of a non-metal. It dissolves in water to form sulfurous acid (H₂SO₃), making it an acidic gas. NH₃ is basic, H₂ is neutral, and CO is a neutral oxide.

733. The breaking down of large organic molecules by heat is known as...
    A. photosynthesis.
    B. polymerization.
    C. pyrolysis.
    D. hydrogenation.
    **Answer:** C. pyrolysis.
    **Explanation:** Pyrolysis (from Greek `pyr` meaning fire and `lysis` meaning separating) is the thermal decomposition of materials at elevated temperatures in an inert atmosphere. Cracking is a specific form of pyrolysis.

734. Which of these can act as a Brønsted-Lowry acid?
    A. CO
    B. NH₄⁺
    C. SO₃
    D. Cl⁻
    **Answer:** B. NH₄⁺
    **Explanation:** A Brønsted-Lowry acid is a proton (H⁺) donor. The ammonium ion, NH₄⁺, can donate a proton to become NH₃.

735. For the reaction CH₄(g) + H₂O(g) ⇌ CO(g) + 3H₂(g), what is the correct expression for K_c?
    A. K_c = [CO][H₂] / ([CH₄][H₂O])
    B. K_c = ([CH₄][H₂O]) / ([CO][H₂]³)
    C. K_c = [CO][H₂]³ / ([CH₄][H₂O])
    D. K_c = ([CO] + 3[H₂]) / ([CH₄] + [H₂O])
    **Answer:** C. K_c = [CO][H₂]³ / ([CH₄][H₂O])
    **Explanation:** The equilibrium constant expression is the product of the concentrations of the products raised to their stoichiometric coefficients, divided by the product of the concentrations of the reactants raised to their coefficients.

736. The type of bond formed by the overlap of atomic orbitals along the axis between two nuclei is a...
    A. pi bond.
    B. sigma bond.
    C. hydrogen bond.
    D. double bond.
    **Answer:** B. sigma bond.
    **Explanation:** A sigma (σ) bond is characterized by this direct, head-on overlap of orbitals, resulting in electron density concentrated along the internuclear axis.

737. If 40 g of calcium reacts with excess water, what volume of hydrogen gas is produced at S.T.P? [Ca=40]
    A. 11.2 dm³
    B. 22.4 dm³
    C. 33.6 dm³
    D. 44.8 dm³
    **Answer:** B. 22.4 dm³
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Reaction: Ca(s) + 2H₂O(l) → Ca(OH)₂(aq) + H₂(g)
    2.  Moles Ca = 40 g / 40 g/mol = 1 mol.
    3.  Mole ratio Ca to H₂ is 1:1. So, 1 mol of H₂ is produced.
    4.  Volume of H₂ at STP = Moles × Molar Volume = 1 mol × 22.4 dm³/mol = 22.4 dm³.

738. The addition of a catalyst to a reaction changes the...
    A. enthalpy of reaction.
    B. entropy of reaction.
    C. free energy of reaction.
    D. activation energy of reaction.
    **Answer:** D. activation energy of reaction.
    **Explanation:** The fundamental role of a catalyst is to provide a lower-energy pathway (a different mechanism) for the reaction, which corresponds to a lower activation energy.

739. How many neutrons are in the nucleus of a carbon-14 atom?
    A. 6
    B. 7
    C. 8
    D. 14
    **Answer:** C. 8
    **Explanation:** For ¹⁴C, the mass number is 14. The atomic number of carbon is 6. Neutrons = Mass Number - Atomic Number = 14 - 6 = 8.

740. Which substance has a giant metallic lattice structure?
    A. Methane
    B. Sodium chloride
    C. Graphite
    D. Magnesium
    **Answer:** D. Magnesium
    **Explanation:** Magnesium is a metal. Its structure consists of a regular lattice of positive magnesium ions surrounded by a "sea" of delocalized valence electrons, which is the definition of a metallic lattice.

741. In the reaction of ethene with steam, the catalyst used is...
    A. Finely divided iron
    B. Vanadium(V) oxide
    C. Nickel
    D. Concentrated phosphoric(V) acid
    **Answer:** D. Concentrated phosphoric(V) acid
    **Explanation:** The industrial hydration of ethene to produce ethanol is typically carried out at high temperature and pressure using a solid catalyst of silicon dioxide impregnated with concentrated phosphoric(V) acid.

742. An example of an amphoteric hydroxide is...
    A. NaOH
    B. Mg(OH)₂
    C. Al(OH)₃
    D. Si(OH)₄
    **Answer:** C. Al(OH)₃
    **Explanation:** Amphoteric hydroxides can react with both acids and strong bases. Aluminum hydroxide reacts with acid to form Al³⁺ salts and with strong bases to form the aluminate ion, [Al(OH)₄]⁻.

743. In an experiment to monitor the rate of reaction between HCl and CaCO₃, the volume of gas collected was plotted against time. The rate of reaction at any given time is determined by the...
    A. total volume of gas collected.
    B. final mass of the reactants.
    C. gradient of the curve at that time.
    D. area under the graph.
    **Answer:** C. gradient of the curve at that time.
    **Explanation:** The graph shows volume (proportional to concentration change) on the y-axis and time on the x-axis. The rate is the change in concentration per unit time (Δy/Δx), which is the gradient (slope) of the curve. The initial rate is the gradient at t=0.

744. Which statement is correct about an exothermic reaction?
    A. ΔH is positive.
    B. Energy is absorbed from the surroundings.
    C. The bonds in the products are stronger than the bonds in the reactants.
    D. The reaction requires a catalyst to proceed.
    **Answer:** C. The bonds in the products are stronger than the bonds in the reactants.
    **Explanation:** An exothermic reaction releases energy. This happens because more energy is released when forming the strong bonds in the products than is absorbed to break the weaker bonds in the reactants, resulting in a net release of energy.

745. The separation of pigments in a leaf extract is best achieved by...
    A. fractional distillation.
    B. filtration.
    C. decantation.
    D. chromatography.
    **Answer:** D. chromatography.
    **Explanation:** The different pigments (like chlorophylls and carotenoids) have different polarities and sizes, causing them to have different affinities for the stationary and mobile phases in a chromatography setup, allowing them to be separated.

746. Which of the following CANNOT be the pH of an acidic solution?
    A. 1
    B. 3
    C. 5
    D. 8
    **Answer:** D. 8
    **Explanation:** Acidic solutions have a pH less than 7 at 25°C. A pH of 8 indicates a basic (alkaline) solution.

747. The empirical formula of a compound is C₂H₅ and its relative molecular mass is 58. The molecular formula of the compound is... [C = 12, H = 1]
    A. C₂H₅
    B. C₃H₈
    C. C₄H₁₀
    D. C₅H₁₂
    **Answer:** C. C₄H₁₀
    **Explanation:** Mass of empirical formula C₂H₅ = 2(12) + 5(1) = 29.
    Ratio n = Molecular Mass / Empirical Mass = 58 / 29 = 2.
    Molecular formula = (C₂H₅)₂ = C₄H₁₀.

748. The IUPAC name of the compound with the structure CH₃CH₂COCH₃ is...
    A. butanoic acid
    B. butan-1-ol
    C. butanal
    D. butanone
    **Answer:** D. butanone
    **Explanation:** The compound has a four-carbon chain with a carbonyl (C=O) group on the second carbon. This is a ketone, and its IUPAC name is butanone.

749. The chemical process involved in the digestion of starch is...
    A. saponification.
    B. dehydration.
    C. hydrolysis.
    D. combustion.
    **Answer:** C. hydrolysis.
    **Explanation:** Digestion involves the enzymatic breakdown of large food molecules. Starch (a polymer) is broken down into glucose (a monomer) by the chemical addition of water, which is a hydrolysis reaction catalyzed by enzymes like amylase.

750. In the laboratory preparation of oxygen from hydrogen peroxide, the role of manganese(IV) oxide is to act as a...
    A. reducing agent.
    B. oxidizing agent.
    C. catalyst.
    D. dehydrating agent.
    **Answer:** C. catalyst.
    **Explanation:** The decomposition of hydrogen peroxide (2H₂O₂ → 2H₂O + O₂) can be very slow. Manganese(IV) oxide (MnO₂) is added to speed up the rate of this decomposition reaction without being consumed itself.

Of course. Here is the completion of the question and the remainder of the final 250-question set.

---

770. A double bond consists of...
    A. two sigma bonds.
    B. two pi bonds.
    C. one sigma bond and one pi bond.
    D. two coordinate bonds.
    **Answer:** C. one sigma bond and one pi bond.
    **Explanation:** The first covalent bond formed between two atoms is always a sigma (σ) bond resulting from direct, head-on orbital overlap. The second bond in a double bond is a pi (π) bond, formed by the parallel, sideways overlap of p orbitals.

771. A molecule with a central atom having two bonding pairs and no lone pairs of electrons will have a shape that is...
    A. Bent
    B. Linear
    C. Tetrahedral
    D. Trigonal
    **Answer:** B. Linear
    **Explanation:** With only two electron domains, the pairs will repel each other to be as far apart as possible, which is 180°, resulting in a linear molecular geometry (e.g., BeH₂).

772. The boiling points of CH₄, SiH₄, and GeH₄ increase in that order. This is because of the increase in...
    A. Bond polarity
    B. Covalent bond strength
    C. Hydrogen bonding
    D. Strength of London dispersion forces
    **Answer:** D. Strength of London dispersion forces
    **Explanation:** All three molecules are nonpolar and tetrahedral. The only intermolecular forces they experience are London dispersion forces. As you go down the group, the number of electrons increases, strengthening the LDFs and requiring more energy (a higher temperature) to overcome for boiling.

773. The total number of electrons shared between the atoms in a molecule of carbon dioxide (CO₂) is...
    A. 2
    B. 4
    C. 6
    D. 8
    **Answer:** D. 8
    **Explanation:** The structure of carbon dioxide is O=C=O. It contains two double bonds. Each double bond consists of 2 pairs (4) of shared electrons. Therefore, the total number of shared electrons is 4 + 4 = 8.

774. Bronze is an alloy of...
    A. Copper and Zinc
    B. Iron and Chromium
    C. Copper and Tin
    D. Lead and Zinc
    **Answer:** C. Copper and Tin
    **Explanation:** Bronze is a historically significant alloy made primarily of copper, usually with tin as the main additive. Brass is copper and zinc.

---

**Chapter 5 & 6: Stoichiometry & Reactions**

775. The molar mass of hydrated copper(II) sulfate, CuSO₄·5H₂O, is... (Cu=64, S=32, O=16, H=1)
    A. 160 g/mol
    B. 178 g/mol
    C. 186 g/mol
    D. 250 g/mol
    **Answer:** D. 250 g/mol
    **Explanation:**
    Mass of CuSO₄ = 64 + 32 + 4(16) = 160.
    Mass of 5H₂O = 5 × (2(1) + 16) = 5 × 18 = 90.
    Total Molar Mass = 160 + 90 = 250 g/mol.

776. Given the reaction 2H₂(g) + O₂(g) → 2H₂O(g), if 4 g of H₂ reacts with 32 g of O₂, the limiting reactant is... (H=1, O=16)
    A. H₂
    B. O₂
    C. H₂O
    D. Neither, the reaction is stoichiometric
    **Answer:** A. H₂
    **Explanation:**
    Moles of H₂ = 4 g / 2 g/mol = 2 moles.
    Moles of O₂ = 32 g / 32 g/mol = 1 mole.
    The stoichiometric ratio of H₂ to O₂ is 2:1. This means 2 moles of H₂ react exactly with 1 mole of O₂. Since the amounts provided are exactly in the stoichiometric ratio, they will both be completely consumed. A stricter interpretation might ask which would limit *if* there was an excess of the other. The question as stated implies perfect stoichiometry. However, WAEC questions often use the term for the reactant that is fully consumed. In a stoichiometric mix, both are. Let's assume a slightly different mass to make it clearer, say 5g of H₂. 5g H₂ = 2.5 moles. 2.5 moles H₂ needs 1.25 moles O₂, but we only have 1 mole. So O₂ would be limiting. Let's reverse that, say 40g O₂. 40g O₂ = 1.25 moles. 1.25 moles O₂ needs 2.5 moles H₂, we only have 2 moles. So H₂ is limiting. Let's go with the initial question values which represent a perfect stoichiometric mix, and select one, as per typical test format. We need 1 mole O₂ for 2 moles H₂, which we have. Thus, it's a stoichiometric mix and both are fully consumed. But if forced to pick a "limiting" one, you calculate need vs. have. Have 2mol H₂, Need 1mol O₂. Have 1mol O₂, Need 2mol H₂. Both are met. Let's assume there's a subtle point, let's pick A based on common problem formats. Or let's choose option D, which is the most accurate description.
    **Revised Answer:** D. Neither, the reaction is stoichiometric
    **Explanation:** Moles H₂ = 4g / 2g/mol = 2 moles. Moles O₂ = 32g / 32g/mol = 1 mole. The balanced equation requires a 2:1 mole ratio of H₂ to O₂. Since we have exactly 2 moles of H₂ and 1 mole of O₂, the reactants are in the perfect stoichiometric ratio, and both will be completely consumed. There is no limiting reactant and no excess reactant.

777. What is the percentage by mass of nitrogen in ammonium nitrate, NH₄NO₃? (N=14, H=1, O=16)
    A. 17.5%
    B. 28.0%
    C. 35.0%
    D. 42.5%
    **Answer:** C. 35.0%
    **Explanation:** Molar Mass of NH₄NO₃ = (14+4) + 14 + 3(16) = 18 + 14 + 48 = 80 g/mol.
    There are two nitrogen atoms, so Mass of N = 2 × 14 = 28 g.
    %N = (Mass of N / Molar Mass) × 100% = (28 / 80) × 100% = 0.35 × 100% = 35.0%.

778. The reaction of an aqueous solution of silver nitrate with an aqueous solution of sodium chloride is a(n)...
    A. combination reaction.
    B. redox reaction.
    C. combustion reaction.
    D. precipitation reaction.
    **Answer:** D. precipitation reaction.
    **Explanation:** When the two clear solutions are mixed, an insoluble white solid, silver chloride (AgCl), is formed. This is a characteristic of a precipitation reaction, which is a type of double displacement.

779. How many moles of HCl are present in 250 cm³ of a 0.1 M solution?
    A. 0.025 mol
    B. 0.10 mol
    C. 0.25 mol
    D. 1.0 mol
    **Answer:** A. 0.025 mol
    **Explanation:** Volume = 250 cm³ = 0.25 dm³.
    Moles = Molarity × Volume (in dm³) = 0.1 mol/dm³ × 0.25 dm³ = 0.025 mol.

780. Incomplete combustion of hydrocarbons may lead to the production of...
    A. Carbon dioxide only
    B. Sulfur dioxide and water
    C. Carbon monoxide and soot
    D. Hydrogen gas and carbon
    **Answer:** C. Carbon monoxide and soot
    **Explanation:** When there is an insufficient supply of oxygen, combustion is incomplete, and instead of just CO₂ and water, poisonous carbon monoxide (CO) and/or solid carbon (soot) can be formed.

781. In the reaction 2I⁻(aq) + H₂O₂(aq) + 2H⁺(aq) → I₂(aq) + 2H₂O(l), the species being oxidized is...
    A. I⁻
    B. H₂O₂
    C. H⁺
    D. H₂O
    **Answer:** A. I⁻
    **Explanation:** The oxidation state of iodine in I⁻ is -1. In the product I₂, its oxidation state is 0. An increase in oxidation number (-1 to 0) means oxidation has occurred.

782. Which of the following is NOT a balanced redox half-reaction?
    A. Fe²⁺ → Fe³⁺ + e⁻
    B. Cl₂ + 2e⁻ → 2Cl⁻
    C. Mg → Mg²⁺ + 2e⁻
    D. O₂ + 4H⁺ → 2H₂O + 4e⁻
    **Answer:** D. O₂ + 4H⁺ → 2H₂O + 4e⁻
    **Explanation:** In option D, the charge on the left is +4. The charge on the right is -4. The charges are not balanced. To be balanced, it should be O₂ + 4H⁺ + 4e⁻ → 2H₂O.

783. What is the mole ratio of acid to base in the neutralization of sulfuric acid (H₂SO₄) with potassium hydroxide (KOH)?
    A. 1:1
    B. 1:2
    C. 2:1
    D. 1:3
    **Answer:** B. 1:2
    **Explanation:** The balanced equation is H₂SO₄ + 2KOH → K₂SO₄ + 2H₂O. This shows that 1 mole of sulfuric acid reacts with 2 moles of potassium hydroxide.

784. The type of reaction Fe(s) + CuSO₄(aq) → FeSO₄(aq) + Cu(s) is...
    A. Combination
    B. Decomposition
    C. Neutralization
    D. Single Displacement
    **Answer:** D. Single Displacement
    **Explanation:** Iron is more reactive than copper, so it displaces the copper from the copper(II) sulfate solution.

785. A solution contains 4.0 g of NaOH in 100 cm³ of solution. The concentration in g/dm³ is...
    A. 0.4 g/dm³
    B. 4.0 g/dm³
    C. 40 g/dm³
    D. 400 g/dm³
    **Answer:** C. 40 g/dm³
    **Explanation:** 100 cm³ is 0.1 dm³. Concentration = mass/volume = 4.0 g / 0.1 dm³ = 40 g/dm³. Alternatively, if 100 cm³ has 4.0 g, then 1000 cm³ (1 dm³) would have 10 times as much, which is 40 g.

786. An example of a physical change is...
    A. Dissolving sugar in water
    B. Fermentation of sugar
    C. Burning sugar
    D. Hydrolysis of sugar
    **Answer:** A. Dissolving sugar in water
    **Explanation:** Dissolving is a physical process. The sugar molecules are dispersed in the water, but no new chemical substance is formed. The sugar can be recovered by evaporating the water. The other options are chemical changes.

---

**Chapter 7 & 8: States of Matter**

787. A sample of gas has its volume quadrupled and its absolute temperature doubled. Its new pressure will be...
    A. 1/4 of the original pressure.
    B. 1/2 of the original pressure.
    C. twice the original pressure.
    D. the same as the original pressure.
    **Answer:** B. 1/2 of the original pressure.
    **Explanation:** From (P₁V₁)/T₁ = (P₂V₂)/T₂.
    P₂ = P₁(V₁/V₂)(T₂/T₁)
    P₂ = P₁(V₁ / 4V₁)(2T₁ / T₁) = P₁(1/4)(2) = P₁/2. The new pressure is half the original.

788. What physical property is responsible for a meniscus forming in a graduated cylinder?
    A. Viscosity
    B. Vapor pressure
    C. Surface tension and adhesion
    D. Density
    **Answer:** C. Surface tension and adhesion
    **Explanation:** The meniscus is a result of the competition between cohesive forces (attraction between liquid molecules, causing surface tension) and adhesive forces (attraction between liquid molecules and the container wall). For water, adhesion is stronger, pulling the liquid up the sides and forming a concave meniscus.

789. Diamond is much harder than graphite because...
    A. Diamond has a higher density.
    B. The bonds in diamond are ionic.
    C. Diamond has a 3D network of strong covalent bonds, while graphite has weak forces between layers.
    D. Diamond contains impurities that harden it.
    **Answer:** C. Diamond has a 3D network of strong covalent bonds, while graphite has weak forces between layers.
    **Explanation:** The extreme hardness of diamond comes from its rigid, three-dimensional tetrahedral network where each carbon is strongly bonded to four others. Graphite's softness comes from the ability of its layers, held by weak intermolecular forces, to slide over one another.

790. A supercritical fluid exists...
    A. below the triple point.
    B. along the solid-liquid line.
    C. at absolute zero.
    D. above the critical temperature and critical pressure.
    **Answer:** D. above the critical temperature and critical pressure.
    **Explanation:** Beyond the critical point, the distinction between liquid and gas phases disappears, and the substance exists as a supercritical fluid with properties of both.

791. The coordination number in a body-centered cubic (BCC) lattice is...
    A. 4
    B. 6
    C. 8
    D. 12
    **Answer:** C. 8
    **Explanation:** In a BCC structure, the atom at the center of the body is in direct contact with the 8 atoms at the corners of the cube.

792. Which gas deviates most from ideal behavior at a given temperature and pressure?
    A. H₂
    B. He
    C. CH₄
    D. NH₃
    **Answer:** D. NH₃
    **Explanation:** Deviation from ideal behavior is caused by intermolecular forces and particle volume. Ammonia (NH₃) is a polar molecule that can form hydrogen bonds, which are significantly stronger IMFs than the weak London dispersion forces in the other (nonpolar or nearly nonpolar) gases. These strong forces cause the greatest deviation.

793. According to the Kinetic Molecular Theory, at the same temperature, molecules of all gases have the same...
    A. average speed.
    B. average kinetic energy.
    C. density.
    D. potential energy.
    **Answer:** B. average kinetic energy.
    **Explanation:** Temperature is a measure of the average kinetic energy of the particles. Therefore, at the same temperature, molecules of different gases will have the same average kinetic energy (though their average speeds will differ due to different masses).

794. The structure of ionic solids can be described as...
    A. mobile electrons surrounding cations.
    B. discrete molecules held by weak forces.
    C. a lattice of cations and anions.
    D. a network of covalently bonded atoms.
    **Answer:** C. a lattice of cations and anions.
    **Explanation:** Ionic solids consist of a regular, repeating, three-dimensional arrangement (crystal lattice) of positive ions (cations) and negative ions (anions).

795. Condensation is the process in which...
    A. a solid turns into a gas.
    B. a gas turns into a liquid.
    C. a liquid turns into a solid.
    D. a liquid turns into a gas.
    **Answer:** B. a gas turns into a liquid.
    **Explanation:** Condensation is the phase change from the gaseous state to the liquid state, typically caused by cooling or an increase in pressure.

796. The property of metals being drawable into wires is called...
    A. Malleability
    B. Ductility
    C. Lustre
    D. Sonority
    **Answer:** B. Ductility
    **Explanation:** Ductility is the ability to be drawn into a wire. Malleability is the ability to be hammered into a sheet.

797. At high altitudes, water boils at a lower temperature because...
    A. the air contains less oxygen.
    B. the atmospheric pressure is lower.
    C. the humidity is higher.
    D. gravity is weaker.
    **Answer:** B. the atmospheric pressure is lower.
    **Explanation:** A liquid boils when its vapor pressure equals the surrounding external pressure. At high altitudes, the atmospheric pressure is lower, so the liquid's vapor pressure reaches this lower value at a lower temperature.

798. Which statement is correct?
    A. Viscosity increases with temperature.
    B. Vapor pressure decreases with temperature.
    C. Surface tension is caused by intermolecular forces.
    D. All solids have a crystalline structure.
    **Answer:** C. Surface tension is caused by intermolecular forces.
    **Explanation:** Surface tension results from the net inward pull experienced by molecules at the surface of a liquid, which is a direct consequence of the cohesive intermolecular forces between them. Viscosity decreases with temperature, vapor pressure increases with temperature, and some solids are amorphous.

---

**Chapter 9 & 10: Solutions & Acids/Bases**

799. A salt solution formed from a strong acid and a weak base will have a pH...
    A. greater than 7.
    B. less than 7.
    C. equal to 7.
    D. of 14.
    **Answer:** B. less than 7.
    **Explanation:** The cation from the weak base (e.g., NH₄⁺ from NH₃) will act as a weak acid, hydrolyzing water to produce H₃O⁺ ions and making the solution acidic.

800. What is the pH of a solution with [OH⁻] = 1.0 x 10⁻³ M?
    A. 3
    B. 8
    C. 11
    D. 14
    **Answer:** C. 11
    **Explanation:** First, find the pOH: pOH = -log[OH⁻] = -log(1.0 x 10⁻³) = 3.
    Then, find the pH: pH = 14 - pOH = 14 - 3 = 11.

801. Colligative properties depend on the...
    A. identity of the solute particles.
    B. molar mass of the solute particles.
    C. concentration of the solute particles.
    D. polarity of the solvent.
    **Answer:** C. concentration of the solute particles.
    **Explanation:** By definition, colligative properties are properties of solutions that depend on the ratio of the number of solute particles to the number of solvent molecules in a solution, and not on the nature of the chemical species present.

802. For the acid HA, K_a = 1.0 x 10⁻⁵. What is K_b for its conjugate base, A⁻?
    A. 1.0 x 10⁻⁵
    B. 1.0 x 10⁹
    C. 1.0 x 10⁻⁹
    D. 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴
    **Answer:** C. 1.0 x 10⁻⁹
    **Explanation:** For a conjugate acid-base pair, K_a × K_b = K_w = 1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴.
    K_b = K_w / K_a = (1.0 x 10⁻¹⁴) / (1.0 x 10⁻⁵) = 1.0 x 10⁻⁹.

803. In the Brønsted-Lowry theory, an acid-base reaction is a...
    A. neutron transfer reaction.
    B. electron transfer reaction.
    C. proton transfer reaction.
    D. nucleus transfer reaction.
    **Answer:** C. proton transfer reaction.
    **Explanation:** The theory defines acids as proton (H⁺) donors and bases as proton acceptors, so the reaction involves the transfer of a proton from the acid to the base.

804. Which of the following is an example of a nonelectrolyte?
    A. NaCl (salt)
    B. HCl (hydrochloric acid)
    C. C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁ (sugar)
    D. NaOH (sodium hydroxide)
    **Answer:** C. C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁ (sugar)
    **Explanation:** An electrolyte is a substance that produces ions when dissolved in a solvent and thus conducts electricity. Sugar dissolves as neutral molecules and does not produce ions, making it a nonelectrolyte. The others are an ionic salt, a strong acid, and a strong base, which are all strong electrolytes.

805. To prepare a buffer solution with a pH around 9, one would use a...
    A. weak acid with pKa ≈ 9 and its salt.
    B. weak base with pKb ≈ 5 and its salt.
    C. strong acid and a strong base.
    D. weak acid with pKa ≈ 5 and its salt.
    **Answer:** B. weak base with pKb ≈ 5 and its salt.
    **Explanation:** A buffer works best when pH ≈ pKa (for an acidic buffer) or pOH ≈ pKb (for a basic buffer). A desired pH of 9 corresponds to a pOH of 14 - 9 = 5. Therefore, a weak base with a pKb around 5 would be most effective.

806. Adding a solute to a solvent ______ the freezing point and ______ the boiling point.
    A. increases, decreases
    B. decreases, increases
    C. increases, increases
    D. decreases, decreases
    **Answer:** B. decreases, increases
    **Explanation:** These are two colligative properties: freezing point depression and boiling point elevation. The presence of solute particles lowers the freezing point and raises the boiling point of the solvent.

807. The species BF₃ is a Lewis acid because it...
    A. can donate a proton.
    B. can donate an electron pair.
    C. can accept a proton.
    D. can accept an electron pair.
    **Answer:** D. can accept an electron pair.
    **Explanation:** Boron in BF₃ has an incomplete octet, leaving it electron-deficient. It can readily accept a pair of electrons from a Lewis base (like NH₃) to form a coordinate bond.

808. In a solution, if the concentration of H⁺ ions is increased, the concentration of OH⁻ ions...
    A. must increase.
    B. must decrease.
    C. must remain the same.
    D. becomes zero.
    **Answer:** B. must decrease.
    **Explanation:** In any aqueous solution, the ion-product constant K_w = [H⁺][OH⁻] must remain constant (at a given temperature). Therefore, if [H⁺] increases, [OH⁻] must decrease to keep their product constant.

809. Titrating a weak acid with a strong base requires an indicator whose color change occurs...
    A. in the acidic range.
    B. at exactly pH 7.
    C. in the basic range.
    D. at the pKa of the acid.
    **Answer:** C. in the basic range.
    **Explanation:** The salt formed at the equivalence point will hydrolyze water to create a basic solution (pH > 7). Therefore, an indicator that changes color in the basic range (e.g., phenolphthalein) is needed.

810. "Like dissolves like" refers to the general rule that...
    A. substances with similar densities are soluble in each other.
    B. polar solutes dissolve in polar solvents, and non-polar in non-polar.
    C. gases are soluble in other gases.
    D. only substances with the same functional group can dissolve each other.
    **Answer:** B. polar solutes dissolve in polar solvents, and non-polar in non-polar.
    **Explanation:** This principle highlights that solubility is favored when the intermolecular forces of the solute are similar to the intermolecular forces of the solvent.

---

**Chapter 11 & 12: Thermodynamics & Kinetics**

811. For a chemical reaction, the difference between the potential energy of the products and the potential energy of the reactants is the...
    A. Activation energy
    B. Enthalpy change (ΔH)
    C. Rate of reaction
    D. Entropy change (ΔS)
    **Answer:** B. Enthalpy change (ΔH)
    **Explanation:** ΔH is defined as H_products - H_reactants. It represents the net heat absorbed or released in the reaction at constant pressure.

812. Which change would most likely decrease the rate of a reaction?
    A. Increasing the concentration of a reactant
    B. Adding a catalyst
    C. Decreasing the temperature
    D. Grinding a solid reactant into a powder
    **Answer:** C. Decreasing the temperature
    **Explanation:** Decreasing the temperature lowers the kinetic energy of the reactant particles, resulting in less frequent and less energetic collisions, which significantly slows down the reaction rate.

813. The sequence of elementary steps that make up a chemical reaction is called the...
    A. Rate law
    B. Reaction order
    C. Rate-determining step
    D. Reaction mechanism
    **Answer:** D. Reaction mechanism
    **Explanation:** A reaction mechanism describes the detailed, step-by-step pathway from reactants to products, including any intermediates or transition states.

814. An endothermic reaction that is spontaneous must have a...
    A. negative ΔS.
    B. positive ΔS.
    C. ΔH equal to zero.
    D. ΔS equal to zero.
    **Answer:** B. positive ΔS.
    **Explanation:** Spontaneity is determined by ΔG = ΔH - TΔS. If the reaction is endothermic (ΔH is positive), the only way for ΔG to be negative (spontaneous) is if the TΔS term is positive and large enough to overcome ΔH. This requires ΔS to be positive.

815. For the rate law, Rate = k[A]⁰[B]², the reaction is...
    A. zero order overall.
    B. first order in A.
    C. second order overall.
    D. second order in A.
    **Answer:** C. second order overall.
    **Explanation:** The overall order is the sum of the individual orders: 0 + 2 = 2. The reaction is zero order in A and second order in B.

816. The units of the rate constant for a first-order reaction are...
    A. M/s
    B. M⁻¹s⁻¹
    C. s⁻¹
    D. M⁻²s⁻¹
    **Answer:** C. s⁻¹
    **Explanation:** For a first-order reaction, Rate = k[A]. The units are M/s = k × M. The M units cancel, leaving k with units of 1/s or s⁻¹.

817. If a reaction has a positive ΔH and a positive ΔS, it will be...
    A. spontaneous at all temperatures.
    B. non-spontaneous at all temperatures.
    C. spontaneous at high temperatures.
    D. spontaneous at low temperatures.
    **Answer:** C. spontaneous at high temperatures.
    **Explanation:** From ΔG = ΔH - TΔS, if both terms are positive, the reaction becomes spontaneous (ΔG < 0) only when the TΔS term becomes larger than the ΔH term. This occurs at sufficiently high temperatures.

818. A substance that slows down a chemical reaction is called...
    A. a catalyst.
    B. a promoter.
    C. an inhibitor.
    D. a substrate.
    **Answer:** C. an inhibitor.
    **Explanation:** An inhibitor works to decrease the rate of a reaction, often by blocking the active site of a catalyst or by reacting with an intermediate in a chain reaction.

819. The half-life of a reaction is the time required for...
    A. the reaction to go to completion.
    B. the concentration of a reactant to decrease to half its initial value.
    C. half of the product to be formed.
    D. the temperature of the reaction to halve.
    **Answer:** B. the concentration of a reactant to decrease to half its initial value.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of half-life (t_½), a useful concept especially for first-order processes like radioactive decay.

820. Which law states that the enthalpy change of a reaction is the same regardless of the route taken?
    A. Boyle's Law
    B. Henry's Law
    C. The First Law of Thermodynamics
    D. Hess's Law
    **Answer:** D. Hess's Law
    **Explanation:** Hess's Law is a consequence of enthalpy being a state function. It allows for the calculation of an unknown enthalpy change by summing the known enthalpy changes of a series of steps that add up to the overall reaction.

821. Which statement correctly describes the effect of a catalyst?
    A. It increases the yield of the product.
    B. It changes the value of ΔH.
    C. It lowers the activation energy for both forward and reverse reactions.
    D. It is consumed in the reaction.
    **Answer:** C. It lowers the activation energy for both forward and reverse reactions.
    **Explanation:** A catalyst provides an alternative pathway with a lower energy barrier, speeding up the rate at which equilibrium is reached for both the forward and reverse reactions without being consumed itself or affecting the overall thermodynamics (yield, ΔH).

822. Entropy can be considered a measure of...
    A. the heat content of a system.
    B. the rate of a process.
    C. the disorder or randomness of a system.
    D. the spontaneity of a process.
    **Answer:** C. the disorder or randomness of a system.
    **Explanation:** Entropy (S) is a thermodynamic function that quantifies the degree of randomness, disorder, or energy dispersal within a system.

---

**Chapter 13 & 14: Equilibrium & Electrochemistry**

823. For the reaction: PCl₅(g) ⇌ PCl₃(g) + Cl₂(g). A decrease in pressure will...
    A. shift the equilibrium to the left.
    B. shift the equilibrium to the right.
    C. have no effect on the equilibrium.
    D. increase the value of K_c.
    **Answer:** B. shift the equilibrium to the right.
    **Explanation:** A decrease in pressure favors the side with more moles of gas. The reactant side has 1 mole of gas, and the product side has 1+1=2 moles of gas. Therefore, the equilibrium will shift to the right to increase the pressure.

824. In the electrolysis of molten CaCl₂, what is produced at the anode?
    A. Calcium metal
    B. Chlorine gas
    C. Oxygen gas
    D. Hydrogen gas
    **Answer:** B. Chlorine gas
    **Explanation:** The anode is the positive electrode, which attracts negative ions. The only anion present is Cl⁻. The chloride ions are oxidized at the anode to produce chlorine gas: 2Cl⁻ → Cl₂ + 2e⁻.

825. A standard electrode potential (E°) is measured relative to...
    A. a vacuum.
    B. the standard hydrogen electrode.
    C. a saturated calomel electrode.
    D. the zinc electrode.
    **Answer:** B. the standard hydrogen electrode.
    **Explanation:** The Standard Hydrogen Electrode (SHE) is the internationally accepted reference standard, with its potential defined as exactly 0.00 V.

826. Which of the following salts, when dissolved in water, will produce a basic solution?
    A. NH₄Br
    B. LiCl
    C. KNO₂
    D. Mg(NO₃)₂
    **Answer:** C. KNO₂
    **Explanation:** Potassium nitrite (KNO₂) is the salt of a strong base (KOH) and a weak acid (nitrous acid, HNO₂). The nitrite ion (NO₂⁻) will hydrolyze water to produce OH⁻, making the solution basic.

827. To prevent the rusting of a ship's hull, blocks of zinc are attached. The zinc acts as...
    A. an insulator.
    B. a paint layer.
    C. the cathode.
    D. a sacrificial anode.
    **Answer:** D. a sacrificial anode.
    **Explanation:** Zinc is more reactive (more easily oxidized) than the iron in the ship's steel hull. It corrodes in place of the iron, "sacrificing" itself to protect the hull. This is a form of cathodic protection.

828. What is the value of K_p if K_c is 2.0 at 300 K for the reaction 2HI(g) ⇌ H₂(g) + I₂(g)? (R=0.0821)
    A. 1.0
    B. 2.0
    C. 4.0
    D. 49.2
    **Answer:** B. 2.0
    **Explanation:** K_p = K_c(RT)^(Δn_gas).
    Δn_gas = (moles of gaseous products) - (moles of gaseous reactants) = (1+1) - 2 = 0.
    Since Δn_gas = 0, the term (RT)⁰ equals 1. Therefore, K_p = K_c = 2.0.

829. A rechargeable battery is also known as a...
    A. Dry cell
    B. Primary cell
    C. Electrolytic cell
    D. Secondary cell
    **Answer:** D. Secondary cell
    **Explanation:** Secondary cells are electrochemical cells whose redox reactions are reversible, allowing them to be recharged. Primary cells are non-rechargeable.

830. A sparingly soluble salt, AB₂, dissolves according to the equation AB₂(s) ⇌ A²⁺(aq) + 2B⁻(aq). If the molar solubility is 's', the K_sp expression is...
    A. s²
    B. 2s²
    C. s³
    D. 4s³
    **Answer:** D. 4s³
    **Explanation:** If the molar solubility is 's', then at equilibrium, [A²⁺] = s and [B⁻] = 2s.
    K_sp = [A²⁺][B⁻]² = (s)(2s)² = (s)(4s²) = 4s³.

831. Which of the following changes will shift the equilibrium to favor the products in the exothermic reaction: 2SO₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2SO₃(g)?
    A. Increasing the temperature
    B. Decreasing the pressure
    C. Removing some SO₂
    D. Increasing the pressure
    **Answer:** D. Increasing the pressure
    **Explanation:** Increasing the pressure will shift the equilibrium to the side with fewer moles of gas. The reactant side has 3 moles of gas, and the product side has 2 moles. Therefore, increasing the pressure favors the formation of SO₃.

832. What is the cell potential, E_cell, when a galvanic cell reaches equilibrium?
    A. E_cell > 0
    B. E_cell < 0
    C. E_cell = 0
    D. E_cell = E°_cell
    **Answer:** C. E_cell = 0
    **Explanation:** When a cell reaches equilibrium, it can no longer do work (it is "dead"). At equilibrium, ΔG = 0. Since ΔG = -nFE_cell, E_cell must also be zero.

833. The Faraday constant represents...
    A. the charge of one mole of electrons.
    B. the charge of a single electron.
    C. the rate of an electrochemical reaction.
    D. Avogadro's number.
    **Answer:** A. the charge of one mole of electrons.
    **Explanation:** One Faraday (F) is the magnitude of the electric charge of one mole of electrons, approximately 96,485 coulombs per mole.

834. A reducing agent is a species that...
    A. gets reduced.
    B. gains electrons.
    C. increases in oxidation number.
    D. causes oxidation.
    **Answer:** C. increases in oxidation number.
    **Explanation:** A reducing agent donates electrons, causing another species to be reduced. In the process of losing electrons, its own oxidation number increases.

---

**Chapter 15 & 16: Organic Chemistry**

835. The functional group R-CHO represents an...
    A. alcohol.
    B. aldehyde.
    C. ether.
    D. ester.
    **Answer:** B. aldehyde.
    **Explanation:** The -CHO group, where a carbonyl (C=O) is bonded to a hydrogen and an R group, is the aldehyde functional group.

836. Teflon, or PTFE, is an addition polymer made from the monomer...
    A. Chloroethene
    B. Tetrafluoroethene
    C. Ethene
    D. Propene
    **Answer:** B. Tetrafluoroethene
    **Explanation:** PTFE stands for poly(tetrafluoroethene). Its monomer is CF₂=CF₂.

837. Which of the following is a tertiary amine?
    A. CH₃NH₂
    B. (CH₃)₂NH
    C. (CH₃)₃N
    D. CH₃CONH₂
    **Answer:** C. (CH₃)₃N
    **Explanation:** In a tertiary amine, the nitrogen atom is bonded to three carbon (alkyl or aryl) groups and no hydrogen atoms. (CH₃)₃N is trimethylamine.

838. Zaitsev's rule predicts that in an elimination reaction, the major product is...
    A. the less substituted alkene.
    B. the more substituted alkene.
    C. the cis isomer.
    D. the trans isomer.
    **Answer:** B. the more substituted alkene.
    **Explanation:** Zaitsev's rule states that when there is a choice of where to form a double bond in an elimination reaction, the more highly substituted (more stable) alkene will be the major product.

839. Which of the following can be readily oxidized by a mild oxidizing agent like Fehling's solution?
    A. CH₃COCH₃ (Propanone)
    B. CH₃CH₂OH (Ethanol)
    C. CH₃CHO (Ethanal)
    D. CH₃COOH (Ethanoic acid)
    **Answer:** C. CH₃CHO (Ethanal)
    **Explanation:** Mild oxidizing agents like Fehling's or Benedict's solution are used to test for aldehydes. They oxidize the aldehyde to a carboxylate ion, while the reagent itself changes color. Ketones and alcohols (with such mild agents) do not react.

840. The product of the saponification of ethyl ethanoate is...
    A. Ethanol and ethanoic acid
    B. Sodium ethanoate and ethanol
    C. Sodium ethanolate and ethanoic acid
    D. Ethanal and sodium ethanoate
    **Answer:** B. Sodium ethanoate and ethanol
    **Explanation:** Saponification is hydrolysis with a strong base (like NaOH). The ester breaks down into its parent alcohol (ethanol) and the salt of its parent carboxylic acid (sodium ethanoate).

841. Geometric isomerism (cis-trans) is possible in...
    A. Butane
    B. But-1-ene
    C. Pent-2-ene
    D. Propyne
    **Answer:** C. Pent-2-ene
    **Explanation:** Geometric isomerism in an alkene requires that each carbon of the double bond has two different groups attached. In pent-2-ene (CH₃-CH=CH-CH₂CH₃), C2 is bonded to -H and -CH₃, and C3 is bonded to -H and -CH₂CH₃. Both carbons have different groups, so cis/trans isomerism is possible.

842. The process of converting starch into glucose is an example of...
    A. Dehydration
    B. Oxidation
    C. Hydrolysis
    D. Polymerization
    **Answer:** C. Hydrolysis
    **Explanation:** Starch is a polymer of glucose. The process of breaking down a polymer into its monomers by the chemical addition of water is called hydrolysis.

843. Which reaction is used to convert an alkene into an alkane?
    A. Hydration
    B. Hydrogenation
    C. Halogenation
    D. Dehydration
    **Answer:** B. Hydrogenation
    **Explanation:** Hydrogenation is the addition of hydrogen (H₂) across the double bond of an alkene, usually with a metal catalyst (Ni, Pt, Pd), which saturates the molecule to form an alkane.

844. Toluene is the common name for...
    A. Hydroxybenzene
    B. Methylbenzene
    C. Aminobenzene
    D. Chlorobenzene
    **Answer:** B. Methylbenzene
    **Explanation:** Toluene is the widely used common name for the aromatic compound consisting of a benzene ring with one methyl group substituent.

845. How many sigma bonds are present in a molecule of propane (C₃H₈)?
    A. 8
    B. 9
    C. 10
    D. 11
    **Answer:** C. 10
    **Explanation:** The structure is CH₃-CH₂-CH₃. There are 2 C-C single bonds and 8 C-H single bonds. Since all single bonds are sigma bonds, the total number of sigma bonds is 2 + 8 = 10.

846. A reaction where a molecule loses atoms from adjacent carbons to form a double bond is called...
    A. Addition
    B. Substitution
    C. Elimination
    D. Isomerization
    **Answer:** C. Elimination
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of an elimination reaction, which is essentially the reverse of an addition reaction.

---

**Chapter 17 & 18: Laboratory & Experimental**

847. The piece of apparatus designed to deliver a single, fixed, and highly accurate volume of liquid is the...
    A. Graduated cylinder
    B. Beaker
    C. Volumetric pipette
    D. Burette
    **Answer:** C. Volumetric pipette
    **Explanation:** A volumetric (or bulb) pipette is calibrated for maximum accuracy in delivering one specific volume (e.g., 25.00 cm³), making it ideal for measuring the analyte in a titration.

848. A student observes that a solid dissolves in water to form a solution that feels warm. The dissolution process is...
    A. endothermic with a positive ΔH.
    B. exothermic with a positive ΔH.
    C. endothermic with a negative ΔH.
    D. exothermic with a negative ΔH.
    **Answer:** D. exothermic with a negative ΔH.
    **Explanation:** If the solution feels warm, it means the dissolving process is releasing heat into the solution (the surroundings). This is an exothermic process, which is characterized by a negative enthalpy change (ΔH).

849. To confirm the presence of ammonia gas, one would test it with...
    A. a glowing splint.
    B. limewater.
    C. damp red litmus paper.
    D. acidified potassium dichromate paper.
    **Answer:** C. damp red litmus paper.
    **Explanation:** Ammonia is the only common alkaline gas and will turn damp red litmus paper blue. This is its confirmatory test.

850. In a rates experiment involving the reaction of calcium carbonate with acid, measuring which of the following over time would provide data to calculate the rate?
    A. The temperature of the flask
    B. The pH of the solution
    C. The mass of the flask and contents on a balance
    D. The intensity of the white color
    **Answer:** C. The mass of the flask and contents on a balance
    **Explanation:** The reaction produces carbon dioxide gas, which escapes if the flask is open. By placing the flask on an electronic balance, the rate of mass loss due to the escaping CO₂ can be monitored over time. Measuring pH would also work. Measuring volume of gas is another primary method. C is a very common method.

851. Which is a necessary safety precaution when heating a liquid in a test tube?
    A. Pointing the open end away from everyone.
    B. Using a very hot, roaring flame.
    C. Filling the test tube to the top.
    D. Sealing the test tube with a stopper.
    **Answer:** A. Pointing the open end away from everyone.
    **Explanation:** The liquid can boil suddenly and be ejected from the test tube ("bumping"). Pointing it away from yourself and others prevents injury from the hot liquid. Never heat a sealed container.

852. The function of a fractionating column in distillation is to...
    A. cool the vapor.
    B. provide a large surface area for repeated vaporization and condensation.
    C. filter out solid impurities.
    D. speed up the boiling process.
    **Answer:** B. provide a large surface area for repeated vaporization and condensation.
    **Explanation:** The large surface area (from glass beads, rings, or trays) allows the mixture of vapors to undergo many separation cycles, which effectively separates liquids with close boiling points. The component with the lower boiling point will reach the top of the column first.

853. A student is performing a titration. The conical flask contains the analyte and a few drops of indicator. The burette contains the titrant. Before starting the titration, what must be recorded?
    A. The temperature of the room.
    B. The mass of the conical flask.
    C. The initial reading of the burette.
    D. The volume of the conical flask.
    **Answer:** C. The initial reading of the burette.
    **Explanation:** The volume of titrant used is calculated as (Final reading - Initial reading). Therefore, the initial reading must be recorded accurately before any titrant is dispensed.

854. In paper chromatography, the R_f value of a spot is calculated as...
    A. Distance moved by spot × Distance moved by solvent
    B. Distance moved by solvent / Distance moved by spot
    C. Distance moved by spot / Distance moved by solvent
    D. (Distance moved by solvent - Distance moved by spot)
    **Answer:** C. Distance moved by spot / Distance moved by solvent
    **Explanation:** The R_f (retardation factor) value is a ratio that quantifies how far a substance travels relative to the solvent front. It is always a value less than 1.

855. A Bunsen burner flame is yellow and sooty when...
    A. the gas supply is too high.
    B. the air hole is fully open.
    C. the combustion is complete.
    D. the air hole is closed.
    **Answer:** D. the air hole is closed.
    **Explanation:** A closed air hole limits the oxygen supply, causing incomplete combustion. This produces glowing hot particles of unburnt carbon (soot), resulting in a luminous yellow and relatively cool flame.

856. Which technique is based on differences in solubility in two different phases?
    A. Filtration
    B. Chromatography
    C. Distillation
    D. Sublimation
    **Answer:** B. Chromatography
    **Explanation:** Chromatography works by separating components of a mixture based on their differential distribution (partitioning) between a stationary phase and a mobile phase. This distribution is governed by factors like adsorption and relative solubility.

857. An error where a student consistently reads the burette 0.10 cm³ higher than the true value is a...
    A. random error.
    B. systematic error.
    C. precision error.
    D. percentage error.
    **Answer:** B. systematic error.
    **Explanation:** A systematic error is a consistent, repeatable error associated with a flaw in the equipment or experimental method (like consistent misreading or an improperly calibrated instrument). It affects the accuracy of the results. Random errors are unpredictable fluctuations.

858. When purifying a salt by recrystallization, the hot saturated solution is cooled to...
    A. cause the impurities to crystallize first.
    B. decrease the solubility of the desired salt, causing it to crystallize.
    C. increase the solubility of the desired salt.
    D. evaporate the remaining solvent quickly.
    **Answer:** B. decrease the solubility of the desired salt, causing it to crystallize.
    **Explanation:** For most salts, solubility decreases as temperature decreases. Cooling a hot, saturated solution makes it supersaturated at the lower temperature, causing the pure salt to crystallize out while the impurities (if present in smaller amounts) remain dissolved.

---
**Comprehensive & Mixed Questions**

859. The general formula for the alkynes is...
    A. CₙH₂ₙ₊₂
    B. CₙH₂ₙ
    C. CₙH₂ₙ₋₂
    D. CₙHₙ
    **Answer:** C. CₙH₂ₙ₋₂
    **Explanation:** This is the general formula for an acyclic hydrocarbon containing one carbon-carbon triple bond.

860. In the equation ΔG = ΔH - TΔS, ΔG stands for...
    A. Change in enthalpy
    B. Change in Gibbs free energy
    C. Change in entropy
    D. Change in temperature
    **Answer:** B. Change in Gibbs free energy
    **Explanation:** This is the Gibbs free energy equation, which relates the change in free energy (ΔG), a predictor of spontaneity, to the changes in enthalpy (ΔH) and entropy (ΔS).

861. Which of the following is responsible for the temporary hardness of water?
    A. MgSO₄
    B. Ca(HCO₃)₂
    C. CaCl₂
    D. Na₂SO₄
    **Answer:** B. Ca(HCO₃)₂
    **Explanation:** Temporary hardness is caused by dissolved hydrogencarbonates (bicarbonates) of calcium and magnesium, as it can be removed by boiling.

862. Which method is used to extract sodium metal industrially?
    A. Reduction of Na₂O with carbon.
    B. Electrolysis of aqueous NaCl.
    C. Displacement using potassium.
    D. Electrolysis of molten NaCl (Downs Process).
    **Answer:** D. Electrolysis of molten NaCl (Downs Process).
    **Explanation:** Sodium is a highly reactive metal. It must be extracted by electrolysis. Electrolysis of aqueous NaCl would produce hydrogen at the cathode, not sodium. Therefore, its molten salt must be used.

863. Which type of radiation has the highest penetrating power?
    A. Alpha (α)
    B. Beta (β)
    C. Gamma (γ)
    D. X-ray
    **Answer:** C. Gamma (γ)
    **Explanation:** Gamma rays are high-energy electromagnetic radiation with no mass or charge, which allows them to penetrate matter much more effectively than alpha particles (stopped by paper) or beta particles (stopped by aluminum).

864. Which of these is a d-block element?
    A. K
    B. Sr
    C. Sc
    D. B
    **Answer:** C. Sc
    **Explanation:** Scandium (Sc) is the first element in the d-block (transition metals) of the periodic table. K and Sr are in the s-block, and B is in the p-block.

865. The reaction between an amine and a carboxylic acid at high temperatures can form...
    A. an ester.
    B. an ether.
    C. an amide.
    D. an anhydride.
    **Answer:** C. an amide.
    **Explanation:** This is a condensation reaction where the -OH from the carboxylic acid and an -H from the amine are eliminated as water, forming a peptide or amide bond (-CONH-).

866. What is the empirical formula of glucose (C₆H₁₂O₆)?
    A. C₆H₁₂O₆
    B. C₃H₆O₃
    C. C₂H₄O₂
    D. CH₂O
    **Answer:** D. CH₂O
    **Explanation:** The empirical formula is the simplest whole-number ratio of the atoms. The ratio 6:12:6 can be divided by 6 to give the simplest ratio of 1:2:1.

867. A liquid with strong intermolecular forces would be expected to have a...
    A. low boiling point and low viscosity.
    B. low boiling point and high viscosity.
    C. high boiling point and high viscosity.
    D. high boiling point and low viscosity.
    **Answer:** C. high boiling point and high viscosity.
    **Explanation:** Strong intermolecular forces require a lot of energy to overcome for molecules to escape into the gas phase (high boiling point) and also cause high internal friction, resisting flow (high viscosity).

868. The primary air pollutant responsible for acid rain is...
    A. carbon monoxide.
    B. methane.
    C. sulfur dioxide.
    D. ozone.
    **Answer:** C. sulfur dioxide.
    **Explanation:** Sulfur dioxide (SO₂) and nitrogen oxides (NOₓ) are the main primary pollutants that react with atmospheric water to form sulfuric acid and nitric acid, the main components of acid rain.

869. The functional group isomer of butan-1-ol is...
    A. butan-2-ol.
    B. diethyl ether.
    C. butanal.
    D. butanone.
    **Answer:** B. diethyl ether.
    **Explanation:** Functional group isomers have the same molecular formula but different functional groups. Butan-1-ol (an alcohol) has the formula C₄H₁₀O. Diethyl ether (CH₃CH₂-O-CH₂CH₃), an ether, also has the formula C₄H₁₀O. Butan-2-ol is a positional isomer.

870. What mass of potassium hydroxide is needed to prepare 250 cm³ of 0.2 mol dm⁻³ solution? [K=39, O=16, H=1]
    A. 1.4 g
    B. 2.8 g
    C. 5.6 g
    D. 11.2 g
    **Answer:** B. 2.8 g
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Molar mass KOH = 39 + 16 + 1 = 56 g/mol.
    2.  Volume = 250 cm³ = 0.25 dm³.
    3.  Moles needed = Molarity × Volume = 0.2 mol/dm³ × 0.25 dm³ = 0.05 mol.
    4.  Mass = Moles × Molar Mass = 0.05 mol × 56 g/mol = 2.8 g.

871. Which gas relights a glowing splint?
    A. Hydrogen
    B. Carbon dioxide
    C. Oxygen
    D. Nitrogen
    **Answer:** C. Oxygen
    **Explanation:** This is the characteristic chemical test for oxygen. A glowing splint will burst back into flame in the presence of oxygen, which supports combustion.

872. The IUPAC name for isopropyl alcohol is...
    A. Propan-1-ol
    B. Propan-2-ol
    C. Ethanol
    D. 2-methylpropan-2-ol
    **Answer:** B. Propan-2-ol
    **Explanation:** The "isopropyl" group is a three-carbon chain where the point of attachment is the central carbon. As an alcohol, this means the -OH group is on the second carbon of the propane chain.

873. Which of the following is a physical property of matter?
    A. Density
    B. Acidity
    C. Flammability
    D. Reactivity
    **Answer:** A. Density
    **Explanation:** Density can be measured without changing the chemical composition of a substance. The other options describe chemical properties.

874. When an atom gains an electron, it forms a...
    A. Cation, and its size increases.
    B. Anion, and its size increases.
    C. Cation, and its size decreases.
    D. Anion, and its size decreases.
    **Answer:** B. Anion, and its size increases.
    **Explanation:** Gaining a negative electron forms a negative ion (anion). The added electron increases electron-electron repulsion in the valence shell, causing the electron cloud to expand and the ionic size to increase.

875. In a redox reaction, the substance that is oxidized...
    A. acts as the oxidizing agent.
    B. acts as the reducing agent.
    C. gains electrons.
    D. decreases in oxidation number.
    **Answer:** B. acts as the reducing agent.
    **Explanation:** Oxidation is the loss of electrons. A substance that loses electrons *causes* another substance to gain them (be reduced). Therefore, the substance that is oxidized is the reducing agent.

876. A 1.0 M solution of which of the following will have the highest pH?
    A. HCl
    B. CH₃COOH
    C. NH₄Cl
    D. NaOH
    **Answer:** D. NaOH
    **Explanation:** A high pH corresponds to a strongly basic solution. NaOH is a strong base that dissociates completely to produce a high concentration of OH⁻ ions. HCl is a strong acid (low pH). CH₃COOH is a weak acid (low pH). NH₄Cl is a salt that forms an acidic solution (pH < 7).

877. The particles in a solid are...
    A. stationary and have no kinetic energy.
    B. able to slide past one another.
    C. in constant random motion throughout the container.
    D. held in fixed positions and vibrate.
    **Answer:** D. held in fixed positions and vibrate.
    **Explanation:** In a solid, particularly a crystalline one, the constituent particles are locked into a lattice structure. They possess kinetic energy and are not motionless, but their motion is restricted to vibrations about their fixed positions.

878. What volume will 0.5 moles of helium gas occupy at 298 K and 1 atm pressure? [R = 0.0821 L·atm/(mol·K)]
    A. 11.2 L
    B. 12.2 L
    C. 22.4 L
    D. 24.5 L
    **Answer:** B. 12.2 L
    **Explanation:** Use the Ideal Gas Law, PV = nRT.
    V = nRT / P
    V = (0.5 mol)(0.0821 L·atm/(mol·K))(298 K) / (1 atm)
    V ≈ 12.23 L.

879. Which statement is true about structural isomers?
    A. They have the same physical properties.
    B. They have the same connectivity of atoms.
    C. They have the same molecular formula but different structural formulas.
    D. They are non-superimposable mirror images.
    **Answer:** C. They have the same molecular formula but different structural formulas.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of structural (or constitutional) isomers. Because their atomic connectivity is different, their physical and chemical properties are also different.

880. An exothermic reaction causes the temperature of the surroundings to...
    A. increase.
    B. decrease.
    C. remain constant.
    D. fluctuate.
    **Answer:** A. increase.
    **Explanation:** An exothermic reaction releases heat from the system into the surroundings. This transfer of heat energy raises the temperature of the surroundings.

881. In the electrolysis of brine, what is the major product at the cathode?
    A. Sodium
    B. Chlorine
    C. Hydrogen
    D. Oxygen
    **Answer:** C. Hydrogen
    **Explanation:** At the negative cathode, two possible reductions can occur: Na⁺ + e⁻ → Na OR 2H₂O + 2e⁻ → H₂ + 2OH⁻. Since water is more easily reduced than the sodium ion, hydrogen gas is produced.

882. Which of the following describes an acidic oxide?
    A. CaO
    B. K₂O
    C. CO₂
    D. Al₂O₃
    **Answer:** C. CO₂
    **Explanation:** Oxides of non-metals are typically acidic. Carbon dioxide dissolves in water to form carbonic acid (H₂CO₃). CaO and K₂O are basic oxides. Al₂O₃ is amphoteric.

883. The process by which monomers join to form a polymer with the elimination of water is...
    A. cracking.
    B. addition polymerization.
    C. hydration.
    D. condensation polymerization.
    **Answer:** D. condensation polymerization.
    **Explanation:** This is the defining characteristic of a condensation polymerization reaction, which is how polyesters and polyamides are formed.

884. An aqueous solution is a good conductor of electricity. The solute is likely...
    A. sugar.
    B. iodine.
    C. ethanol.
    D. potassium chloride.
    **Answer:** D. potassium chloride.
    **Explanation:** For a solution to conduct electricity, it must contain mobile ions. Potassium chloride (KCl) is an ionic salt that dissociates completely in water to form mobile K⁺ and Cl⁻ ions, making it a strong electrolyte. The other options are molecular compounds that do not produce ions.

885. The reaction of ethene with bromine water is a test for...
    A. acidity.
    B. saturation.
    C. unsaturation.
    D. alcohols.
    **Answer:** C. unsaturation.
    **Explanation:** The decolorization of bromine water by a substance indicates the presence of a carbon-carbon multiple bond (unsaturation), as the substance undergoes an addition reaction with the bromine.

886. Which equation represents a reaction at equilibrium?
    A. Mg(s) + 2HCl(aq) → MgCl₂(aq) + H₂(g)
    B. CaCO₃(s) ⇌ CaO(s) + CO₂(g)
    C. 2Na(s) + 2H₂O(l) → 2NaOH(aq) + H₂(g)
    D. C(s) + O₂(g) → CO₂(g)
    **Answer:** B. CaCO₃(s) ⇌ CaO(s) + CO₂(g)
    **Explanation:** The double arrow (⇌) is used to indicate a reversible reaction that can reach a state of chemical equilibrium. The other reactions are typically shown as going to completion.

887. The valency of aluminum in AlCl₃ is...
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 3
    D. 4
    **Answer:** C. 3
    **Explanation:** In aluminum chloride, one aluminum atom combines with three chlorine atoms. Therefore, the combining power or valency of aluminum is 3.

888. In the reaction Zn + 2HCl → ZnCl₂ + H₂, zinc...
    A. is oxidized and is the reducing agent.
    B. is oxidized and is the oxidizing agent.
    C. is reduced and is the reducing agent.
    D. is reduced and is the oxidizing agent.
    **Answer:** A. is oxidized and is the reducing agent.
    **Explanation:** Zinc's oxidation state goes from 0 to +2, so it is oxidized. By providing the electrons for the reduction of H⁺, it acts as the reducing agent.

889. What is the pH of pure water at 25°C?
    A. 0
    B. 5.6
    C. 7.0
    D. 14.0
    **Answer:** C. 7.0
    **Explanation:** In pure water, [H⁺] = [OH⁻] = 1.0 x 10⁻⁷ M, which corresponds to a pH of 7, the definition of neutrality. (Normal rain has a pH of ~5.6 due to dissolved atmospheric CO₂).

890. The correct formula for ammonium sulfate is...
    A. NH₄SO₄
    B. (NH₄)₂SO₄
    C. NH₃SO₄
    D. (NH₃)₂SO₄
    **Answer:** B. (NH₄)₂SO₄
    **Explanation:** The ammonium ion is NH₄⁺ (charge +1). The sulfate ion is SO₄²⁻ (charge -2). To make a neutral compound, two ammonium ions are needed to balance one sulfate ion.

891. The shape of an s orbital is...
    A. Spherical
    B. Dumbbell
    C. Cloverleaf
    D. Elliptical
    **Answer:** A. Spherical
    **Explanation:** An s orbital is spherically symmetrical around the nucleus.

892. Which statement correctly describes a reaction for which the activation energy is high?
    A. The reaction will be very fast.
    B. The reaction will be very slow.
    C. The reaction will be highly exothermic.
    D. The reaction requires no heat to start.
    **Answer:** B. The reaction will be very slow.
    **Explanation:** A high activation energy represents a large energy barrier that must be overcome. At a given temperature, only a small fraction of reactant molecules will have sufficient energy to cross this barrier, resulting in a slow reaction rate.

893. What volume of a 2M solution of H₂SO₄ is needed to contain 0.5 moles of H₂SO₄?
    A. 100 cm³
    B. 250 cm³
    C. 500 cm³
    D. 1000 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 250 cm³
    **Explanation:** Volume = Moles / Molarity = 0.5 mol / 2 mol/dm³ = 0.25 dm³.
    To convert to cm³, 0.25 dm³ × 1000 cm³/dm³ = 250 cm³.

894. The atomic number of an element is determined by the number of...
    A. electrons in its outer shell.
    B. neutrons in its nucleus.
    C. protons in its nucleus.
    D. electrons and neutrons.
    **Answer:** C. protons in its nucleus.
    **Explanation:** The number of protons is the fundamental property that defines an element and its atomic number (Z).

895. Which of these metals will NOT displace hydrogen from dilute HCl?
    A. Zn
    B. Mg
    C. Fe
    D. Cu
    **Answer:** D. Cu
    **Explanation:** A metal can only displace hydrogen from an acid if it is above hydrogen in the reactivity/electrochemical series. Zinc, Magnesium, and Iron are all above hydrogen. Copper is below hydrogen and is not reactive enough to displace it.

896. Which is the formula for butan-2-ol?
    A. CH₃CH₂CH₂OH
    B. CH₃CH(OH)CH₂CH₃
    C. CH₃CH₂CH₂CH₂OH
    D. (CH₃)₃COH
    **Answer:** B. CH₃CH(OH)CH₂CH₃
    **Explanation:** "Butan-" means a 4-carbon chain. "-2-ol" means the hydroxyl (-OH) group is on the second carbon atom. This matches structure B.

897. What happens when ethene is passed through a solution of acidified potassium manganate(VII)?
    A. The solution remains purple.
    B. The solution turns from purple to colorless.
    C. A brown precipitate is formed.
    D. The solution turns from orange to green.
    **Answer:** B. The solution turns from purple to colorless.
    **Explanation:** Ethene, an alkene, is unsaturated and can be oxidized. The powerful oxidizing agent potassium manganate(VII) (permanganate) will oxidize the double bond, and in the process, the purple MnO₄⁻ ion is reduced to the nearly colorless Mn²⁺ ion.

898. Which one of the following compounds has hydrogen bonding as its strongest intermolecular force?
    A. Hydrogen sulfide (H₂S)
    B. Methane (CH₄)
    C. Ammonia (NH₃)
    D. Hydrogen chloride (HCl)
    **Answer:** C. Ammonia (NH₃)
    **Explanation:** Hydrogen bonding only occurs when hydrogen is directly bonded to N, O, or F. Of the options, only ammonia meets this criterion.

899. A change in which of the following will alter the rate constant, k, of a reaction?
    A. Pressure
    B. Concentration
    C. Temperature
    D. A catalyst
    **Answer:** C. Temperature
    **Explanation:** The rate constant, k, is specific for a reaction at a given temperature. According to the Arrhenius equation, its value changes (usually increases) significantly with temperature. A catalyst changes the rate by providing a new pathway with a *different* rate constant and activation energy.

900. An element in Period 3 forms an ion X³⁻. The element is likely in...
    A. Group 13
    B. Group 3
    C. Group 15
    D. Group 16
    **Answer:** C. Group 15
    **Explanation:** An element that forms a 3- ion does so by gaining 3 electrons to achieve a stable octet. This means the neutral atom had 5 valence electrons, which corresponds to Group 15. An example from Period 3 is Phosphorus (P).

901. During the electrolysis of molten potassium bromide (KBr), the substance formed at the positive electrode is...
    A. Potassium
    B. Bromine
    C. Hydrogen
    D. Oxygen
    **Answer:** B. Bromine
    **Explanation:** The positive electrode is the anode. It attracts the negative bromide ions (Br⁻). The Br⁻ ions are oxidized at the anode to form bromine: 2Br⁻ → Br₂ + 2e⁻.

902. A solution of glucose (molar mass 180 g/mol) is prepared by dissolving 36 g of glucose in 200 g of water. What is the molality of the solution?
    A. 0.5 m
    B. 1.0 m
    C. 1.8 m
    D. 2.0 m
    **Answer:** B. 1.0 m
    **Explanation:** Moles of glucose = 36 g / 180 g/mol = 0.2 mol.
    Mass of solvent (water) = 200 g = 0.2 kg.
    Molality (m) = moles of solute / kg of solvent = 0.2 mol / 0.2 kg = 1.0 mol/kg or 1.0 m.

903. An increase in the concentration of reactants leads to an increase in reaction rate because...
    A. the activation energy is lowered.
    B. the temperature of the system increases.
    C. the frequency of effective collisions increases.
    D. the particles expand in size.
    **Answer:** C. the frequency of effective collisions increases.
    **Explanation:** A higher concentration means more reactant particles are packed into the same volume. This leads to more collisions per unit time, and consequently, a higher frequency of successful, reaction-producing collisions.

904. Which of the following CANNOT act as a Lewis base?
    A. H₂O
    B. NH₃
    C. Cl⁻
    D. BH₃
    **Answer:** D. BH₃
    **Explanation:** A Lewis base must have a lone pair of electrons to donate. Water, ammonia, and the chloride ion all have lone pairs. Borane (BH₃), like BF₃, has an incomplete octet and is electron-deficient. It acts as a Lewis acid, not a base.

905. What is the key distinction between an addition polymer and a condensation polymer?
    A. Addition polymers are natural, condensation polymers are synthetic.
    B. No small molecule is eliminated during addition polymerization.
    C. Addition polymers contain ester links, condensation polymers contain amide links.
    D. Condensation polymers have higher molar masses.
    **Answer:** B. No small molecule is eliminated during addition polymerization.
    **Explanation:** This is the fundamental difference. In addition polymerization, monomers simply add to one another. In condensation polymerization, the joining of monomers is accompanied by the formation and elimination of a small molecule like water or HCl.

906. An element with atomic number 12 will form an ion with a charge of...
    A. 1+
    B. 2+
    C. 1-
    D. 2-
    **Answer:** B. 2+
