    **Explanation:** The element with Z=12 is Magnesium (Mg). Its electron configuration is [Ne] 3s². It will readily lose its two valence electrons to achieve a stable noble gas configuration, forming the Mg²⁺ ion.

907. What type of reaction is 2H₂O₂(aq) → 2H₂O(l) + O₂(g)?
    A. Combination
    B. Neutralization
    C. Displacement
    D. Decomposition
    **Answer:** D. Decomposition
    **Explanation:** A single reactant (hydrogen peroxide) is breaking down into simpler substances (water and oxygen). This is a decomposition reaction. It is also a disproportionation (redox) reaction, but decomposition is the best classification based on structure.

908. A saturated hydrocarbon is one that...
    A. is dissolved in water.
    B. contains only single C-C bonds.
    C. will decolorize bromine water.
    D. is liquid at room temperature.
    **Answer:** B. contains only single C-C bonds.
    **Explanation:** A saturated hydrocarbon (an alkane) has the maximum possible number of hydrogen atoms bonded to its carbon skeleton because it contains no C-C multiple bonds.

909. For the exothermic reaction A + B ⇌ C + D, a decrease in temperature will...
    A. increase the rate and shift the equilibrium to the right.
    B. decrease the rate and shift the equilibrium to the right.
    C. increase the rate and shift the equilibrium to the left.
    D. decrease the rate and shift the equilibrium to the left.
    **Answer:** B. decrease the rate and shift the equilibrium to the right.
    **Explanation:**
    Kinetics: Decreasing the temperature always decreases the reaction rate.
    Equilibrium: The forward reaction is exothermic. According to Le Chatelier's principle, removing heat (decreasing T) will shift the equilibrium to the side that produces heat, which is the forward direction (right).

910. The correct way to read the volume in a burette is to...
    A. read the top of the meniscus and estimate to one decimal place.
    B. read the bottom of the meniscus at eye level and estimate to two decimal places.
    C. subtract the reading from 50.00 cm³.
    D. read the value closest to the 1.00 cm³ mark.
    **Answer:** B. read the bottom of the meniscus at eye level and estimate to two decimal places.
    **Explanation:** This ensures accuracy and precision. Reading must be at eye level to avoid parallax error, and standard burettes are precise enough to be read to the nearest 0.05 cm³ (i.e., to two decimal places).

911. Which method is most suitable for separating iron filings from sulfur powder?
    A. Using a magnet
    B. Dissolving in water
    C. Sublimation
    D. Filtration
    **Answer:** A. Using a magnet
    **Explanation:** Iron is ferromagnetic and will be attracted to a magnet, while sulfur will not. This provides a simple physical method of separation.

912. The species responsible for conducting electricity in molten potassium iodide (KI) are...
    A. K atoms and I atoms.
    B. K⁺ ions and I₂ molecules.
    C. Mobile electrons.
    D. Mobile K⁺ ions and I⁻ ions.
    **Answer:** D. Mobile K⁺ ions and I⁻ ions.
    **Explanation:** In the molten (liquid) state, the ions of an ionic compound are free to move and carry electric charge. In solid KI, the ions are fixed in a lattice and cannot conduct electricity.

913. An aqueous solution of ammonium chloride turns blue litmus paper red because of the presence of...
    A. chloride ions.
    B. ammonium ions.
    C. ammonia molecules.
    D. water molecules.
    **Answer:** B. ammonium ions.
    **Explanation:** Ammonium chloride is a salt of a weak base. The ammonium ion (NH₄⁺) is the conjugate acid and reacts with water in a hydrolysis equilibrium (NH₄⁺ + H₂O ⇌ NH₃ + H₃O⁺), producing excess hydronium (H₃O⁺) ions, which makes the solution acidic.

914. How many grams of H₂ are needed to react completely with 28g of N₂ according to the equation N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) → 2NH₃(g)? [N=14, H=1]
    A. 2 g
    B. 3 g
    C. 6 g
    D. 12 g
    **Answer:** C. 6 g
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Molar mass N₂ = 28 g/mol. So, 28g of N₂ is 1 mole.
    2.  From the balanced equation, the mole ratio of N₂ to H₂ is 1:3.
    3.  Moles of H₂ needed = 1 mol N₂ × (3 mol H₂ / 1 mol N₂) = 3 moles H₂.
    4.  Molar mass H₂ = 2 g/mol.
    5.  Mass of H₂ = 3 mol × 2 g/mol = 6 g.

915. Allotropes are different forms of the same...
    A. compound in the same physical state.
    B. element in the same physical state.
    C. compound in different physical states.
    D. element in different physical states.
    **Answer:** B. element in the same physical state.
    **Explanation:** This is the precise definition of allotropy, e.g., diamond, graphite, and fullerenes are all solid allotropes of carbon.

916. What is the functional group in ethanoic acid?
    A. Hydroxyl (-OH)
    B. Carbonyl (-CO-)
    C. Carboxyl (-COOH)
    D. Ester (-COO-)
    **Answer:** C. Carboxyl (-COOH)
    **Explanation:** The -COOH group is the functional group that defines a carboxylic acid.

917. In the process of vulcanization, sulfur atoms...
    A. break down the rubber polymer chains.
    B. form cross-links between rubber polymer chains.
    C. replace hydrogen atoms in the rubber.
    D. act as a catalyst for polymerization.
    **Answer:** B. form cross-links between rubber polymer chains.
    **Explanation:** The sulfur forms disulfide bridges between the long polyisoprene chains of natural rubber. These cross-links prevent the chains from slipping past each other easily, making the rubber stronger, less sticky, and more elastic.

918. Which gas will be produced when dilute sulfuric acid is added to sodium sulfite (Na₂SO₃)?
    A. Hydrogen (H₂)
    B. Oxygen (O₂)
    C. Sulfur dioxide (SO₂)
    D. Sulfur trioxide (SO₃)
    **Answer:** C. Sulfur dioxide (SO₂)
    **Explanation:** The reaction is Na₂SO₃(s) + H₂SO₄(aq) → Na₂SO₄(aq) + H₂O(l) + SO₂(g). The unstable sulfurous acid formed initially decomposes into water and pungent sulfur dioxide gas.

919. A solution of pH 2 is how many times more acidic than a solution of pH 5?
    A. 3 times
    B. 10 times
    C. 100 times
    D. 1000 times
    **Answer:** D. 1000 times
    **Explanation:** The pH scale is logarithmic. A difference of 1 pH unit is a 10-fold difference in [H⁺]. A difference of 3 pH units (5 - 2 = 3) is a 10 × 10 × 10 = 10³ = 1000-fold difference in acidity.

920. An important use of chlorine in water treatment is to...
    A. remove suspended solids.
    B. kill harmful microorganisms.
    C. soften the water.
    D. remove dissolved salts.
    **Answer:** B. kill harmful microorganisms.
    **Explanation:** Chlorination is the final disinfection step in water purification. Chlorine is a powerful oxidizing agent that kills bacteria, viruses, and other pathogens, making the water safe to drink.

921. What is the molarity of a solution made by dissolving 19.6 g of H₂SO₄ in enough water to make 200 cm³ of solution? [H=1, S=32, O=16]
    A. 0.5 M
    B. 1.0 M
    C. 2.0 M
    D. 5.0 M
    **Answer:** B. 1.0 M
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Molar mass H₂SO₄ = 98 g/mol.
    2.  Moles H₂SO₄ = 19.6 g / 98 g/mol = 0.2 mol.
    3.  Volume = 200 cm³ = 0.2 dm³.
    4.  Molarity = Moles / Volume = 0.2 mol / 0.2 dm³ = 1.0 M.

922. The Haber process requires conditions of high pressure because...
    A. the forward reaction is exothermic.
    B. the forward reaction leads to a decrease in the number of moles of gas.
    C. the reactants are gases.
    D. the iron catalyst works better at high pressure.
    **Answer:** B. the forward reaction leads to a decrease in the number of moles of gas.
    **Explanation:** N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g). The reaction converts 4 moles of gas into 2 moles of gas. According to Le Chatelier's principle, increasing the pressure will favor the side with fewer moles, shifting the equilibrium to the right and increasing the yield of ammonia.

923. In which reaction is water acting as an oxidizing agent?
    A. 2Na(s) + 2H₂O(l) → 2NaOH(aq) + H₂(g)
    B. C(s) + H₂O(g) → CO(g) + H₂(g)
    C. SO₃(g) + H₂O(l) → H₂SO₄(aq)
    D. CaO(s) + H₂O(l) → Ca(OH)₂(aq)
    **Answer:** A. 2Na(s) + 2H₂O(l) → 2NaOH(aq) + H₂(g)
    **Explanation:** An oxidizing agent gets reduced. In reaction A, the oxidation state of hydrogen in H₂O is +1, and in the product H₂, it is 0. Since the oxidation number of H decreases, water is reduced, meaning it acted as the oxidizing agent for Sodium (which was oxidized from 0 to +1).

924. An example of a compound containing both ionic and covalent bonds is...
    A. Methane (CH₄)
    B. Water (H₂O)
    C. Sodium chloride (NaCl)
    D. Sodium hydroxide (NaOH)
    **Answer:** D. Sodium hydroxide (NaOH)
    **Explanation:** In NaOH, there is an ionic bond between the sodium cation (Na⁺) and the polyatomic hydroxide anion (OH⁻). Within the hydroxide ion, the oxygen and hydrogen atoms are held together by a covalent bond.

925. The general trend for reactivity of alkali metals (Group 1) is that it...
    A. increases down the group.
    B. decreases down the group.
    C. remains constant down the group.
    D. increases then decreases down the group.
    **Answer:** A. increases down the group.
    **Explanation:** Alkali metals react by losing their single valence electron. As you go down the group, the atomic size increases, and the valence electron is further from the nucleus and more shielded. This makes it easier to remove the electron, so reactivity increases.

926. What distinguishes a strong acid from a weak acid in solution?
    A. The strong acid is more concentrated.
    B. The strong acid has a higher molar mass.
    C. The strong acid has a higher pH.
    D. The strong acid dissociates completely, while the weak acid dissociates partially.
    **Answer:** D. The strong acid dissociates completely, while the weak acid dissociates partially.
    **Explanation:** Acid strength is a measure of the degree of ionization (dissociation). Strong acids ionize almost 100% in solution, whereas weak acids establish an equilibrium where only a small fraction of the molecules have dissociated.

927. What is the systematic name for Fe₂(SO₄)₃?
    A. Iron(II) sulfate
    B. Iron(III) sulfate
    C. Iron(II) sulfite
    D. Iron(III) sulfide
    **Answer:** B. Iron(III) sulfate
    **Explanation:** The sulfate ion (SO₄²⁻) has a charge of -2. Since there are three sulfate ions, the total negative charge is -6. To balance this, the two iron ions must have a total charge of +6, meaning each iron ion is Fe³⁺. The name is therefore iron(III) sulfate.

928. Which of the following is NOT an allotrope of carbon?
    A. Diamond
    B. Graphite
    C. Silicon carbide
    D. Fullerene
    **Answer:** C. Silicon carbide
    **Explanation:** Allotropes are different forms of the same *element*. Silicon carbide (SiC) is a compound containing two different elements, carbon and silicon.

929. The main chemical responsible for the pungent smell of vinegar is...
    A. hydrochloric acid.
    B. ethanoic acid.
    C. formic acid.
    D. sulfuric acid.
    **Answer:** B. ethanoic acid.
    **Explanation:** Vinegar is essentially a dilute aqueous solution of ethanoic acid (acetic acid), CH₃COOH, which gives it its characteristic sour taste and pungent smell.

930. The process by which solid iodine crystals turn directly into purple vapor upon heating is...
    A. melting.
    B. boiling.
    C. evaporation.
    D. sublimation.
    **Answer:** D. sublimation.
    **Explanation:** Sublimation is the direct phase transition from solid to gas, bypassing the liquid phase. Iodine is a classic example of a substance that readily sublimes.

931. Which of the following CANNOT be determined from a balanced chemical equation?
    A. The mole ratios of reactants and products.
    B. The mass relationships between reactants and products.
    C. The rate of the reaction.
    D. The states of the reactants and products.
    **Answer:** C. The rate of the reaction.
    **Explanation:** A balanced equation describes the stoichiometry (the amounts) of a reaction but gives no information about the kinetics (how fast the reaction proceeds). The reaction rate must be determined experimentally.

932. The primary source of hydrocarbons for the chemical industry is...
    A. coal.
    B. natural gas.
    C. crude oil (petroleum).
    D. biomass.
    **Answer:** C. crude oil (petroleum).
    **Explanation:** Crude oil is a complex mixture of thousands of different hydrocarbons, which are separated by fractional distillation and then processed (e.g., by cracking and reforming) to provide the primary feedstock for the organic chemical industry.

933. Which metal is used as a catalyst in the hydrogenation of vegetable oils to make margarine?
    A. Iron
    B. Platinum
    C. Nickel
    D. Copper
    **Answer:** C. Nickel
    **Explanation:** The hydrogenation of the C=C double bonds in unsaturated fats (oils) to produce more saturated fats (margarine) is typically catalyzed by finely divided nickel. Platinum or palladium also work but are more expensive.

934. The reaction: 2K(s) + Br₂(l) → 2KBr(s) is an example of a...
    A. redox and combination reaction.
    B. decomposition and neutralization reaction.
    C. single displacement and endothermic reaction.
    D. double displacement and redox reaction.
    **Answer:** A. redox and combination reaction.
    **Explanation:** Two simpler substances (elements) combine to form a single, more complex compound (combination). Also, K is oxidized (0 to +1) and Br is reduced (0 to -1), so it is a redox reaction.

935. In an experiment to determine the formula of a hydrated salt, a student heats the salt to drive off the water. The measurement that is NOT required is...
    A. mass of empty crucible.
    B. mass of crucible + hydrated salt.
    C. mass of crucible + anhydrous salt after heating.
    D. temperature at which the water boils.
    **Answer:** D. temperature at which the water boils.
    **Explanation:** To find the formula, you need the mass of the hydrated salt, the mass of the anhydrous salt, and thus the mass of the water lost. These are found using the three mass measurements. The boiling point of water is not needed for the stoichiometric calculation.

936. Which one of the following compounds will form a solution with a pH less than 7?
    A. KCl
    B. Na₂CO₃
    C. CH₃COONH₄
    D. AlCl₃
    **Answer:** D. AlCl₃
    **Explanation:** Aluminum chloride is the salt of a strong acid (HCl) and a "weak base" equivalent (Al(OH)₃). The highly charged Al³⁺ ion strongly hydrolyzes water ( [Al(H₂O)₆]³⁺ ⇌ [Al(H₂O)₅(OH)]²⁺ + H⁺ ), releasing H⁺ ions and making the solution quite acidic.

937. A test for the sulfate ion (SO₄²⁻) involves the addition of...
    A. AgNO₃(aq) after acidifying with HNO₃.
    B. NaOH(aq) followed by warming.
    C. BaCl₂(aq) after acidifying with HCl.
    D. HCl(aq) and testing the gas with limewater.
    **Answer:** C. BaCl₂(aq) after acidifying with HCl.
    **Explanation:** The addition of barium chloride solution produces a white precipitate of barium sulfate, which is notably insoluble in dilute acid. The acid is added first to eliminate interfering ions like carbonate.

938. The monomer for natural rubber is...
    A. Ethene
    B. Isoprene (2-methylbuta-1,3-diene)
    C. Propene
    D. Butadiene
    **Answer:** B. Isoprene (2-methylbuta-1,3-diene)
    **Explanation:** Natural rubber is a polymer of the monomer isoprene.

939. An element is in Group 15 of the Periodic Table. It is likely to have...
    A. an oxidation number of +5 or -3.
    B. a valency of 2.
    C. metallic properties.
    D. a charge of +3 in its common ion.
    **Answer:** A. an oxidation number of +5 or -3.
    **Explanation:** Group 15 elements have 5 valence electrons. They can lose all 5 (forming a +5 oxidation state, e.g., in PCl₅ or N₂O₅) or gain 3 electrons to complete an octet (forming a -3 oxidation state, e.g., in NH₃ or P³⁻).

940. When drawing a graph from experimental results, the independent variable is usually plotted on the...
    A. y-axis.
    B. x-axis.
    C. origin.
    D. top of the page.
    **Answer:** B. x-axis.
    **Explanation:** By scientific convention, the independent variable (the one you control or change, like time or concentration) is plotted on the horizontal x-axis, and the dependent variable (the one you measure, like volume or temperature) is plotted on the vertical y-axis.

941. When Cr₂O₇²⁻ is reduced to Cr³⁺ in acidic solution, the colour change observed is from...
    A. Purple to colourless
    B. Orange to green
    C. Green to orange
    D. Blue to red
    **Answer:** B. Orange to green
    **Explanation:** The dichromate(VI) ion (Cr₂O₇²⁻) is orange. The chromium(III) ion (Cr³⁺) is green in aqueous solution. This color change is characteristic of redox reactions involving dichromate as an oxidizing agent.

942. An example of a compound with polar bonds but a non-polar molecule is...
    A. H₂O
    B. NH₃
    C. SF₆
    D. SO₂
    **Answer:** C. SF₆
    **Explanation:** Sulfur hexafluoride (SF₆) has a perfectly symmetrical octahedral shape. The individual S-F bonds are polar, but their dipoles are arranged symmetrically and cancel each other out completely, resulting in a non-polar molecule.

943. An ionic compound is likely to be soluble in...
    A. hexane.
    B. water.
    C. vegetable oil.
    D. benzene.
    **Answer:** B. water.
    **Explanation:** Following the "like dissolves like" principle, ionic compounds (which are extremely polar) are most likely to dissolve in a highly polar solvent like water, which can form strong ion-dipole interactions to stabilize the ions.

944. A nuclide is represented as ²⁴₁₂Mg. This indicates that an atom of magnesium has...
    A. 12 protons, 12 neutrons and 12 electrons.
    B. 12 protons, 12 neutrons and 24 electrons.
    C. 12 protons, 24 neutrons and 12 electrons.
    D. 24 protons, 12 neutrons and 12 electrons.
    **Answer:** A. 12 protons, 12 neutrons and 12 electrons.
    **Explanation:** Atomic number Z = 12 (protons). Mass number A = 24. Neutrons = A - Z = 24 - 12 = 12. In a neutral atom, electrons = protons = 12.

945. What happens when hydrogen chloride gas dissolves in water?
    A. Hydrogen ions are formed.
    B. The solution becomes basic.
    C. Covalent bonds in water break.
    D. The pH of the solution increases.
    **Answer:** A. Hydrogen ions are formed.
    **Explanation:** HCl is a strong acid. When it dissolves in water, it ionizes completely (HCl + H₂O → H₃O⁺ + Cl⁻) to form hydronium ions (H₃O⁺), which are often simplified as hydrogen ions (H⁺), making the solution acidic and lowering the pH.

946. Which of the following CANNOT be broken down into simpler substances by chemical means?
    A. Water (H₂O)
    B. Brass (an alloy)
    C. Iron (Fe)
    D. Methane (CH₄)
    **Answer:** C. Iron (Fe)
    **Explanation:** Iron is an element. Elements are the fundamental building blocks of matter and cannot be broken down into simpler substances by chemical reactions. Water and methane are compounds; brass is a mixture.

947. To obtain a sample of kerosene from a mixture of kerosene and water, you would use...
    A. distillation.
    B. chromatography.
    C. filtration.
    D. a separating funnel.
    **Answer:** D. a separating funnel.
    **Explanation:** Kerosene and water are immiscible and have different densities. They will form two separate layers, which can be easily separated by draining the lower layer (water) from a separating funnel.

948. What is the atomicity of sulfur in its most stable form at room temperature?
    A. 1
    B. 2
    C. 4
    D. 8
    **Answer:** D. 8
    **Explanation:** The most stable allotrope of sulfur at room temperature is rhombic sulfur, which consists of puckered rings of eight sulfur atoms covalently bonded together (S₈ molecules). Therefore, its atomicity is 8.

949. Which of the following is true of the reaction N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g), ΔH = -92kJ?
    A. It is endothermic and a high temperature will favor the products.
    B. It is endothermic and a low temperature will favor the products.
    C. It is exothermic and a high temperature will favor the products.
    D. It is exothermic and a low temperature will favor the products.
    **Answer:** D. It is exothermic and a low temperature will favor the products.
    **Explanation:** The negative ΔH indicates the forward reaction is exothermic. According to Le Chatelier's principle, removing heat (using a low temperature) will shift the equilibrium to the side that produces heat, which is the forward direction (favoring the products).

950. Which compound can react with both NaOH(aq) and HCl(aq)?
    A. NaCl
    B. CH₃COOH
    C. C₂H₅OH
    D. H₂N-CH₂-COOH (Glycine)
    **Answer:** D. H₂N-CH₂-COOH (Glycine)
    **Explanation:** Glycine is an amino acid. It has a basic amino group (-NH₂) which will react with acid (HCl), and an acidic carboxyl group (-COOH) which will react with a base (NaOH). Substances that can react with both acids and bases are amphoteric.

951. An electron is located in an orbital for which n=4 and l=1. The electron is in which subshell?
    A. 4s
    B. 4p
    C. 4d
    D. 4f
    **Answer:** B. 4p
    **Explanation:** The principal quantum number n=4 gives the shell number. The azimuthal quantum number l=1 corresponds to the 'p' subshell. Therefore, the electron is in the 4p subshell.

952. What volume of a 0.5M NaOH solution is required to neutralize 20 cm³ of a 0.25M H₂SO₄ solution?
    A. 10 cm³
    B. 20 cm³
    C. 30 cm³
    D. 40 cm³
    **Answer:** B. 20 cm³
    **Explanation:** Balanced equation: H₂SO₄ + 2NaOH → Na₂SO₄ + 2H₂O.
    Mole ratio n_a/n_b = 1/2.
    Using the formula (MₐVₐ)/(MᵦVᵦ) = nₐ/nᵦ:
    (0.25 × 20) / (0.5 × Vᵦ) = 1/2
    5 / (0.5 Vᵦ) = 0.5
    5 = 0.25 Vᵦ
    Vᵦ = 5 / 0.25 = 20 cm³.

953. When a substance undergoes combustion, it reacts with...
    A. nitrogen.
    B. hydrogen.
    C. water.
    D. oxygen.
    **Answer:** D. oxygen.
    **Explanation:** Combustion is a high-temperature exothermic redox reaction between a fuel and an oxidant, which is most commonly atmospheric oxygen.

954. Which of these materials is a synthetic polymer?
    A. Wool
    B. Silk
    C. Terylene
    D. Starch
    **Answer:** C. Terylene
    **Explanation:** Terylene (also known as PET or Dacron) is a synthetic polyester. Wool, silk (proteins), and starch (a polysaccharide) are all examples of natural polymers.

955. An ion X³⁺ contains 10 electrons. The number of neutrons in its nucleus is 14. What is the mass number of X?
    A. 13
    B. 14
    C. 24
    D. 27
    **Answer:** D. 27
    **Explanation:** The ion X³⁺ has lost 3 electrons. If it has 10 electrons, the neutral atom had 10+3=13 electrons. Therefore, the number of protons is 13.
    Mass number = Protons + Neutrons = 13 + 14 = 27.

956. An example of a non-electrolyte in aqueous solution is...
    A. Ethanoic acid
    B. Sodium chloride
    C. Ammonia
    D. Ethanol
    **Answer:** D. Ethanol
    **Explanation:** Ethanol (C₂H₅OH) dissolves in water as neutral molecules and does not produce ions, so it does not conduct electricity. Ethanoic acid and ammonia are weak electrolytes (dissociate partially), and sodium chloride is a strong electrolyte (dissociates fully).

957. What happens at the cathode during the electrolysis of molten PbBr₂?
    A. Pb²⁺ ions are reduced.
    B. Br⁻ ions are reduced.
    C. Pb²⁺ ions are oxidized.
    D. Br⁻ ions are oxidized.
    **Answer:** A. Pb²⁺ ions are reduced.
    **Explanation:** The cathode is the negative electrode. It attracts the positive lead(II) ions, Pb²⁺. At the cathode, these ions gain electrons and are reduced to form molten lead metal: Pb²⁺ + 2e⁻ → Pb.

958. An organic compound decolourized bromine water but showed no reaction with Tollen's reagent. The compound could be...
    A. an alkene.
    B. an alkane.
    C. an aldehyde.
    D. a ketone.
    **Answer:** A. an alkene.
    **Explanation:** Decolorizing bromine water indicates the presence of unsaturation (a C=C or C≡C bond), so it could be an alkene or alkyne. Showing no reaction with Tollen's reagent means it is not an aldehyde. An alkane is saturated. A ketone is saturated and also does not react with Tollen's reagent, but it also won't react with bromine water. Therefore, it must be an alkene.

959. According to the octet rule, a nitrogen atom...
    A. gains 3 electrons to form a N³⁻ ion.
    B. loses 5 electrons to form a N⁵⁺ ion.
    C. forms 3 covalent bonds.
    D. can form up to 8 covalent bonds.
    **Answer:** C. forms 3 covalent bonds.
    **Explanation:** A nitrogen atom has 5 valence electrons. To achieve a stable octet (8 electrons), it most commonly forms three covalent bonds (sharing 3 electrons) and has one lone pair.

960. The process used to obtain different fractions from petroleum is...
    A. catalytic cracking.
    B. destructive distillation.
    C. fractional distillation.
    D. steam distillation.
    **Answer:** C. fractional distillation.
    **Explanation:** Fractional distillation separates crude oil (petroleum) into fractions with different boiling point ranges, such as gasoline, kerosene, and diesel fuel.

961. The bond angle in the ammonium ion (NH₄⁺) is...
    A. 104.5°
    B. 107°
    C. 109.5°
    D. 120°
    **Answer:** C. 109.5°
    **Explanation:** The central nitrogen atom in the ammonium ion has four bonding pairs and zero lone pairs. This is a perfectly symmetrical tetrahedral arrangement, leading to bond angles of 109.5°.

962. If ΔH is positive and ΔS is negative for a reaction, the reaction...
    A. is always spontaneous.
    B. is always non-spontaneous.
    C. is spontaneous only at high temperatures.
    D. is spontaneous only at low temperatures.
    **Answer:** B. is always non-spontaneous.
    **Explanation:** From ΔG = ΔH - TΔS, if ΔH is positive and ΔS is negative, the -TΔS term will always be positive. The sum of two positive terms (ΔH and -TΔS) will always result in a positive ΔG. Therefore, the reaction is never spontaneous at any temperature.

963. An acid-base indicator is typically a...
    A. strong acid with a colored anion.
    B. weak acid or weak base with differently colored conjugate forms.
    C. salt that changes color with pH.
    D. neutral compound that reacts with H⁺ and OH⁻.
    **Answer:** B. weak acid or weak base with differently colored conjugate forms.
    **Explanation:** An indicator works because it is a weak acid (HIn) whose color is different from its conjugate base (In⁻). The equilibrium HIn ⇌ H⁺ + In⁻ shifts according to the pH of the solution, causing the observed color change.

964. Which of the following describes the properties of an alkali metal?
    A. Forms acidic oxides and has a high density.
    B. Is a poor conductor of electricity and forms covalent chlorides.
    C. Is a soft solid with a low melting point and reacts vigorously with water.
    D. Has a high electronegativity and forms a -1 ion.
    **Answer:** C. Is a soft solid with a low melting point and reacts vigorously with water.
    **Explanation:** Alkali metals (Group 1) are known for being soft enough to cut, having relatively low melting points for metals, and being extremely reactive, especially with water.

965. The formula for iron(III) oxide is...
    A. FeO
    B. FeO₂
    C. Fe₂O
    D. Fe₂O₃
    **Answer:** D. Fe₂O₃
    **Explanation:** Iron(III) indicates the Fe³⁺ ion. The oxide ion is O²⁻. To create a neutral compound, two Fe³⁺ ions (total charge +6) are needed to balance three O²⁻ ions (total charge -6), giving the formula Fe₂O₃.

966. How much heat is released when 2 moles of H₂ react according to the equation 2H₂(g) + O₂(g) → 2H₂O(l); ΔH = -572 kJ?
    A. 286 kJ
    B. 572 kJ
    C. 1144 kJ
    D. 2 x 572 x 6.02 x 10²³ kJ
    **Answer:** B. 572 kJ
    **Explanation:** The given ΔH value is for the reaction as written. The equation shows that when 2 moles of H₂ react, 572 kJ of heat is released.

967. The mass number of an element remains unchanged during...
    A. alpha decay.
    B. beta decay.
    C. gamma decay.
    D. nuclear fission.
    **Answer:** C. gamma decay.
    **Explanation:** Gamma decay is the emission of a high-energy photon. It does not involve the loss of any protons or neutrons, so both the atomic number and the mass number of the nucleus remain unchanged. Alpha and beta decay both change the nucleus.

968. A gas jar of chlorine and a gas jar of hydrogen sulfide are connected. A yellow solid is formed. The solid is...
    A. Chlorine sulfide
    B. Hydrogen chloride
    C. Sulfur
    D. A mixture of chlorine and hydrogen
    **Answer:** C. Sulfur
    **Explanation:** This is a redox reaction where hydrogen sulfide is oxidized and chlorine is reduced: H₂S(g) + Cl₂(g) → 2HCl(g) + S(s). The yellow solid formed is elemental sulfur.

969. In a reaction mechanism, a species that is produced in one elementary step and consumed in a subsequent step is called...
    A. a catalyst.
    B. a product.
    C. a reactant.
    D. an intermediate.
    **Answer:** D. an intermediate.
    **Explanation:** A reaction intermediate has a finite (though often very short) lifetime and does not appear in the overall balanced equation. It is formed and then used up as the reaction proceeds along its pathway.

970. Which statement is correct about metals?
    A. They form basic oxides.
    B. They are found on the right side of the periodic table.
    C. They gain electrons to form negative ions.
    D. They are brittle and poor conductors of heat.
    **Answer:** A. They form basic oxides.
    **Explanation:** The oxides of most metals (especially active metals) are basic, reacting with water to form hydroxides or with acids to form salts. The other statements are incorrect descriptions of metals.

971. Which substance would you use to prepare a sample of carbon dioxide gas?
    A. Carbon and oxygen
    B. Methane and water
    C. Calcium carbonate and hydrochloric acid
    D. Sodium hydroxide and carbonic acid
    **Answer:** C. Calcium carbonate and hydrochloric acid
    **Explanation:** This is the standard laboratory preparation of CO₂. The acid reacts with the carbonate to produce carbon dioxide gas, which can be easily collected: CaCO₃(s) + 2HCl(aq) → CaCl₂(aq) + H₂O(l) + CO₂(g).

972. The term "cracking" in the petroleum industry refers to...
    A. separating crude oil into fractions.
    B. building up small molecules into larger ones.
    C. purifying gasoline to remove sulfur.
    D. breaking down large hydrocarbon molecules into smaller ones.
    **Answer:** D. breaking down large hydrocarbon molecules into smaller ones.
    **Explanation:** Cracking uses heat and/or catalysts to break the C-C bonds in long-chain alkanes (which have low commercial value) to produce more valuable products like petrol (shorter-chain alkanes) and alkenes (for polymerization).

973. An ion with a 2+ charge has the electron configuration [Ar] 3d⁵. The element is...
    A. Fe
    B. Mn
    C. Cr
    D. V
    **Answer:** B. Mn
    **Explanation:** The ion has 18 + 5 = 23 electrons. Since it lost 2 electrons to get a 2+ charge, the neutral atom had 23 + 2 = 25 electrons. The element with atomic number 25 is Manganese (Mn), whose configuration is [Ar] 4s² 3d⁵. Losing the two 4s electrons gives the [Ar] 3d⁵ configuration for the Mn²⁺ ion.

974. An increase in the temperature of a gas in a flexible container (like a balloon) at constant pressure will cause...
    A. the volume to decrease.
    B. the volume to increase.
    C. the number of moles to decrease.
    D. the pressure to increase.
    **Answer:** B. the volume to increase.
    **Explanation:** This is a direct application of Charles's Law (V ∝ T). At constant pressure, as the temperature increases, the gas particles move faster and collide more forcefully, causing the flexible container to expand until the pressure inside equals the pressure outside again.

975. In the reaction SO₂ + H₂O ⇌ H₂SO₃, SO₂ can be considered a...
    A. Brønsted-Lowry base
    B. Lewis base
    C. Lewis acid
    D. Reducing agent
    **Answer:** C. Lewis acid
    **Explanation:** In this reaction, the sulfur atom in SO₂ accepts a lone pair of electrons from the oxygen atom in the water molecule to form a new S-O bond. A species that accepts an electron pair is a Lewis acid.

976. The boiling point is the temperature at which the vapor pressure of a liquid...
    A. is zero.
    B. equals the external pressure.
    C. is at its minimum.
    D. equals the triple point pressure.
    **Answer:** B. equals the external pressure.
    **Explanation:** This is the definition of the boiling point. When the escaping tendency of the liquid (vapor pressure) is strong enough to overcome the pressure of the surrounding atmosphere, boiling occurs throughout the liquid.

977. Which of the following CANNOT act as a reducing agent?
    A. H₂S
    B. SO₂
    C. C
    D. CO₂
    **Answer:** D. CO₂
    **Explanation:** A reducing agent must be able to be oxidized (increase its oxidation number). In CO₂, the carbon atom is in its highest possible oxidation state (+4). It cannot be oxidized further, so CO₂ cannot act as a reducing agent. The other species can all be oxidized (S in H₂S from -2 to 0/+4/+6; S in SO₂ from +4 to +6; C from 0 to +2/+4).

978. What type of bonding accounts for the high melting point of silicon dioxide (quartz)?
    A. Ionic bonds
    B. Metallic bonds
    C. Hydrogen bonds
    D. Covalent network bonds
    **Answer:** D. Covalent network bonds
    **Explanation:** Silicon dioxide has a giant covalent network structure where each silicon atom is strongly and covalently bonded to four oxygen atoms in a tetrahedral arrangement. To melt it, these strong covalent bonds must be broken, which requires a very large amount of energy.

979. Which property is characteristic of a reaction that has reached equilibrium?
    A. The concentrations of reactants are zero.
    B. The rate of the forward reaction is zero.
    C. The rates of the forward and reverse reactions are equal.
    D. The temperature of the system is always 298 K.
    **Answer:** C. The rates of the forward and reverse reactions are equal.
    **Explanation:** This is the defining characteristic of a dynamic chemical equilibrium. Macroscopic properties are constant because the rate of formation of products is exactly balanced by the rate of their conversion back into reactants.

980. The complete hydrolysis of fats (triglycerides) yields glycerol and...
    A. amino acids.
    B. monosaccharides.
    C. fatty acids.
    D. alcohols.
    **Answer:** C. fatty acids.
    **Explanation:** Fats are esters formed from one molecule of glycerol and three molecules of long-chain carboxylic acids (fatty acids). Hydrolysis breaks these ester linkages, regenerating the glycerol and fatty acids.

981. To make a 0.5 M solution, how many moles of solute should be dissolved in 2.0 dm³ of solution?
    A. 0.25 mol
    B. 0.5 mol
    C. 1.0 mol
    D. 2.0 mol
    **Answer:** C. 1.0 mol
    **Explanation:** Moles = Molarity × Volume (in dm³) = 0.5 mol/dm³ × 2.0 dm³ = 1.0 mol.

982. A chemical reaction has a positive ΔH and a positive ΔS. For the reaction to be spontaneous...
    A. TΔS must be less than ΔH.
    B. T must be high.
    C. T must be low.
    D. the pressure must be high.
    **Answer:** B. T must be high.
    **Explanation:** From the Gibbs equation, ΔG = ΔH - TΔS. For ΔG to be negative (spontaneous) when both ΔH and ΔS are positive, the TΔS term must be larger than the ΔH term. This is achieved at sufficiently high temperatures.

983. Which of the following is NOT a form of carbon?
    A. Diamond
    B. Fullerene
    C. Coke
    D. Calcite
    **Answer:** D. Calcite
    **Explanation:** Calcite is a mineral form of calcium carbonate (CaCO₃). It is a compound, not an elemental form of carbon. Diamond, fullerene, and coke (an amorphous form) are all forms of carbon.

984. What is the conjugate base of the ammonium ion, NH₄⁺?
    A. NH₃
    B. NH₂⁻
    C. H₂O
    D. OH⁻
    **Answer:** A. NH₃
    **Explanation:** A conjugate base is what remains after an acid has donated a proton. When the acid NH₄⁺ donates a proton (H⁺), the remaining species is ammonia, NH₃.

985. The reaction between an alcohol and a sodium metal produces...
    A. a salt and water.
    B. an alkoxide and hydrogen gas.
    C. an alkene and water.
    D. an ester and hydrogen gas.
    **Answer:** B. an alkoxide and hydrogen gas.
    **Explanation:** The alcohol acts as a very weak acid. The highly reactive sodium metal displaces the acidic hydrogen from the -OH group, forming a sodium alkoxide salt and liberating hydrogen gas (e.g., 2CH₃OH + 2Na → 2CH₃ONa + H₂).

986. What is the key functional group present in an amino acid?
    A. A hydroxyl group and a carboxyl group.
    B. An amino group and a hydroxyl group.
    C. An amino group and a carboxyl group.
    D. An amino group and an amide group.
    **Answer:** C. An amino group and a carboxyl group.
    **Explanation:** By definition, an amino acid is a molecule that contains both a basic amino group (-NH₂) and an acidic carboxyl group (-COOH).

987. During fractional distillation, the substance with the lowest boiling point...
    A. condenses at the bottom of the column.
    B. is collected first from the top of the column.
    C. remains as a residue.
    D. separates into a different layer.
    **Answer:** B. is collected first from the top of the column.
    **Explanation:** The component with the lowest boiling point is the most volatile. It will vaporize most easily, rise to the top of the fractionating column, and be the first to be condensed and collected.

988. In the name 2,2-dimethylpropane, the suffix "-ane" signifies that the molecule...
    A. contains a double bond.
    B. contains only single carbon-carbon bonds.
    C. is cyclic.
    D. is aromatic.
    **Answer:** B. contains only single carbon-carbon bonds.
    **Explanation:** The "-ane" suffix in IUPAC nomenclature is characteristic of alkanes, which are saturated hydrocarbons containing only single bonds between carbon atoms.

989. The charge on an ion is determined by the...
    A. number of protons only.
    B. number of neutrons only.
    C. sum of protons and neutrons.
    D. difference between the number of protons and electrons.
    **Answer:** D. difference between the number of protons and electrons.
    **Explanation:** Protons are positively charged and electrons are negatively charged. An excess of protons results in a positive ion (cation), while an excess of electrons results in a negative ion (anion).

990. The most common isotope of hydrogen, Protium (¹H), has...
    A. 1 proton and 1 neutron.
    B. 1 proton and 0 neutrons.
    C. 0 protons and 1 neutron.
    D. 1 proton and 2 neutrons.
    **Answer:** B. 1 proton and 0 neutrons.
    **Explanation:** For ¹H, the atomic number is 1 (1 proton) and the mass number is 1. The number of neutrons = mass number - atomic number = 1 - 1 = 0.

991. The shape of the ammonia (NH₃) molecule is...
    A. Tetrahedral
    B. Trigonal planar
    C. Trigonal pyramidal
    D. Bent
    **Answer:** C. Trigonal pyramidal
    **Explanation:** The central nitrogen has 3 bonding pairs and 1 lone pair. The four electron domains have a tetrahedral arrangement, but the molecular shape described by the atoms' positions is trigonal pyramidal.

992. When sugar dissolves in water, the forces broken are ______ and the forces formed are ______.
    A. covalent bonds; ionic bonds
    B. hydrogen bonds; covalent bonds
    C. ionic bonds; hydrogen bonds
    D. intermolecular forces; intermolecular forces
    **Answer:** D. intermolecular forces; intermolecular forces
    **Explanation:** To dissolve, the weak intermolecular forces between sugar molecules and the hydrogen bonds between water molecules must be overcome. New, favorable intermolecular forces (hydrogen bonds) then form between the sugar molecules and water molecules.

993. The purpose of chlorination in water treatment is for...
    A. filtration.
    B. coagulation.
    C. softening.
    D. disinfection.
    **Answer:** D. disinfection.
    **Explanation:** Chlorine is added to water to kill harmful microorganisms like bacteria and viruses, making the water safe for consumption.

994. In the contact process, the reaction 2SO₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2SO₃(g) is exothermic. According to Le Chatelier's principle, a high yield of SO₃ is favored by...
    A. low pressure and high temperature.
    B. high pressure and low temperature.
    C. low pressure and low temperature.
    D. high pressure and high temperature.
    **Answer:** B. high pressure and low temperature.
    **Explanation:** High pressure favors the side with fewer moles of gas (2 moles vs 3 moles), which is the product side. Low temperature favors the exothermic forward reaction.

995. When iron rusts, its oxidation state changes from...
    A. 0 to +2 or +3.
    B. +2 to 0.
    C. 0 to -2.
    D. +3 to +2.
    **Answer:** A. 0 to +2 or +3.
    **Explanation:** Elemental iron (Fe) has an oxidation state of 0. In rust (hydrated iron(III) oxide, Fe₂O₃·xH₂O), the iron is in the +3 state. Iron(II) is an intermediate. Thus, iron is oxidized.

996. The property that describes the ability of a substance to resist scratching is...
    A. Lustre
    B. Hardness
    C. Malleability
    D. Density
    **Answer:** B. Hardness
    **Explanation:** Hardness is defined as a material's resistance to scratching or indentation.

997. Which of these is a correct statement?
    A. The atomic radius of Na is smaller than that of Na⁺.
    B. The ionic radius of Cl is larger than that of Cl⁻.
    C. The atomic radius of K is larger than that of Na.
    D. The first ionization energy of K is greater than that of Na.
    **Answer:** C. The atomic radius of K is larger than that of Na.
    **Explanation:** Potassium (K) is below Sodium (Na) in Group 1. Atomic radius increases down a group because an extra electron shell is added. The other statements are incorrect.

998. The percentage of water of crystallization in blue vitriol, CuSO₄·5H₂O, is... [Cu=64, S=32, O=16, H=1]
    A. 10.8%
    B. 25.0%
    C. 36.0%
    D. 64.0%
    **Answer:** C. 36.0%
    **Explanation:**
    Mass of 5H₂O = 5 × (2+16) = 90.
    Total Molar Mass = (64+32+64) + 90 = 160 + 90 = 250.
    % water = (Mass of water / Total Molar Mass) × 100% = (90 / 250) × 100% = 0.36 × 100% = 36.0%.

999. In which of the following is the nitrogen atom in its highest oxidation state?
    A. NH₃
    B. N₂O
    C. NO₂
    D. HNO₃
    **Answer:** D. HNO₃
    **Explanation:**
    In NH₃, N is -3.
    In N₂O, N is +1.
    In NO₂, N is +4.
    In HNO₃, H is +1, O is -2. (+1) + N + 3(-2) = 0 => N - 5 = 0 => N is +5.
    +5 is the highest oxidation state for nitrogen.

1000. An organic reaction that results in the conversion of an alkene to an alcohol is a(n)...
    A. hydrogenation reaction.
    B. dehydration reaction.
    C. hydration reaction.
    D. esterification reaction.
    **Answer:** C. hydration reaction.
    **Explanation:** Hydration is the addition of a water molecule across the double bond of an alkene, typically catalyzed by an acid, to produce an alcohol.
